# Changelog

All notable changes to **Tadah.HPO** (hyper-parameter optimisation) are documented here.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- `repeat_points.h`: `RunRecord` and `RecordedEval`, the run's usable evaluations by the point evaluated (bit for bit), `RepeatPoints`, a search's use of it with the gradients the search formed, and `repeat_policy<Vec>(host)`, and `repeat_use(reuse, strategy, with_gradient, has_gradient)`, the one rule by which a call at a recorded point is answered (`RepeatUse`), which `RepeatPoints::use` and `gradient_budget_warnings` apply; `HPO_Host::recorded(point)` (the run's evaluation of the point an evaluation of `point` would make: after the snap onto the box and the RHOFORM projection; cleared by `prepare_best_publication`); `HPOTargets::repeat_reuse_blocker()` and `HPO_Host::repeat_reuse_blocker()`, why two evaluations of one point can give different losses in the run, or `""`; `Observable::carried_state()` (what an observable carries from one evaluation to the next; `cohesive_energy` its lattice constant; one that does not say carries something); `ValidationMetricTargets::has_relative_cap()`; `run_dlib_bfgs_impl` (DLIB:BFGS on any host), `detail::bfgs_box_search` (an overload with an evaluation count: `BfgsBoxEnd::Revisit`, `BfgsBoxResult::revisit_from`), `detail::stalled_reach` and `detail::BfgsObjective` (`settle()`: counts a loss answered from the record that no call followed, when the search ends); `EndReason::NoStep` (`no step within the OPTIM box`) and `EndReason::Revisit` (`back at an earlier point`); `HPOLog::record_repeat_reused`, `record_repeat_evaluated` and `record_line_search_not_run`; `HPO_Host::count_reused_repeat(const RecordedEval&)` (a reused repeat in the RESTART window and among a PRESTAGE exploration's candidates), and `detail::throw_if_stagnated` takes the count of reused calls since the last new best (a new parameter); `describe_point` takes the digits to print (10 by default). `InitStream` and `InitContext::stream` (the block a METHOD chain draws for: an `INIT` block or a `RESTART` round), `InitStrategy::draws_from_seed()`, `seed_streams::kRestartPoints`.
- **The ALGO help reaches the CLI** (`optimizer_help.h`): `explain_optimiser(name, out)`, which tadah sets as MLIP's explain hook, prints for `ALGO` or `LIB` (any letter case) the table of the optimisers (`print_optimiser_table`: by library, sorted, whether each is global or local, gradient-based or derivative-free -- MLSL and AUGLAG: the local search they run --, needs a stop or ignores the box; then the refused backends and how to see one), for `LIB:ALGO` or an `ALGO` alone that backend's ALGO help (`OptimizerSpec::print_caps`, a refused one its refusal), for a `LIB` its section of the table, and throws `tadah explain: unknown optimiser ...` for a `LIB:ALGO` that names no backend; it returns false for any other name. `OptimizerSpec::print_caps(std::ostream&, lib, algo)` (the `std::cout` overload stays). `optimizer_libraries()`, `refused_backend_names()` and `require_registered_backend(lib, algo, where)` (`optimizer_caps.h`; below). `print_caps` was called only from HPO's tests. Tests: `test_optimizer_help.cpp`.
- `gradient_cost(spec)` and `gradient_budget_warnings(root, reuse)` (`backend_rules.h`): what one gradient of a search costs, and the startup WARNINGs of the phases whose budget cannot pay for one step (below), in a run that answers a point a search asks for again from its record when `reuse` (`validate_hpo_setup` passes whether `HPOTargets::repeat_reuse_blocker()`, which the host forwards, is `""`).
- `PcTaskResult::hotvar_errors` and `failvar_errors` (`pc_task_exec.h`): the LAMMPS message of each `--hotvar` and `--failvar` whose read-back raised an error, one per variable ("" for one that was read; empty when every one was), as `outvar_errors` is for the outvars; the PC worker protocol is version 4 (its `RESULT` frame carries them).
- **`OPTIM MPARAMS` is checked against the model's function at startup** (`Function_Base::mparams()`, Tadah.MODELS): `OPTIM MPARAMS (i)` is refused on a function that reads fewer than i values (`BF_Linear`, `BF_Polynomial2`, `Kern_Linear`, `Kern_Quadratic` and `Kern_LQ` read none: the search changed nothing), on an integer value (`MPARAMS (1)` of `Kern_Polynomial`, the degree; a candidate was truncated) and, for a value that must be positive (`Kern_RBF`'s sigma), on a box whose low bound is not > 0. Test: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **`OPTIM` on a value its key takes only as an integer, or on an internal key, is refused at its line.** A value every format (blueprint `value_type`) of the key gives `INT` or `UINT` (`SBASIS`; a candidate such as 14.47 was trained as 14): `OPTIM SBASIS (1) is not a valid search target: value 1 of SBASIS is an integer (SBASIS takes UINT), ...`; `OPTIM OALGO (1)` keeps its own message. An internal key (`DSIZE`, `RCUT2BMAX`, `ESTDEV`, ..., computed by tadah; `OPTIM ESTDEV` failed every evaluation with "Attempt to add internal key"): `OPTIM ESTDEV is not a valid search target: ESTDEV is an internal key ...`. Test: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **`OPTIM RCUT2B` / `RCUTMB` on a cutoff that a descriptor takes from its setfl file is refused at startup** (`D_Base::cutoff_fixed_by`, Tadah.MODELS): the cutoff of D2_EAM and DM_EAM, also D2_EAM's or DM_EAM's own value inside a D2_mJoin or DM_mJoin (the other joined descriptors' values stay searchable): `OPTIM RCUT2B (1) is not a valid search target: D2_EAM takes the cutoff 5.58190742024 of its setfl file 'Ta.eam.alloy' (SETFL) and refuses another value, so every candidate but the -c value would fail; leave RCUT2B (1) out of OPTIM.` These descriptors refuse a cutoff other than the file's (Tadah.MODELS 1.6.0), so such a search scored only its `-c` value: every other candidate failed its evaluation (FAILSCORE, the journal line "D2_EAM: RCUT2B ... differs from the cutoff ... of the setfl file") and the run ended with status 0. Before this release's per-evaluation trainer, a thread's reused trainer did not build its D2_EAM again, so that check did not run past the thread's first evaluation, and `best_pot.tadah` recorded a candidate cutoff that `tadah predict` refuses. Test: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- `PcWorkerPool::worker_pid(slot)`: the pid of the worker process the pool holds for a slot, -1 when it holds none (after a reaped death until the next task respawns it, after `shutdown()`); `std::out_of_range` for a slot outside the pool. It waits for a task in flight on the slot.
- Tests: `tadah.hpo_headers` (`ctest -L headers`, cmake-tools' `tadah_add_header_check`) compiles every header of `include/` on its own and twice in a row, with what a user of `tadah.hpo` gets and the module's `TADAH_USE_DLIB`.
- Tests: `hpo_configure_without_ceres` (label `cmake`) configures HPO once more, with `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` OFF and without tests, from the cache of the build that runs it and the source directories of its dependencies (`FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED`: nothing is downloaded), and checks through CMake's file API that `tadah.hpo` has an include directory with `Eigen/Dense` and no Ceres: every HPO test build checks the configuration without Ceres. The initial cache of that configure (`hpo_write_no_ceres_preset()`, `tests/configure_without_ceres/no_ceres_preset.cmake`) holds every cache entry of the build that is not `INTERNAL` or `STATIC` and names no path in its build tree (`CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`, a toolchain file, the libraries it found, its options), the source directory of every dependency, given as `FETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_<NAME>` or populated under `FETCHCONTENT_BASE_DIR`, and `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` OFF, `BUILD_TESTING` OFF; the environment is CTest's. `hpo_configure_without_ceres_preset` (label `cmake`) checks on a throw-away pair of projects that a configure from it finds a package found only through `-DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`, and gets the build's options, both kinds of dependency source and a `FETCHCONTENT_BASE_DIR` of its own. In a build without Ceres the test cases that use `LIB CERES` as an example backend expect its refusal for their phase (`CERES support is not built; reconfigure with -DTADAH_ENABLE_CERES=ON`; `tests/ceres_build.h`), where seven of them failed expecting the Ceres validators' messages; `RHOFORM NONNEG/MONOTONE: one WARNING when an optimiser can leave the box`, whose optimiser is `CERES:LBFGS`, is Skipped there, naming it, and its sections that need no Ceres are a test case of their own (`RHOFORM NONNEG/MONOTONE: no box WARNING when every phase keeps to the box`).
- Tests: every hidden LAMMPS-driven case has a CTest entry and runs in the suite (`ctest -L bench`, four entries, about 8 s together): `hpo_lammps_hessian` (the LAMMPS cases of `test_lammps_hessian.cpp`: the 4-atom bcc Ta Hessian and its vflag=1 microbench), `hpo_phase3_pilot` (`test_hpo_phase3_pilot.cpp`: the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint chain on real relaxations, `dvirial_dW` and `dlx/dW` against finite differences), `hpo_bench_pair_tadah` (`bench_pair_tadah.cpp`) and `hpo_host_e2e` (`test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp`: `HPO_Host`'s analytical gradient without a `PC_LAMMPS` line, with a `PC_OBSERVABLE` and with a `PC_LAMMPS` script run in process; the last pins that its provider, which gives no direct θ term, sends every θ to finite differences). The first three run on a Ta potential shipped with the tests, `tests/data/bench_ta.tadah`: a copy of Tadah.MLIP's Quick Start potential (`docs/source/quickstart/pot.tadah` at Tadah.MLIP c91e8ef), whose header gives its provenance and why it is used (a linear model with 9 weights, C11 = 212 GPa and C12 = 91 GPa in the cases' cell); `hpo_host_e2e` trains its own model. `TADAH_BENCH_POT` still names another potential. These 19 cases are hidden Catch cases (`[.][!benchmark]`; the bump-gate case, the twentieth, runs in `hpo_bumpgate_e2e`) and had no CTest entry. Without `TADAH_BENCH_POT` the first 16 called `TADAH_SKIP`: the default `$TADAH_PATH/HPO_test/pot.tadah`, an HPO-trained M_BLR Ta potential of one developer's workspace, is shipped by no repository. They no longer skip: a potential that is missing, or cannot be read, parsed or run, is a failure. With the Tadah.LAMMPS test potentials the two lattice-constant cases failed their precondition of at least 4 weights (they perturb `WEIGHTS[2]`); with the HPO examples' energies-only model (C12 = -103 GPa) the `box_dofs=6` Hessian case failed its sign checks; with the `HPO_test` potential the iso lattice-constant case failed its 1e-4 tolerance, 1.1e-4 (the box Hessian, Fixed below). The `box_dofs=6` case now pins that the Voigt shear rows of the Hessian vanish on its cell (|H| < 1e-2: exactly 0, and 9.95e-4 while the diagonal was the energy's second difference; 9.154 eV on the same cell made triclinic). The cell is an orthogonal box, whose tilt factors LAMMPS ignores, so the assembler's shear strains move nothing: this is the recorded box-convention issue of the analytic-gradient path, not fixed here. The case had asserted that the entries are > 0, which held or failed on the sign of the round-off.
- Tests: what NLopt's MLSL does next to a failure region with each LD_* local search (`test_fd_bounds.cpp`, `No usable gradient: MLSL moves on from a rejected local start ...` and `... MLSL ends when an LD_LBFGS, LD_TNEWTON* or LD_VAR* local search fails at the edge of the failure region ...`). On a one-dimensional stub whose loss fails below a wall, under `G_MLSL_LDS` with an `INNER` block of each of the 12 LD_* algorithms, and `GD_MLSL_LDS` (NLopt's default `LD_MMA`, or the same `INNER` blocks):
  - at a rejected local start (a sample whose stencil point fails), the local search makes 1 request (2 for `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ` and the `LD_AUGLAG` inners), does not come back to it, and MLSL goes on (60 evaluations, `MAXEVAL_REACHED`);
  - a local search converges on the edge of the failure region, where the stencil of every point below it reaches the wall. With `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR1` or `LD_VAR2` its line search finds no usable point there and returns an NLopt failure, and NLopt's MLSL ends its whole search with it (`mlsl.c` ends on any error of a local search): `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: ... NLopt ended right after it (nlopt failure)` after 141 to 185 of 400 evaluations. With `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ`, `LD_SLSQP` and the `LD_AUGLAG` inners MLSL reaches `MAXEVAL`. HPO cannot end one local search without ending MLSL (a forced stop is an error to MLSL as well), so no code changed; whether HPO should keep MLSL going there is an open question.
  `AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG` with each LD_* `INNER` block stop at the rejected start, as documented.
- `shutdown_script_executor()` (`script_executor.h`): stops the script executor, joining its workers; `IterHealthRecord::layout_matches`, `n_weights` and `layout_cols`: whether the column layout describes the weights (as many columns as weights), and both counts; `IterHealthRecord::block_has_nan_inf`: which blocks hold a NaN or infinite weight (they get no aggregates).
- **Tests of the MPI build (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`), and the suite runs in it.** The registered suite is the serial build's and runs no MPI code; in a `TADAH_HPO_MPI` build `ctest -L mpi` now runs, through CMake's `MPIEXEC_EXECUTABLE` at 1, 2 and 4 ranks, `hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix` and: `hpo_mpi_signal_<scenario>` (`tests/mpi/test_signal_mpi.cpp`: a signal one rank receives during rank 0's seed, T0 or PT pass-2 calibration, the `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` calibration, a population initialization, a replica rung or a walker sweep ends every rank `SHUTDOWN`, 128 + the signal; see Fixed) and `hpo_mpi_e2e_<case>` (`tests/mpi/hpo_mpi_e2e.py`, the built `tadah` on 1 and 2 ranks: `RESTART` refused on several ranks before any evaluation, tested at spec level only so far; a gradient search (example 01, DLIB BFGS) refused on several ranks and its one-sided differences counted on one -- finite differences run only in gradient searches, which a multi-rank launch refuses, so their counters cannot be run on several ranks; example 27's D2_Knot5 + DM_REAM seed under ANNEAL_PT on the LAMMPS EAM-collapse fast path on every rank, the evaluated set and `best_pot.tadah` those of the 1-rank run, at an `Ecoh` target the seed misses so that the global best is a search evaluation rank 1 makes and publishes; the `RHOGATE` and `RHOBARGATE` counts adding up to the 1-rank run's, every rank's density report of the `-c` potential the 1-rank run's, and rank 1's report of the global best the 1-rank run's report of its best; Skipped in a build without the `tadah` executable). The rest of the suite runs in the MPI build too: test_hpo initialises MPI as `tadah` does (a one-rank job) and its LAMMPS cases create their instances on `hpo_lammps_comm()` (106 cases aborted there, "MPI_Comm_rank() function was called before MPI_INIT"); the seven `pc pool:` cases that start `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` workers are Skipped in that build, which has no PROCESS mode (`pc pool: in the MPI build the worker refuses to start`), and the PC_ISOLATION journal and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` cases assert its THREAD-only behaviour (the latter runs `tadah` under the launcher of one rank, `TADAH_MPIEXEC`: started without it, Open MPI 5 makes its session directory under the case's `TMPDIR`, a regular file, reports the failure but returns `MPI_SUCCESS` from `MPI_Init_thread`, and the first communicator creation crashes).
- **`.gitattributes`: git converts no line ending of the vendored ASA's upstream files** (`external/asa/asa.c`, `asa.h`, `asa_usr_asa.h`, `ASA-LICENSE`, `ASA-NOTES`, `ASA-README.txt`, attribute `-text`), whose CRLF endings the TADAH-PATCH hunks of `asa.c` are written in (`external/asa/TADAH-PATCHES.md`). Nothing but a note protected them: under `core.autocrlf=input` or `true` `git add --renormalize` wrote them with LF endings, and with a global attributes file `* text eol=lf` so did `git add` of an edit, rewriting every line of `asa.c`. Tests: `hpo_vendored_asa_line_endings` (every line of the six files ends in CRLF), `hpo_vendored_asa_gitattributes` (git stores their bytes unchanged under those settings, in a scratch repository; Skipped when CMake finds no git) and `hpo_vendored_asa_gate_tool` (`tests/check_vendored_asa.py` on fake trees).
- **`kNloptTestedVersion`** (`nlopt_tested_version.h`): the NLopt version, 2.10.0, that the NLOPT entries of the refusal table (`backend_refusal()`) and the NLopt behaviour HPO pins were measured on. The test case `the NLopt HPO is built against is the version its NLopt refusals were measured on` (`test_nlopt_version.cpp`) fails when `nlopt_version()` reports another version (`FETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_NLOPT`, a new `GIT_TAG`), naming the repros to run again (`docs/issues/nlopt_newuoa_bound_stall.md`, the other `docs/issues/nlopt_*.md`, the NLOPT entries of `kRefused` and their tests) before the constant changes: nothing recorded the version, and an NLopt upgrade kept the `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` refusal untested.
- Tests: `hpo_tadah_cli_kernel_line_once` (label `e2e`): at `-v 1` a `M_KRR Kern_RBF` run of four evaluations with a `PC_LAMMPS` script prints the kernel's settings line once, under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` and `PROCESS`; `hpo_tadah_cli_kernel_line_two_runs`: two `TASK hpo` blocks of a task file, `MPARAMS 1.0` then `2.0`, print `Kern_RBF | sigma: 1 | gamma: 1` once and `Kern_RBF | sigma: 2 | gamma: 0.25` once.

### Removed
- `reject_deferred_ceres_nlls()` (`ceres_adapter.h`): Ceres' least-squares solvers are entries of the refusal table (*Fixed*), whose text every caps lookup gives.
- The environment variable `TADAH_HPO_FORCE_REBUILD`: every evaluation builds its model (see Fixed), which is what it asked for.

### Changed
- **A point the run has evaluated is answered from its evaluation when a search asks for it again, if every evaluation of the run is a function of its point** (`nlopt_adapter.h`, `dlib_adapter.h`, `repeat_points.h`, `hpo_host_impl.hpp`). `HPO_Host` records each evaluation of the run that gave a usable loss, whichever backend asked for it, by the point it evaluated (after the snap onto the box and the RHOFORM projection, bit for bit; `HPO_Host::recorded`; one entry per usable evaluation, the point twice and the loss, a few hundred bytes for a few dimensions), and NLopt's objective and DLIB:BFGS answer a call at such a point that asks for nothing the record lacks with the recorded loss (and the gradient, when the search formed one there): no host call, no row, not counted in HPO's count of evaluations, which counts each search's own evaluations and is what DLIB:BFGS's `MAXEVAL` (a MULTISTART start's `BUDGET_PER_START`, a PRESTAGE phase's `MAXEVAL`) bounds. NLopt counts every call against its own `MAXEVAL`, a call answered from an earlier search's evaluation too, so an NLopt phase's `MAXEVAL` or start's `BUDGET_PER_START` is charged for it (an `LN_SBPLX` refinement of `MAXEVAL 4` whose first call is the exploration's best: 3 evaluations, `NLopt counts its calls, and 1 of them asked for points the run had evaluated ...`; 4 while each search's record was its own). That holds for a point an earlier search of the run evaluated too: a PRESTAGE refinement starts from the exploration's best point (an LN_SBPLX exploration and a DLIB:BFGS refinement on the termination tests: 10 evaluations; 11 while the refinement evaluated its start again), a MULTISTART start reaches a point an earlier start evaluated (two DLIB:BFGS starts that end at a bound: the bound evaluated once), and a RESTART round from `METHOD CONFIG` takes round 0's path (LN_SBPLX: 9 evaluations, 14 before, 5 of them repeats); the search's first such reuse names it (`DLIB:BFGS asked for the point of eval 4 (SGRID2B[2] = 4), which an earlier search of the run evaluated: the loss of eval 4 is reused, without an evaluation ...`). The gradients stay each search's own (they depend on its coordinates and finite-difference settings): a search that asks for a gradient at a point another search evaluated evaluates the stencil (NUMERICAL, below) or the point (ANALYTICAL, HYBRID), so MULTISTART starts that end at one bound each evaluate its stencil. TADAH:RANDOM, TADAH:GRID, the TADAH annealers, ASA, CERES and DLIB:GFS evaluate every point they ask for; their evaluations are recorded for the searches that answer from the record. A PRESTAGE exploration's call answered from the record offers its point to the refinement's candidates, as the evaluation would have (`count_reused_repeat`): a RESTART round's exploration that takes round 0's path makes no evaluation and still hands on its candidates (`PRESTAGE: 3 usable candidate(s) for TOPK 5 (the exploration made 0 evaluation(s), 0 without a usable loss, and answered 3 call(s) from the run's record of earlier evaluations); refining 3`); an exploration that asked for no point at all is still an error. Under `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` a call that asks for a gradient the record lacks takes the recorded loss as the loss at the point of its finite differences and evaluates only their stencil (`RepeatUse::Centre`): MLSL's local search asks for the gradient at a sample it evaluated without one, and each local start was evaluated again (3 of 60 evaluations of `G_MLSL_LDS` with `INNER LD_MMA` on the tests' bowl). The RESTART stagnation window counts each reused call as one evaluation without a new best (`HPO_Host::count_reused_repeat`): a search that only asks for points the run has makes no evaluation, and without it `LD_MMA` held at a bound (u = 0) with RESTART as its only stop would spin without end, and with MAXEVAL would never restart, NLopt's count of calls ending it (`StagnationStop fired at a repeat of eval 16 answered from the run's record, after eval 18; short-circuiting optimiser (threshold=5 evals without improvement, 3 of them call(s) answered from the run's record)`, and a window that ends at an evaluation counts its reused calls the same way; the termination tests: 23 evaluations and one restart either way, 26 and one restart when every repeat was evaluated). DLIB:BFGS asks for the gradient of the point its line search ended on right after its loss; when that loss was answered from the record the gradient takes it as an evaluation's and is no second reuse (in DIAGNOSTICS or the window), so a reused call stands for exactly the evaluation it replaces. Under `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` that gradient evaluates the point (below), and the evaluation answers the loss call too: the two calls are one evaluation, counted once in the window, a repeat evaluated again and no reuse (a PRESTAGE refinement's start: `DLIB:BFGS asked for the point of eval 4 (SGRID2B[2] = 4) and its gradient, which an earlier search of the run evaluated; it is evaluated again: ... GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL ...`); a reused loss that no such gradient follows is a reuse, counted at the next call or when the search ends (`BfgsObjective::settle`). Counted both as a reuse and as an evaluation, the start took two calls of the window, and a refinement under `RESTART { AFTER_STAGNATION 2 }` was ended at its first evaluation and restarted where it ends `FTOL_ABS reached` (as it did while each search's record was its own), and a call that MAXEVAL refused was counted as a repeat evaluated again. `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` re-solve their subproblem with a larger rho and land on the same clamped corner (mma.c), then ask for it at their next iteration; `LN_SBPLX`, `LN_NELDERMEAD`, `GN_ESCH` and `GN_CRS2_LM` reflect or mutate onto a point they had; `LD_SLSQP` and `LN_COBYLA` step onto a bound point they had (the termination tests' 1-D straddle: 5 and 1 such calls); `AUGLAG` evaluates the point its local search returned once more; DLIB:BFGS's backtracking trials are clamped onto one point. Each repeat was a fit (and every `PC_LAMMPS` script). The host says when a reuse is exact (`repeat_reuse_blocker()`): not with a `PC_LAMMPS` line (its scripts run on LAMMPS instances kept from one evaluation to the next, and a `--hotvar` starts from the best evaluation's value), a `PC_OBSERVABLE` that carries state (`cohesive_energy`), or a `--skip-rel` filter (it compares with the best value so far); such a run evaluates every repeat again and journals the reason once (`... asked again for the point of eval 34 (RCUT2B = 4, SGRIDMB = 0.4); it is evaluated again: PC_LAMMPS: ...`). The run's record holds its usable evaluations under a blocker too, so a point an earlier search of the run evaluated -- a PRESTAGE refinement's start, a point an earlier MULTISTART start or RESTART round evaluated -- is a repeat evaluated again as well: counted in DIAGNOSTICS `Repeats evaluated again` (example 12: 5, 2 while each search's record was its own, its three refinement starts the difference) and named so when it is the run's first (a PRESTAGE refinement's start on the examples' Ta data with a `PC_LAMMPS` line: `DLIB:BFGS asked for the point of eval 5 (RCUT2B = 5.376917644, SGRIDMB = 0.1563088886), which an earlier search of the run evaluated; it is evaluated again: PC_LAMMPS: ...`; the line said `DLIB:BFGS asked again for the point of eval 5 ...`, though DLIB:BFGS had not asked for it). A failed evaluation is evaluated again, and so is a point whose gradient is asked for and was not formed there under `ANALYTICAL` or `HYBRID`: `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` differentiates the thread's last evaluation without checking its point, so DLIB:BFGS evaluates a point first before such a gradient there that does not follow its evaluation (the provider's `gradient(x)` used to form such a gradient without one; dlib never asked for it). The journal names each search's first reuse and counts its reuses at 10, 100, 1000, ... (a search that only repeats makes no row: `LD_MMA` held at u = 0, where NLopt's XTOL_REL test is vacuous); DIAGNOSTICS has `Repeated points reused` and `Repeats evaluated again`. NLopt counts a reused call in its own MAXEVAL: a derivative-free search still ends after MAXEVAL calls with fewer evaluations, and its line says so (`NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted after 45 of MAXEVAL 60 evaluation(s); NLopt counts its calls, and 15 of them asked for points the run had evaluated and were answered without an evaluation`; it said `asked again for a point` until a call answered from an earlier search's evaluation was named as such, and the count at 10, 100, ... `asked again for points the run evaluated`); a gradient search usually spends HPO's budget first (a loss and its stencil per call), so a reuse leaves the budget to new points and the search can get further (`LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 on the termination tests' straddle: 60 evaluations ending at 1.8659506446, 1.8678643276 before; `LD_MMA` at MAXEVAL 20 on a corner ends XTOL_REACHED, MAXEVAL_REACHED before), but one that mostly asks again ends on NLopt's count (`LD_MMA` held at a bound: 18 evaluations of MAXEVAL 60, 54 of its 60 calls answered). The first-reuse journal line says how the backend's MAXEVAL takes a reuse (NLopt: `a reuse is no evaluation: HPO's MAXEVAL does not count it, but NLopt counts it among the calls its own MAXEVAL bounds`; DLIB:BFGS: `a reuse is no evaluation, and MAXEVAL counts evaluations`), and so does DIAGNOSTICS, with the RESTART window. A repeat after the budget is spent is answered too. On the audit's 2-D search on the examples' Ta data (not an HPO example: `PC_ERMSE` and `PC_FRMSE`, `OPTIM RCUT2B (1) 4 6` and `CGRID2B (2) 0.1 2.0`, the optimum on a corner) `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` at MAXEVAL 40 make 10 evaluations (20 before), the same XTOL_REACHED; `LN_SBPLX` 45 in its 60 calls. `LD_SLSQP`'s steps onto that corner are distinct points (3.8e-13 apart) and are evaluated, as before. Tests: `test_repeat_points.cpp`, `test_repeat_record.cpp` (`HPO_Host::recorded`), `test_policy_host_exceptions.cpp` (the window's count), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`[repeat_points]`, the `Run record:` cases, the RESTART cases, and nine pins whose counts or path change: LN_SBPLX 10 rows for its 12 calls, AUGLAG 8, COBYLA 59, the bump-gate refinement 18, LD_SLSQP from 1.8, G_MLSL_LDS's first local start not evaluated again).
- **A `RESTART` round's `RANDOM` and `LHS` methods draw a stream of their own, and a `RESTART` block without `SEED` journals the seed its restart points are drawn with** (`init_strategies.cpp`, `run_policy.h`, `seed_stream.h`). Round r drew `std::mt19937_64(SEED + r)`, the generator an `INIT` block's `RANDOM` and `LHS` draw for that seed, so a round whose `RESTART SEED + r` was an `INIT` block's `SEED` started where round 0 did (`INIT SEED 5`, `RESTART SEED 4`: restart 1 from the `INIT` point); it now draws `seed_stream(SEED + r, kRestartPoints)` (`InitStream::Restart`), so the `RANDOM` and `LHS` points of every seeded `RESTART` round change. `SOBOL` draws no random numbers: its seed still selects block `SEED + r`, which an `INIT` block's `SOBOL` with that seed draws too. Without a `SEED` each round drew `std::random_device() + r` and the journal named no seed; a run now draws one 64-bit seed S from `std::random_device` at its first restart, round r draws with S + r as a block with `SEED S` does, and the journal says so once (`RESTART: the RESTART block has no SEED, so its restart points are drawn from std::random_device: restart r draws with S + r (SEED S in the RESTART block draws the same points again)`) and names the round's seed in its line (`RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED <S + 1>, SEED 6`, which named only `SEED 6`). A chain of `CONFIG` or `WARM` alone draws no seed (with a `SEED` its round lines still name `RESTART SEED <SEED + r>`, which draws nothing, as the startup warning about such a `SEED` says). Example 13 is re-pointed at a search that restarts: `NLOPT:LN_SBPLX` (`MAXEVAL 60`) with `RESTART` `AFTER_STAGNATION 3`, `MAX 2`, `METHOD RANDOM 1`, `SEED 13` climbs to the bound `RCUT2B = 6` in round 0 (eval 3) and stagnates there; restart 1, from 4.541125, is still falling (5.041125, 5.541125) when the window, which counts evaluations without a new best of the run, ends it after 3 evaluations, short of the bound; restart 2, from 5.422967, reaches the bound at eval 12 and stagnates there: 15 evaluations, `STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in 3 evals after 2 restart(s)`, the same best (1.015561e-01 at `RCUT2B = 6`), three runs identical; it ran `DLIB:BFGS`, whose restart points came from `std::random_device` (16 evaluations, only what did not depend on them pinned) until the repeat points above made its search converge before the window passed (9 evaluations, no restart). Its fingerprint, README and the examples page follow. Tests: `test_repeat_record.cpp` (`Run record: a RESTART round's RANDOM and LHS draw a stream of their own`), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Termination: a RESTART SEED draws restart round r's points with SEED + r`, `Termination: without a RESTART SEED the restart points come from std::random_device, and the journal names the seed`, and the round lines of four `RESTART` cases without a `SEED`).
- **BREAKING — `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA` is refused** in every phase (owner decision taken in MK's absence, 2026-10-12: the `OPTIM` box is the configuration's contract), an entry of the refusal table of the caps registry as `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` is: the parser refuses it at the line that closes its block (`ENDOPTIMIZER`, `ENDEXPLORE`, `ENDINNER`), in 1-D and 2-D, naming the phase and `NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA` (`Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA is refused: NLopt 2.10 runs LN_NEWUOA without the OPTIM box (optimize.c calls NEWUOA with lb = ub = NULL), so its evaluations leave the box and the best potential can lie outside the bounds the configuration sets (a 2-D run made 20 of its 25 evaluations outside the box, and its best was one of them); it also needs two OPTIM dimensions or more. Use NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA, Powell's bound-constrained successor of NEWUOA (its STEP rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius)`); no list of alternatives offers it, and the ALGO help prints the refusal. It was registered with `ignores_bounds` and a startup WARNING: a 2-D run of `MAXEVAL 25` made 20 evaluations outside the box and its best, `RCUT2B` 8.11 for a [4, 6] box, became `best_pot.tadah`; a 1-D run ended `FAILED -- dimension 1 must be >= 2` after no evaluation. `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`'s refusal names `LN_BOBYQA` alone (it offered `LN_NEWUOA` where the search may leave the box). The CERES drivers are the only bounds-ignoring backends now, and the WARNING at the first evaluation outside the box names `CERES:*` (it named `NLopt LN_NEWUOA` too). No example used `LN_NEWUOA`. `docs/issues/nlopt_newuoa_ignores_bounds.md`. Tests: `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_scale_validation.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`.
- **A gradient search whose budget cannot pay for one step is warned about at startup** (`validate_hpo_setup`, `gradient_budget_warnings`): a gradient search evaluates the loss at its start and the gradient there (`gradient_cost`: `NUMERICAL` 2n central, n forward, in n dimensions; `ANALYTICAL` 1, the counted analytical gradient; `HYBRID` at least 1) before any point it steps to, so a `MAXEVAL` below that + 2 ends it at its start (`MAXEVAL_REACHED`; measured: LD_LBFGS, DLIB:BFGS and CERES:LBFGS in 2-D take no step with `MAXEVAL` 4 or 5, a first one with 6). An MLSL whose local search takes gradients first evaluates its starting point and its first N samples without a gradient (N its `POPULATION`, NLopt's default 4), and its first local search starts at one of these points, a point the run has evaluated, whose loss the warning counts as the run spends it (`repeat_use`, the rule by which NLopt's objective and DLIB:BFGS answer a call from the run's record of its usable evaluations): where every evaluation of the run is a function of its point and `GRAD_STRATEGY` is `NUMERICAL`, the recorded loss is the centre of the finite differences and only their stencil is evaluated, so it needs N more evaluations (measured on the examples' Ta data: `GD_MLSL` with `INNER LD_LBFGS` in 2-D takes no local step with `MAXEVAL` 9, a first one with 10; `POPULATION 3`: 8 and 9): `the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GD_MLSL: MAXEVAL 9 takes no step of a local search: MLSL evaluates its starting point and its first 4 samples without a gradient (5 evaluations; POPULATION 4, NLopt's default), then its first local search, from one of them, takes the loss there from the run's record (no evaluation) and evaluates the gradient there (4 evaluations: ...) before it evaluates a point it steps to, which needs MAXEVAL 10 or more`; with a `--skip-rel` filter, whose run evaluates every repeat again, the start's loss is evaluated again and it needs N + 1 more (`... evaluates the loss there again (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (...) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 11 or more`; measured: no local step with 10, one with 11) -- under `NUMERICAL` the one reuse blocker such an MLSL can meet, as `PC_LAMMPS` refuses a gradient MLSL and `PC_OBSERVABLE` refuses `NUMERICAL`. Under `ANALYTICAL` or `HYBRID`, whose gradient follows an evaluation of its point (a `PC_OBSERVABLE` run is one of them), the start's loss is evaluated again too, then the analytical gradient (1 evaluation; `HYBRID`: and the stencil of each parameter it differences), N + 1 more than a local search: `... evaluates the loss there again (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (1 evaluation: one analytical gradient, GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 8 or more` (measured on the termination tests' Ta fixture: `ANALYTICAL` takes no local step with `MAXEVAL` 7, one with 8, and so does `HYBRID`, which differences no parameter there). AUGLAG goes straight into its local search. A search on NLopt or DLIB:BFGS that starts at a point the run has evaluated takes the loss there the same way (`start_is_recorded`): a PRESTAGE refinement starts at an exploration candidate, a usable evaluation (`the PRESTAGE refinement NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: MAXEVAL 4 takes no step: its gradient search takes the loss at its starting point from the run's record (no evaluation) and evaluates the gradient there (4 evaluations: ...) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 5 or more`; measured on the termination tests' Ta fixture: `MAXEVAL 5`, which the warning took for one evaluation short, steps at its fifth evaluation, with `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS` and `DLIB:BFGS`), and so does the run's first search under `NOISE_CALIBRATION`, whose seed point the calibration evaluated; evaluated again under `ANALYTICAL`, `HYBRID` or a reuse blocker (`... evaluates the loss at its starting point again (1 evaluation) ...`). Not covered, as the warning cannot know before any evaluation: a start whose evaluation was not usable, and the refinement from INIT points that a PRESTAGE whose exploration has no usable candidate falls back on. One WARNING per phase with a budget of its own that obtains gradients -- the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, each `MULTISTART` start (its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL`), the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement (its `TOPK` share) -- and per `INNER` block whose local search takes gradients and whose own `MAXEVAL`, in NLopt calls, is 1 (under an MLSL whose run answers its local starts' loss from the record, a call costs the gradient alone: `(4 evaluations: ...; MLSL's local searches start at points it evaluated, whose loss is taken from the run's record)`; so under an AUGLAG refinement, whose local search starts at the candidate: `its local search starts at a point the run has evaluated, whose loss is taken from the run's record`), on stderr and in the journal: `the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: MAXEVAL 4 takes no step: its gradient search evaluates the loss at a starting point (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (4 evaluations: central differences in 2 dimensions, GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL) before it evaluates a point it steps to, which needs MAXEVAL 6 or more`. A warning, not a refusal: such a probe of the start can be deliberate. Nothing said so. Tests: `test_gradient_budget.cpp`, `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`, which checks on a test function that the warning is given at exactly the budgets whose run takes no step, on hosts that reuse a repeated point, evaluate it again, or say neither, and `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Gradient budget: ...`, the same on `HPO_Host` through `hpo_run`, under `NUMERICAL`, `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID`).
- **At `VERBOSE` > 0 a run prints a kernel's settings line once** (`Kern_RBF | sigma: 1 | gamma: 1`; `Kern_Polynomial`, `Kern_Sigmoid`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ`): the line of the first model it builds, the `-c` model's, at startup. `hpo_run()` holds a Tadah.MODELS `KernelSettingsLineScope` for the run, and a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker one in which no kernel prints. A second run in the process, such as the second `TASK hpo` of a task file, prints the line of its own `-c` model. Every model a run builds printed it, the startup builds, the model of every evaluation and the potential each `PC_LAMMPS` script of an evaluation runs: 9 lines for 4 evaluations with one script, 7 for 4 evaluations without a script and with `BIAS true` (the released code built the trainer once per worker thread, and the `PC_LAMMPS` potential once per evaluation). With `OPTIM MPARAMS` the line gives the `-c` model's settings, not each evaluation's (`params.tadah` records those). At `VERBOSE 0` no line is printed, as before. Tests: `hpo_tadah_cli_kernel_line_once`, `hpo_tadah_cli_kernel_line_two_runs`.
- `init_script_executor(width)` builds the script executor of `width` workers in place of the one there is (it stored a width that only the first `script_executor()` call of the process read); `script_executor()` builds a one-worker executor at its first use outside a run, as before. `detail::script_executor_width()` is gone. The journal's `ScriptExecutor: <n> workers` line and its fan-out width are the executor's own width.
- **The tests need Python 3.11 or later** (`cmake/hpo_test_python.cmake`, `find_package(Python3 3.11 ...)`): the registry drift gate `hpo_anneal_registry_sync` runs `blueprint/gen_anneal_keys.py`, which reads the registry with `tomllib`. An older interpreter is refused at the configure step (`Could NOT find Python3: Found unsuitable version ..., but required is at least "3.11"`), and an older one first on `PATH` is passed over for a newer one; before, any Python 3 was accepted and the gate failed at test time (`No module named 'tomllib'`). Tests: `hpo_test_python_minimum_old` and `_current` (label `cmake`).
- The journal warning for a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker that died names its pid: `pc-worker 2 (pid 41237) killed/died: ...` (it was `pc-worker 2 killed/died: ...`), so it can be matched with the kernel's out-of-memory line.
- The pool checks the pid a worker reports in `HELLO_ACK` against the pid it forked; another pid fails the handshake (`HELLO_ACK names pid ..., but the worker was forked as pid ...`). It was read and discarded.
- **A candidate whose fit has a target that is not finite is an evaluation that threw, refused before the fit**: Tadah.MODELS' `LinearRegressor::train` refuses a design matrix or target with NaN or an infinity under every `OALGO`, as an intercept pinned by `FIXINDEX` (its `FIXWEIGHT` or `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` value) so large that its share of an energy overflows makes the target the free weights are fitted to. The evaluation scores `FAIL_SCORE`, `hpo_run.log` has `eval <n>: eval-thread caught throw, submitting FAIL_SCORE to keep writer queue contiguous (type=...): LinearRegressor::train: the target vector, less the part of the FIXINDEX weights, holds a value that is not finite (nan or inf) at row 1 (of 1 data row, then 6 ridge rows); no weights were fitted` and counts it under `Evaluations that threw`, and the health monitor records no weights for it. It was fitted, its weights were NaN or infinite and its loss was clamped to `FAIL_SCORE` (a `NaN/Inf clampings` count). Test: `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`[fixweight_e2e]`); its two cases of a fit with weights that are not finite pin the intercept at 2e307, which leaves the target finite and makes the `OALGO 4` weights overflow (they pinned 1e308).
- `best_pot.tadah` and every potential of an evaluation have no `DBFILE` line, whichever the model (Tadah.MLIP: a potential records no training data); an `M_BLR` run's had the training `DBFILE` lines. The header of `best_pot.tadah` still names the training datasets. With `LAMBDA -1` the potentials of an `M_KRR` run record `ALPHA` and `BETA` of the evaluation's fit, as an `M_BLR` run's did. A potential used as the `-c` configuration has no training data: add its `DBFILE` lines (without them the run is in the no-training-`DBFILE` mode, which needs every weight pinned, and is refused at startup otherwise).
- **`best_pot.tadah` and the `BDUMP` and `DUMP` archives have no `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` or `VERBOSE` line**: every potential file of an evaluation (the durable best too) is written through `PotHeaderWriter::write_potential_body`, which leaves out the keys of Tadah.MLIP `remove_run_inputs` (`NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE` and `AUDIT_SAMPLE` too, and `AUDIT` and `HEALTH_LOG` as before); every other key is written as before. The files carried the run's `--hpotarget`, `--validation` and `-v` values (`HPOTARGET config.hpo`, `VALIDATION config.val`, `VERBOSE 1`), so such a potential used as the `-c` configuration without `--validation` validated on the file its line named, which nothing on the command line asked for, and `VERBOSE` set the log level of whatever read it (a retrain, `pair_style tadah`). The evaluation's context keeps the run's settings, so the potential the `PC_LAMMPS` scripts run (`PC_ISOLATION` `THREAD` and `PROCESS`) and the gradient fan-out's are built at the run's `VERBOSE`, as before (built from a context without `VERBOSE`, a kernel model would print its line for every evaluation of a `-v 0` run). The journal still names the hpotarget and validation files (FILES) and the header of `best_pot.tadah` the validation datasets; a potential that has the lines still loads. The reference suite's fingerprints do not change (they hold losses, hyperparameters and counts). Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`The potentials of an evaluation record no input of the run`), `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_lammps_verbose` (new CTest entry: `-v 0` with `M_KRR Kern_RBF` prints no kernel line in either mode).
- **`best_pot.tadah`, its `.r<rank>` mirrors and the `DUMP`/`BDUMP` archives record the cutoffs and radial grids exactly whatever `NUMERIC` is** (Tadah.CORE: `Context::write_potential`, blueprint field `exact_in_potential`): `RCUT2B`, `RCUTMB`, `CGRID2B`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRID2B` and `SGRIDMB`, below 17 as the shortest text that reads back as the same value; every other number follows `NUMERIC` as before, and the `NUMERIC` WARNING (`hpo_run.log` and standard error) names those keys. They were rounded too, so a run with `NUMERIC` below 17 and a `D2_EAM` or `DM_EAM` descriptor wrote potentials that `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` refused (`D2_EAM: RCUT2B 4.51235 differs from the cutoff 4.51234567891 of the setfl file ...`, Tadah.MODELS' setfl-cutoff check of this release), and rounding the cutoff, a bump's centre and its width each on its own could move a `Cut_Dummy` bump that ended at or inside the cutoff past it, so the startup bump-support gate refused such a potential as the `-c` potential of the next run (`bump-support gate: ... CGRID2B bump 1 (centre 2, eta 0.666) reaches 5.003, past RCUT2B = 5 ...` for a `D2_Blip` bump of `RCUT2B 4.9964`, `CGRID2B 1.9951`, `SGRID2B 0.6664` written with `NUMERIC 3`). No example sets `NUMERIC`, so no fingerprint changes. Tests: Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo with NUMERIC below 17 writes potentials whose EAM cutoff predict accepts` and `Subcommand: a Cut_Dummy bump that train writes with NUMERIC below 17 stays inside the cutoff for predict and hpo`; `test_hpo_pot_digits.cpp` (the WARNING names the keys written exactly).

### Fixed
- **DLIB:BFGS does not evaluate the point it stands on again, and ends a search that no step can move** (`dlib_adapter.h`: the adapter runs dlib's `find_min_box_constrained` loop itself, `detail::bfgs_box_search`, with dlib's parts, so every step that moves the point is dlib's, bit for bit). At a point where each coordinate with a step is at a bound its gradient points out of, dlib's step held to the box cannot move the point: its line search evaluated the point again -- up to 100 times while the loss came back above its first value by rounding, which the warm-started `PC_LAMMPS` relaxation does (example 09: 28 of 30 evaluations at `RCUT2B = 6.0`; examples 01, 05, 26 and the reference machine's 12 or 30) -- and BFGS took its gradient there again. A line search whose first trial is its own start is not run: the point, its loss and its gradient stand (`DLIB:BFGS: the step from RCUT2B = 6 cannot move the point within the OPTIM box: the line search is not run, ...`; DIAGNOSTICS `Line searches not run`), and under FTOL_ABS the search ends `DLIB:BFGS: FTOL_ABS reached`. Under `PARAM tol_gradinf` (the norm of the whole gradient, not small at a bound) or `FTOL_ABS 0` such a search went on until MAXEVAL, and without MAXEVAL it never ended (14,295 evaluations of 10 points in 40 s on the audit's 2-D problem). BFGS then falls back to its steepest-descent step from the same point; its step length grows tenfold (to at most the whole step) only after a line search accepted its first trial, which on the unchanged loss happens only while the decrease the Armijo test asks for is below the rounding of the loss, so no later line search starts beyond ten times the largest such step, or the whole step (`detail::stalled_reach`). When the step that far cannot move the point either -- a coordinate at a bound its gradient points out of, a gradient of zero, a step below the rounding of the point, as next to a jump in the loss (whose finite difference is steep while the loss is flat) or one ulp inside dlib's 1e-8 gap of a bound -- no later step can, and the search ends `DLIB:BFGS: no step within the OPTIM box`, a new status (`EndReason::NoStep`), its stop line naming the coordinates (`SGRID2B[2] at its lower bound 4, with a gradient that points out of the box`; `A = 0.499999999, whose step is below the rounding of its value`). When it can, the line search starts there, once (dlib got there, if at all, only through iterations that each evaluated the point again), and if it comes back to the point the search ends with the same status (`from A = 0.5 the line search from the farthest step BFGS takes there (5.551115122e-14 of the steepest-descent step) found no lower loss and came back to the point`). So no two iterations in a row ask for no loss, and an iteration that asks for none checks the stop flag (SIGINT, SIGTERM) as an evaluation does. Every iteration that moves the point is dlib's. Example fingerprints regenerated: 01, 05, 13 and 26 end after 9 evaluations, 09 after 3, 22 after 3 and 23 after 9, all `DLIB:BFGS: FTOL_ABS reached`, where 01, 05, 09 and 26 accepted 0..30 and two statuses, the count depending on the sign of the relaxation's rounding (12, 30, 30 and 12 on the reference machine), and 13, 22 and 23 made 16, 4 and 12; example 13 no longer restarted: its search converged before 3 evaluations passed without a new best (the example now runs a search that restarts: *Changed*, the `RESTART` entry). The termination tests' DLIB:BFGS pins follow (51 to 44 evaluations, 9 to 6 under FAILSCORE_GRADING, a PRESTAGE refinement FTOL_ABS where it spent MAXEVAL 20). Tests: `test_repeat_points.cpp` (DLIB:BFGS cases: at a bound, next to a jump, in the gap of a bound, the line search from the farthest step, and `hpo_shutdown_bfgs_not_run`, label `shutdown`), `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **DLIB:BFGS ends a search that an iteration without an evaluation took back to a point where it stood** (`dlib_adapter.h`, `detail::bfgs_box_search` (e)): `DLIB:BFGS: back at an earlier point`, a new status (`EndReason::Revisit`), with the stop line `DLIB:BFGS stopped: back at an earlier point after 111 evaluation(s): the iteration from A = 0.89349363928454106 (the point of eval 109) made no evaluation: it asked only for points the run had evaluated, and took the search back to A = 0.89349363928412684 (the point of eval 98), where it had stood with the same loss and gradient`. The gradients the record holds are those the search formed where it stood, so such an iteration came back to a point where the search had stood, with the loss it had there: the iterations since left the loss where it was. On a plateau of the loss's rounding (every step passes the Armijo test, its decrease lost in the rounding of the loss) under a stop test that does not end a search whose loss stays -- `FTOL_ABS 0`, or `PARAM tol_gradinf` with a coordinate held at a bound -- BFGS goes round points it has: with the repeats answered from the record (above) it made no evaluation and never ended, MAXEVAL counting evaluations (a fuzz of DLIB:BFGS over random losses on [0, 1] and [0, 1]^2: 14 of 3000 searches, among them a smooth bowl with a coordinate held at a bound under `tol_gradinf`); without the record each lap was evaluated again, until MAXEVAL, and without MAXEVAL without end. dlib's own stop test comes first (under `FTOL_ABS` > 0 such a search ends `FTOL_ABS reached` there), and every iteration that evaluates is dlib's. It is a stop rule, not a proof that the search could not go on: of the fuzz's 3000 searches it ends 22 otherwise -- the 14 that spun, 7 that ran to MAXEVAL 400 on such a plateau (now after 18 to 113 evaluations, the same best) and 1 that ended `no step within the OPTIM box` after 144 evaluations (now 79, its best 2 ulp higher) -- and the other 2978 keep their evaluation counts, best (bit for bit) and status; 20 of them count fewer reused calls in DIAGNOSTICS (a gradient right after a reused loss is no second reuse, above). An NLopt search whose calls are answered from the record is bounded by NLopt's own `MAXEVAL`, which counts calls (`LD_MMA` held at the lower corner of [0, 1]^2: 20 evaluations, 56 of its 60 calls answered); with tolerances alone (`XTOL_REL` at u = 0) one that only asks again still does not end (NLopt's state is not visible to HPO; open). A gradient centred on a recorded loss evaluates its stencil, so it counts in MAXEVAL. Tests: `test_repeat_points.cpp` (`[revisit]`), `test_repeat_record.cpp` (`Run record: DLIB:BFGS's loop ends an iteration that went back to a point without an evaluation`).
- **Ceres' least-squares solvers (`CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT`, `DOGLEG`, `SUBSPACE_DOGLEG`) are refused at the line that closes their block, with their reason**: entries of the refusal table, so every caps lookup gives `CERES:<ALGO> is refused: HPO does not run Ceres' least-squares Solver (...): it minimises a sum of squared residuals with their derivatives, and HPO gives an optimiser one scalar loss, over which these methods degenerate. Use CERES:LBFGS, CERES:WOLFE, CERES:NLCG or CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT, ...`, and the parser names the phase and the line. The run stopped with `HPOEngine error: unknown optimizer CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT (not registered in optimizer_caps)`, no line and no alternatives: the HPOTargets constructor looked the backend up (resolving the `INIT` chain) before validate_backend() and run_inner() could give their deferral text. Tests: `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_hpo.cpp`, `test_phase_specs.cpp`.
- **An unknown `LIB:ALGO` is an error at the line that closes its block**, naming what is wrong (`require_registered_backend`, which `require_driver`, `require_inner_driver` and `reject_refused_backends` call with the phase): ``Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: unknown optimiser NLOPT:LD_FOO: LIB NLOPT has no ALGO LD_FOO; `tadah explain ALGO` lists the optimisers``, or `there is no LIB FOO (LIB is ASA, CERES, DLIB, NLOPT or TADAH)`; `optimizer_caps()` throws the same text with no phase. It threw `unknown optimizer NLOPT:LD_FOO (not registered in optimizer_caps)` from the HPOTargets constructor, with no line, and `reject_refused_backends` accepted an unknown backend, which `validate_phase_backends` then reported with no phase.
- **Every variable at fault in a `PC_LAMMPS` task is journalled**, in its one journal line, joined with `; `, in one order -- the line's variables (`--varloss`, the `PC_FAILPROBE`'s `S`), then the `--hotvar`, then the `--failvar` variables, each group in the line's order --, each with the LAMMPS error its read-back raised or why its value has no number: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (rb.in, occurrence 1): reading --varloss E after the script: its value 'abc' is not a number; reading --failvar F1 after the script: ERROR on proc 0: Variable F1: Divide by 0 in variable formula ...; reading --failvar F2 after the script: its value 'xyz' is not a number`. Only the first was named. The worker no longer fails a task for a `--hotvar` or `--failvar` read-back error: it hands on every value, each error beside its variable (`hotvar_errors`, `failvar_errors`, as `outvar_errors`), and the parent's judgement (`apply_task_result`, `hpo_host_impl.hpp`), the one place every variable is judged, fails the task as before (the variables failscored, the evaluation aborted, band B graded by the readable failvars). Tests: `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp` (the worker's per-variable errors, the task OK) and `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (the line's clauses and their order under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD`, and through the built `tadah` under `THREAD` and `PROCESS`).
- The ALGO help describes the CERES drivers (`print_caps`): the solver, the stops that end a run, `PARAM tol_gradinf`, `max_iter`, `max_ls_iter`, `max_lbfgs_rank` (LBFGS), `nlcg_type` (NLCG) and the finite differences, that `STEP` is rejected, and that they ignore the box. It printed `(no info)`. A refused backend of any library prints its refusal (it was NLopt's alone).
- **A build with `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES=OFF` compiles** (`CMakeLists.txt`). Eigen (3.4.0) is fetched and linked `PUBLIC` to `tadah.hpo` whatever the option: the public headers `adjoint_solver.h`, `lammps_hessian.h`, `observable_registry.h` and `sims_grad.h` (the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint gradient, the LAMMPS Hessian, the observables) include `<Eigen/Dense>` in every build. Eigen was fetched only in the Ceres block and linked only when its target existed, so without Ceres the configure step said nothing and 13 sources of `tadah.hpo` stopped at `Eigen/Dense: No such file or directory` (so since the adjoint kernels, in 1.3.0-beta.1 too); a missing Eigen is now a configure error. Without Ceres, `LIB CERES` is refused at startup and `tadah refit` refuses `REFIT_MODE compact` (Tadah.MLIP).
- **The `PC_LAMMPS` analytical chain adds the direct θ term** (`compute_sims_grad_dWchain`, `compute_sims_grad_for_script`; `sims_grad.h`). A θ that is a descriptor parameter changes the energy and the forces at the same weights. By the implicit function theorem dO/dθ of a relaxed LAMMPS observable is the chain through the weights, (dg/dW + λ·dF/dW)·dW/dθ, plus dg/dθ + λ·dF/dθ at fixed x* and W. The chain had only the first part, as the `PC_OBSERVABLE` chain had before 1.6.0. `SimsGradScriptInput` gains the fields of the second part (`dg_dtheta`, `dF_dtheta`, `direct_theta`), which a provider fills when it can compute them, and the chain adds them with each observable's λ. A script with observables whose input does not carry them sends every θ to finite differences, like an unknown expression. On an analytic stub (`test_sims_grad.cpp`: two atoms and an iso box DOF, θ in the descriptors) the chain now matches central differences of the loss to 2e-10 relative; through W alone it was 0.28 to 3.5 relative off. The path is not reachable: `PC_LAMMPS` refuses every `GRAD_STRATEGY` at startup. `HPO_Host`'s provider computes no direct term, as pair_tadah's adjoint snapshot has no θ derivatives, so that path now takes finite differences for every θ, where it took the chain through W alone. Tests: `test_sims_grad.cpp` (`[direct_theta]`), `test_hpo_analytical.cpp` (`[sims_grad_provider]`), `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`hpo_host_e2e`: the provider on a `PC_LAMMPS` script, every θ to finite differences).
- **`LammpsHessianAssembler`'s box block comes from the pair virial** (`lammps_hessian.cpp`; the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint chain, not reachable: `PC_LAMMPS` refuses every `GRAD_STRATEGY`). Each entry is the central difference of dE/ds_α = −tr(d_α F⁻¹ W), read from the virial W of each strained state, along the deformation the assembler applies (F and d_α from the strained boxes as LAMMPS holds them). The diagonal was the energy's second difference over h_strain² = 1e-10, which multiplies the energy's round-off by 1e10. On the 4-atom Ta cell of the tests the iso entry was 2.6e-6 and the xx entry 1.6e-5 off; on a 16-atom cell with the `HPO_test` potential the iso entry was 1.5e-4 off, and so was the lattice-constant chain dlx/dW against its finite differences (1.1e-4 at the case's step, against its 1e-4 tolerance). The off-diagonal took −dvirial/dε in the Voigt convention, the same for two normal modes. The chain's box part now matches the finite differences to 5e-9 with the shipped potential and 4e-7 with the `HPO_test` one. On an orthogonal box, whose tilts LAMMPS ignores, the shear rows are now exactly 0 (they were the energy's round-off). The rest of the chain's error, 1.2e-5 (iso) and 2.4e-5 (Voigt-6) with the shipped potential, is its atom part. That is λ's atom entries, round-off at the relaxed crystal, times pair_tadah's adjoint snapshot dF/dW, which is not the derivative of the forces (Tadah.LAMMPS, not changed here). Before this fix that part was partly offset by the diagonal's error: 6.0e-6 and 3.3e-6. Tests: `test_lammps_hessian.cpp` (the box block against the central difference of LAMMPS' pressure), `test_hpo_phase3_pilot.cpp` (the box part of the 3.7C and 3.7E chains against the finite differences' plateau).
- **Startup refuses `MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2` without an `SBASIS` it takes** (Tadah.MLIP `checked_sbasis`, through `model_weight_count`): a missing `SBASIS`, or one below the intercept vectors plus one, is refused as for a non-linear kernel, naming the model (`the -c configuration does not build a model: M_KRR Kern_Linear 2: SBASIS 1: the fit needs at least 2 basis vectors ...`). Implementation 2 draws its basis vectors for its solve with every kernel. Startup passed it (the weight count, `DSIZE`, was given before any `SBASIS` check; the released code had no `SBASIS` check at startup for any kernel), and every evaluation threw (`Evaluations that threw: 1 (100.0%)`, `no evaluation produced a usable loss`). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp` (`startup validation: SBASIS of M_KRR Kern_Linear 2 is checked`).
- **Every evaluation trains a model built from its own candidate.** `HPO_Host` builds a `Trainer` from the candidate context (the `-c` configuration with the candidate's `OPTIM` values) for every evaluation. A thread used to keep the `Trainer` of its first evaluation, rewrite only the `OPTIM` keys of its context and call `Trainer::reinit()` (MLIP), which re-applied the descriptor parameters only, so whatever the model, its kernel or basis function and its design matrix read when they were built kept the first evaluation's value, and what a fit wrote into the context stayed for the next. Consequences, now gone:
  - `OPTIM EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT` (the design matrix's row weights, every model) were not applied after a thread's first evaluation: every later evaluation trained the first one's weights, while the potentials recorded the candidate's values;
  - `OPTIM MPARAMS` (the kernel of M_KRR) kept the first value, while every potential recorded the candidate's: a `best_pot.tadah` predicted otherwise than the loss it was reported with;
  - `OALGO 3` potentials gained one `SIGMA` per evaluation of a thread (10, 20, 30 values for 3 weights), and `tadah predict --error` on such a `best_pot.tadah` corrupted the heap (Tadah.MODELS also fixes the append itself);
  - an M_KRR `-c` potential with `WEIGHTS` failed the first evaluation of each thread ("This object is already trained!"; Tadah.MODELS fixes the flag; every evaluation would fail now that each builds its model);
  - a second run in one process (a task file with two `TASK hpo` blocks) trained the first run's model, with the first run's `LAMBDA`, `FWEIGHT`, `FORCE` and every other key it does not search (and started its `PC_LAMMPS --hotvar` values where the first run ended: see below).
  The per-thread slot keeps the trainer of the thread's latest evaluation for `analytical_gradient` and the observables, stamped with its host: `analytical_gradient` of a host whose evaluation did not build the slot's trainer routes every parameter to finite differences (it differentiated another host's model). Building a `Trainer` costs little against a fit (0.023 ms against 0.40 ms for D2_BP with 3 functions on 80 atoms with forces); the setfl file of D2_EAM and DM_EAM is parsed once while its contents are unchanged, and the descriptors' configuration warnings are logged once per process (Tadah.MODELS). The line a kernel prints when it is built at `VERBOSE` > 0 (`Kern_RBF | sigma: ...`) is printed once per run, not for the model of every evaluation (see Changed). Other searches give the losses they gave (a search over a setfl cutoff now stops at startup, see Added): of the 27 reference examples, 24 write a byte-identical `loss.tadah`, `18_anneal_pt` and `19_anneal_pa` the same rows in another completion order (their concurrent replicas), and `13_restart` the same evaluations up to its restart, whose unseeded start point differs from run to run. With `LAMBDA -1` the evidence approximation starts every evaluation from the configured `ALPHA` and `BETA`, not from the previous evaluation's. Potentials from earlier runs that searched `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT`, `SWEIGHT` or `MPARAMS` report an optimum that was never applied: re-run those searches. Tests: `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` (CTest `hpo_trainer_e2e`), Tadah.MLIP's `Task file: a second TASK hpo evaluates its own configuration`.
- **Every run starts its `PC_LAMMPS --hotvar` values from their declared initial value, or from WARM's seed.** The per-chain cache of the values each evaluation hands to the next (`warmstart_cache.h`) is cleared when a run starts (`HPO_Host::prepare_best_publication`, `warmstart::reset_for_fresh_run`); it lived as long as the process, so the second run of a task file with two `TASK hpo` blocks started each hot script from the value the first run's last evaluation converged to, and did not reproduce the same run made alone (example `01_minimal` at `MAXEVAL 3`, `DIGITS 15`: `TOTAL_LOSS/N` 1.043183703093665e-01 against 1.043183703095260e-01; now the second run's `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah` and `best_pot.tadah` are those of the run alone, under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` and `PROCESS`). WARM's seeds, primed before the run, are kept (and since 2026-10-12 an earlier run's WARM seeds are not: `warmstart::begin_run`, the entry on the journal of a second run). The evaluation counter still continues across runs, so the second run's `ITERATION` numbers and `DUMP` names follow the first run's (4-6 after a run of 3). Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`A second run in the same process starts its --hotvar values from their declared start or the WARM seed`), `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (CTest `hpo_hotvar_e2e`).
- The pc-pool test `killing the worker process is contained and respawned` takes the worker's pid from `worker_pid` and has no skip path. It found the pid with `ps --ppid | awk`, and where that found none (no procps `ps` or no `awk`, as in a minimal container) it warned and passed after the first round trip, while the two cases that need the same pid failed there; the `ps`/`awk` helper is gone.
- Two durable-best cases of `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`hpo_durable_e2e`) have no `WARN`-and-return path: `RESULT distinguishes the last saved best from the unsaved one` requires its `FAILED` ending, which its search reaches every time (`NLOPT:LN_SBPLX` draws no random numbers; the second new best, eval 3, is the unsaved one, eval 1 the last saved), and `durable best: hpo_run ends FAILED after storage recovers` checks both outcomes of its four threads, a candidate saved after the failure or none (then `best_pot.tadah` and RESULT keep the last best saved before it): in either, `best_pot.tadah`'s `HPO_SOURCE_EVAL`, `Best potential saved` and RESULT's `Last saved best` or `Unsaved candidate` line name the eval of the journal's last new-best line, which is written once a best is saved. A new case, `... ends FAILED when storage does not recover, keeping the last best saved before the failure` (the lock fault `count=always`), takes the outcome without a save after the failure every time. Each returned with a warning, checking nothing more, when its run took the other path.
- `hpo_host_impl.hpp` includes `hpo_host.h`, whose `HPO_Host` templates it defines: included before `hpo_host.h` it did not compile (`expected initializer before '<'`). Including `hpo_host.h`, as the code does, was not affected.
- **The per-evaluation health monitor (`HEALTH_LOG`) checks the weights of every evaluation.** It read the column layout of `Trainer::dm` (Tadah.MLIP), which nothing builds -- M_BLR and M_KRR fit their own design matrix, and the fast path of a `FIXINDEX` that pins every weight builds none -- and skipped an empty layout, so it never recorded an evaluation: none of its warnings, the NaN/Inf one included, was printed since it was added. `HPO_Host` now passes the weights the model holds (fitted, or provisioned from `FIXWEIGHT`) and the layout `DesignMatrix::build` gives the model's design matrix, `fb->phi_layout(dc.layout())` (bias, D2 and DM blocks for `BF_Linear` and `Kern_Linear`; one block over all columns for a non-linear function, a kernel's over its basis vectors). Defects of the monitor that its first records showed:
  - `compute_iter_health` checked for NaN/Inf only the weights inside the layout's blocks; it checks every weight;
  - a layout with more columns than weights was taken for NaN/Inf weights (a sentinel) and the block it could not read for a block of zero weights; a layout that does not describe the weights is reported as such, `iter_health: eval <n> -- the model has <N> weights but its column layout has <M> columns: the per-block checks are skipped.` (`... the column layout of the model's <N> weights is not contiguous from column 0: ...` for blocks that do not partition as many columns as weights), rate-limited like the other warnings, and the per-block checks are skipped;
  - a NaN weight counted as an exact zero (the comparisons `|w| > max` and `|w| > 1e-15` are false for NaN), so a block of NaN weights, the case of a diverged fit, was reported as a block of zero weights, `iter_health: eval <n> block[<b>/<label>] max_abs -- all weights in this block are exactly zero -- the descriptor block contributed nothing to the fit.`, after the NaN/Inf line (and in `summary` mode that warning's 100-evaluation cooldown hid a block that was zero in the next evaluations), and a block of mostly NaN weights as one with `fewer than 5% of weights in this block exceed 1e-15.`; a block that holds a NaN or infinite weight is flagged (`IterHealthRecord::block_has_nan_inf`), gets no aggregates and no block check, and the NaN/Inf warning describes it;
  - `IterHealthMonitor` changed its maps without a lock (its header asked callers to funnel records through one thread), while `HPO_Host`'s evaluations record concurrently (`ANNEAL_PT`/`ANNEAL_PA` replicas, `DLIB:GFS` batches): it is thread-safe (8 threads recording 400 records each counted 2730 to 3021 of the 3200 warnings).
  The warnings are printed on standard output (core Logger at WARNING), not in `hpo_run.log`. The `DM-skip fast path` test checked that `Trainer::dm` had an empty layout, which held for every evaluation; it checks that no MLIP parallel loop runs over the training structures on the fast path (one does when a weight is fitted). Tests: `test_iter_health.cpp` (a non-finite weight outside the blocks, a layout that does not describe the weights, a block of NaN or mostly NaN weights, concurrent records), `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`[fixweight_e2e]`: the fast path and a fit with a block of zero weights, a fit whose weights are not finite, a fitted block of NaN weights).
- **Every run has worker pools of its own and stops them however it ends** (`RunPools`, `hpo.cpp`). `hpo_run()` builds the script executor of the run's `THREADS` and installs MLIP's parallel backend where it sizes its pools; on every way out -- a return, a signal stop, an error, also one at startup after the pools were built -- it removes the backend, stops the `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker processes and joins the executor's workers, whose thread-local `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` LAMMPS instances and potentials end with them (they did at process exit). Before:
  - a run that threw (at startup after the worker processes were spawned, or an error `drive_run()` rethrows) left them running until process exit, so the next `hpo_run()` of the process failed `pc_worker_pool_init: pool already initialized` (a program that calls `hpo_run()` again; a task file stops at its first failed task);
  - the executor was a function-local static built at its first use, so every later run of the process (a task file with two `TASK hpo` blocks) ran on the first run's `THREADS` while its journal reported its own, and under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` a script of the same name and line options ran on the LAMMPS instance the first run had cached, with the script text that instance had rendered from the first run's file (a script rewritten between the runs ran as the first run had read it); under `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` a later run with a smaller `THREADS` handed tasks to worker ids its pool did not have (`PcWorkerPool::execute: bad slot`: in each of 8 runs of the test every evaluation failed, `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`);
  - MLIP's parallel backend outlived the run, so a later task of the process (`TASK train` after `TASK hpo`) ran its kernels on the HPO pool instead of OpenMP.
  The evaluation counter still carries over (the second run's `ITERATION` numbers continue the first's), and so do an earlier run's WARM hot values and the journal's tallies (the `--hotvar` entry above). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (two runs in one process: the first failing after its worker processes started; `THREADS` 2 then 3; `THREADS` 3 then 1 under `PROCESS`; under `THREAD` each run's LAMMPS instance ends with the run, its log closed with LAMMPS's `Total wall time` line, and the second run runs its rewritten script), `test_script_executor.cpp` (`init_script_executor` gives the executor the width it asks for).
- **`FIXINDEX` is read strictly**, with CORE's `parse_fixindex`, as the trainers read it, by `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT`'s slot mapping and labels (`HPOTargets`, without the weight count), `validate_hpo_setup` (through MLIP's `classify_weight_source`, with it) and `derive_raw_embedding_f0`. `FIXINDEX 13 14` (two values) maps `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT (14)` to `FIXWEIGHT` slot 2, labelled `FIXWEIGHT[14]`, as `FIXINDEX 13,14` does; the lenient CORE `parse_indices` dropped the joined values, and the line was refused with the self-contradicting `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT (14): weight 14 is not pinned by FIXINDEX (13 14)` (`validate_hpo_setup` said `FIXINDEX names 0 weights`, and `derive_raw_embedding_f0` did not see the weights pinned). A descending range (`14-13`, which gave the same message) is refused as such, and a weight named twice is refused: `13,13,14` with two `FIXWEIGHT` values was deduplicated and the run went ahead. `validate_hpo_setup`'s branch for a model without weights (K = 0), with its own lone-key and count checks, is removed: `probe_model` refuses such a model first (`The descriptor size is 0`), so it was not reached. Tests: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`, `test_startup_validation.cpp` (whose `size mismatch` section pins `FIXINDEX 1,2` for one value: its `1,2,3` also named weight 3 of the 2-weight model, which is refused first).
- **`tadah hpo` refuses `FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT` with M_KRR implementation 2 at startup** (`validate_hpo_setup`, through MLIP's `classify_weight_source`): `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2 cannot pin weights: ...`, its solve fitting every weight at once. The setup passed, and every evaluation trained with the pins ignored (an `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` line searched values no fit used). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp`.
- **A signal that one rank of a multi-rank `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` run (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) receives ends every rank as a signal stop.** The ranks agree on how each phase ended (`agree_on_rank_stop`, `run_stop.h`): rank 0's seed/T0 calibration, ANNEAL_PT's calibration pass 2, every `ReplicaPool` epoch (population initialization, the automatic step warm-up and pilot, replica rungs, walker sweeps) and the `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` calibration. The most severe exception of any rank decides, as `ExceptionStash` decides among threads (an error before a signal stop before a stagnation stop): a rank whose own exception is of that kind rethrows it, another throws the error or `PeerShutdown`, a `core::ShutdownRequested` that names the signal and the lowest rank that stopped on one. `drive_run` ends such a rank with `SHUTDOWN (<signal> on MPI rank <r>)` and 128 + the signal; a rank that received the signal itself still reports `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)`. A rank that had not seen the signal ended with an error, status 1 (`FAILED -- TADAH:ANNEAL_PT: rank 0 aborted during seed/T0 calibration`, `... during calibration pass 2`, `... worker failed on another MPI rank during <phase>`). A jitter-calibration probe that threw on one rank (a signal or an error) hung the job: that rank left the calibration before its gathers and waited in `drive_run`'s collective, the others waited in the gathers, no rank returned to `tadah`'s exit handshake, and the job ran until it was killed from outside; such an error now ends every rank with status 1 (`... the INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO calibration failed on another MPI rank` on the others). `NOISE_CALIBRATION`, which every rank runs on its own before the driver, has no such agreement: a signal or an error there that only some ranks see now ends the job through `tadah`'s exit handshake, which aborts it 30 s after that rank's run ends (its `drive_run` collective meets the others' first agreement, and they then wait in the next one); before, that collective met the others' seed/T0 broadcast, and Open MPI 5 aborted the job at once (`MPI_ERR_TRUNCATE` in `MPI_Allreduce`; collectives that do not match are an error in MPI, so another MPI library need not report it). Tests: `hpo_mpi_signal_*`, each scenario checking the phase of the evaluation the signal fell in.
- **The LAMMPS instance that reads a `PC_LAMMPS` script's variables at startup writes the line's `-log` / `-screen` file with the rank's `.r<rank>` suffix in a multi-rank run** (`lmp.log.r0`, `lmp.log.r1`; unchanged on one rank), as every rank-local output does; every rank wrote the one file the line names. Test: `hpo_mpi_e2e_eamfast`.
- The MPI build journals a `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` request (`PC_ISOLATION: THREAD (requested; the only mode of the MPI build)`): its journal had no `PC_ISOLATION` line then (its default already had one). Test: `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` in that build.
- **`tadah hpo` refuses at startup a training `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice** (`validate_hpo_setup` calls Tadah.MLIP's `require_distinct_training_datasets`), naming both entries: the same path, two spellings of it or a link to it. Every evaluation fitted the file's structures twice (an `M_BLR` potential written before records its `DBFILE`, so one used as the `-c` configuration with the `DBFILE` added listed it twice). Test: `test_startup_validation_dbfile.cpp`.
- **The startup refusal of a `-c` configuration whose model does not build ends the model's reason with one full stop** (`probe_model`): `the -c configuration does not build a model: M_KRR Kern_RBF 1: SBASIS 1: ... Set SBASIS to 2 or more. Every evaluation would fail; ...`. It appended `. Every evaluation would fail` to every reason, so one that ends in a full stop (the `SBASIS` refusals, the `MPARAMS` ones and most others) read `..` (also in 1.6.0). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp`.
- **A validation `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice is refused** (`require_distinct_validation_datasets`, called by `HPO_Host` when it reads the `--validation` file, before any validation dataset is read; the rule of the training `DBFILE`, Tadah.MLIP `repeated_dataset`): `the --validation file config.val: DBFILE lists one dataset twice: entry 1 (...) and entry 3 (...) are one file, ...`, naming the same path twice, two spellings of it or a link to it, and the indexed `PC_ERMSE (i)` / `PC_FRMSE (i)` lines as the way to weight datasets. Every validation metric averages over the validation datasets, and the duplicate counted twice in it (on the examples' `elastic` and `eos_bcc` datasets with `elastic` listed again, the first evaluation's `ERMSE[avg]` was 1320.9 meV/atom, the mean of 881.3, 2200.3 and 881.3). Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp`, Tadah.MLIP `Subcommand: hpo refuses a validation DBFILE that lists one dataset twice`.
- **`--validation` takes one file** (`validation_config_file`, at startup): a `VALIDATION` of two or more values (`--validation a.val b.val`) is refused naming them; `hpo_run()` read the first, and the others were not read. The validation datasets are the `DBFILE` lines of that one file. Test: Tadah.MLIP `Subcommand: hpo --validation takes one file`.
