- The refusal of a run without a training `DBFILE` and without `ATOM` ends with the advice of `tadah train`'s refusal of a configuration without `DBFILE` (Tadah.MLIP `potential_has_no_datasets_advice`: a potential records no `DBFILE`; add a `DBFILE` line to a copy of it), as do the startup refusals of `classify_weight_source` (Tadah.MLIP), which `tadah hpo -c best_pot.tadah` without a `DBFILE` line meets. `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` without a training `DBFILE`, which startup lets through only with every weight pinned, is still refused advising `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`, without that advice: with a `DBFILE` line the pins are refused next (`FIXINDEX pins model weights`). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp` (a pin).

## [1.6.0] — 2026-09-25

### Added
- Tests: `test_pair_tadah_notices.cpp`, through the real `pair_style tadah` (LammpsRunner, log.lammps read back): the notices of a deprecated key (`SIGMA`, `MBLOCK`) and of `EMBF0` without DM_REAM are LAMMPS warnings in log.lammps, once, and not on standard error, in the log.lammps of every LAMMPS instance of a process (a second instance had none: the notice was given once per process); a potential with `WCOV` and `WNOISE` loads and computes the energy of the potential without them, bit for bit (Tadah.LAMMPS, Tadah.CORE).
- `run_end.h`: `EndReason`, `end_status()`, `record_end()` and `report_end()`, the vocabulary every TADAH, DLIB and CERES driver records why its search ended in (*Changed*); `GradientProvider::exhausted()`: whether `MAXEVAL` is spent, so the Ceres adapter tells an evaluation refused for the budget from a failed one.
- Per-dataset `PC_ERMSE` / `PC_FRMSE` targets and weights (indexed form `PC_ERMSE (i) <target> <weight>`).
- ASA: `PARAM Gener_Mov_Avr 0` is accepted and switches off the adaptive block resizing, so every generated block holds `Gener_Block` states. Upstream's README documents 0 as "`Gener_Block` is not changed thereafter" and `asa()` resizes only when the window is above 0, but its option check rejected every value below 1, so no setting kept the block size fixed: with `Gener_Block = Gener_Block_Max` the moving average could still shrink it to 1. TADAH-PATCH 7 in `external/asa/asa.c` (`TADAH-PATCHES.md`). Opt-in: the default window (3) and the golden-neutrality gate are unchanged. The block-run banner prints `Gener_Mov_Avr`. Tests: `test_asa.cpp` (every block of a `Gener_Block = Gener_Block_Max = 4`, `Gener_Mov_Avr 0` run holds 4 states; the default window shrinks them).
- Worked examples 22-26 (`examples/`, each with a README and a reference fingerprint): `22_pc_observable` (`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy` in place of `PC_LAMMPS`, `DLIB:BFGS` with the resolved `ANALYTICAL` gradient; a local `config.tadah` with `OALGO 4`, `LAMBDA 1e-6`), `23_per_dataset_targets` (`PC_ERMSE (1)` / `(2)` over a local two-dataset `config.val`, a per-dataset `--skip-above`), `24_optim_auto` (`OPTIM SGRIDMB (1) AUTO`, which resolves to example 03's box and reproduces 03), `25_failprobe_grading` (`FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`, `PC_FAILPROBE` with the documentation's probe, now `examples/common/lammps/failprobe.in`; a `--hotvar` lattice window grades band-A failures of example 02's seeded search: four since the `elastic.tadahdb` cells were corrected, six before) and `26_pc_isolation` (`PC_ISOLATION PROCESS`, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`; example 01's results in either isolation mode). A reference fingerprint can list lines `hpo_run.log` must contain (`"journal"`, each a regex, optionally left out when an environment variable has a given value, with a `NOTE` line; `tools/compare_fingerprint.py`, copied from `tools/tolerance_overrides.json` by `generate_fingerprint.py`): 25's requires its band-A graded failures (four), which the losses and hyperparameters cannot show, and 26's the `PC_ISOLATION: PROCESS` line and the 600 s hard deadline, not applied under `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION=THREAD`.
- The examples' parse guard (`tests/test_examples_parse.cpp`): every `examples/*/config.hpo` is a CTest case of its own that parses the file in a scratch copy of the example, resolves its validation targets and runs `validate_hpo_setup`, as the example's driver would run it. The case list is generated from the directory when the build configures (`CONFIGURE_DEPENDS`), and `examples/tools/parse_guard.manifest` names what each example needs (e.g. the `seed_pot.tadah` that 09 and 10 bootstrap, the pinned weights of 17): an example without a manifest line fails the guard, and so does a manifest line without an example. The directory is passed as `TADAH_HPO_EXAMPLES_DIR`.
- `GradientProvider::failure` (`GradientFailure`, `GradientFailureInfo`, `gradient_provider.h`): why `gradient()` returned false — `Budget` (MAXEVAL spent), `Stencil` (a failed evaluation the gradient reads: the loss at the point, under every `GRAD_STRATEGY`, or a finite-difference stencil point, see *Fixed*) or `NotFinite` (another cause: an analytical gradient that cannot be formed or is not finite, a quotient that overflows between usable losses) — with the point and the stencil point for the journal. `gradient_stop_status()` and `report_gradient_stop()` give a search that a point without a usable gradient ended its status and journal line. The DLIB, NLopt and Ceres adapters read it (*Fixed*).
- `OptimizerSpec::failscore_grading` and `HPOTargets::set_failscore_grading()`: the `FAILSCORE_GRADING` the finite differences read (a graded failure at the point is differenced, *Fixed*). `HPOTargets` sets it in the outer spec, its `INNER` block, the `PRESTAGE` exploration and that one's `INNER` block; `set_failscore_grading()`, which the `TADAH_FAILSCORE_GRADING` override of `hpo_run()` uses, sets `FAILSCORE_GRADING` and all of them; the hand-written copy constructor keeps it, so the `MULTISTART` starts, the `PRESTAGE` refinement and each `RESTART` round carry it.
- `single_local_search()` (`phase_specs.h`): the local search a run is, when it is one — the spec itself for a local backend, and for `AUGLAG`, which HPO runs without constraints, so it runs its local search once from the starting point, that search — and `nullopt` for a global one (`MLSL`, `GD_STOGO`, …). The NLopt adapter stops a gradient-based one at a starting point without a usable gradient and reads the `MAXEVAL` and `MAXTIME` of `AUGLAG`'s local search (*Fixed*).
- `seed_stream.h`: `seed_stream(seed, salt, idx)`, the `std::mt19937_64` an optimiser's random consumer draws for its `SEED` (seeded through a `std::seed_seq` of the seed, the consumer's salt and an index; `anneal::make_stream` forwards to it), and `seed_streams`, the registry of the consumers' salts (the `ANNEAL` family's `kAnnealChain`, `kAnnealProbes`, `kAnnealPtSwap`, `kAnnealPaResample`, `kAnnealWarmup`; `kRandomSearch` for `TADAH:RANDOM`, `kAsa` for `ASA`, `kRefinementPhase` for the key of a `PRESTAGE` refinement), checked pairwise distinct at compile time (*Fixed*); `StreamPhase` (`Search`, `Refinement`) and `phase_stream_seed(seed, phase)`, the seed every random stream of an optimiser run is drawn from: the `SEED` itself in a search, a key derived from it (the first number of `seed_stream(SEED, kRefinementPhase)`) in a `PRESTAGE` refinement (*Fixed*).
- `OptimizerSpec::stream_phase`: the `StreamPhase` of the spec; `prestage_refinement_spec()` sets `Refinement` (the copy constructor keeps it, so the refinement's `MULTISTART` starts have it too).
- `testing::ScopedAnalyticalHelperFailure` (`hpo_host.h`): a test seam; while an object lives, `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` fails its gradient helper with `std::runtime_error(what)` at every pass that reaches it, as the helper throws where it cannot form the gradient at a point whose fit trained (*Fixed*).
- Tests: `test_ream_fastpath.cpp` (DM_REAM through the real pair style, EAMFAST vs generic, EMBF0 ZERO/FREE: isolated atom 0.0, forces/virial bitwise equal across references; the EAMFAST and generic kernels agree under either reference in energy, fnorm, virial pressure and forces), `test_hpo_embf0_e2e.cpp` (hidden `[embf0_e2e]`: CEMBFUNC/SEMBFUNC-only search, every candidate's isolated atom exactly 0), `test_hpo_embf0_journal.cpp`, EMBF0 cases in the startup-validation, init-strategy and compact-fitter tests.
- **A drift gate for the example fingerprints** (CTest `hpo_example_fingerprint_overrides_sync`, `examples/tools/generate_fingerprint.py <examples> --overrides tools/tolerance_overrides.json --check`): every field of a `reference_fingerprint.json` that the generator takes from `tolerance_overrides.json` (tolerances, `evals_tol`, accepted statuses, journal checks, the tolerance rationale) must equal that file, and every overrides entry must have a fingerprint. The rationales of 01, 05 and 26 had been edited in the overrides only. The generator and the gate share one definition of those fields (`tolerances()`, `carry_over()`), so a regenerated fingerprint always passes; a regeneration drops journal checks the overrides no longer have, and the gate reports a fingerprint that still accepts statuses or keeps journal checks the overrides dropped. Unit tests: CTest `hpo_example_fingerprint_tool` (`examples/tools/test_generate_fingerprint.py`).
- Worked example `27_dm_ream_embf0`: `NLOPT:LN_SBPLX` over the two negative DM_REAM embedding knots of a `tadah refit` seed under `EMBF0 ZERO`; its `run.sh` asserts that the isolated-atom energy is exactly 0 for every evaluated candidate. The examples' parse guard takes a `config <file>` directive for an example whose driver passes its own file with `-c` (`tools/parse_guard.manifest`).
- `ctest -L e2e`: the hidden host end-to-end groups run under CTest, one entry (process) each: `hpo_fixweight_e2e` (`[fixweight_e2e]`: pinned and `OPTIM`'d weights, exact potential files, seed anchors, LAMMPS read-backs), `hpo_durable_e2e` (`[durable_e2e]`: best-potential persistence, transient I/O and run endings; it spawns `test_hpo` children), `hpo_embf0_e2e` (`[embf0_e2e]`), `hpo_rcut_e2e` (`[rcut_e2e]`), `hpo_rhoform_e2e` (`[rhoform_e2e]`: `RHOFORM` through `hpo_run`, the positive-form projection), `hpo_rhogate_e2e` (`[rhogate_e2e]`), `hpo_rhobargate_e2e` (`[rhobargate_e2e]`) and `hpo_bumpgate_e2e` (`[bumpgate_e2e]`: the feasibility gates reject before training). They never ran under CTest. `[rcut_e2e]`'s `OPTIM` box now contains its `CONFIG` seed, which the seed-in-box rule (1.5.0) requires; it failed on that rule before reaching its check. A `[durable_e2e]` case checks that a `MULTISTART` setup that would fail every start (an analytical gradient without a training `DBFILE`) is refused at startup, before any start; `run_starts`' unit test covers a failure confined to the starts. The three cases that run the built `tadah` executable, which `TADAH_BINARY` names (`[tadah_cli]`: two WARM seed anchors and the exit status of a persistence failure), have entries of their own, `hpo_tadah_cli_seed_anchor_warm`, `hpo_tadah_cli_seed_anchor_nlopt` and `hpo_tadah_cli_durable_exit`: in a build with the `tadah` target the entry sets `TADAH_BINARY` to it, and without `TADAH_BINARY` the case calls `TADAH_SKIP`, so CTest reports it as Skipped instead of passing it unchecked. `archive writer never deletes a file it could not open` (`test_append_log.cpp`) is reported as Skipped when it runs as root, where permissions are not enforced.
- `test_strict_parse.cpp`: the text `core::format_roundtrip` writes for every zero or normal double (the bounds of `hpo_optim_resolved.hpo`, the hpotargets `tadah refit` writes) reads back through the hpotarget number grammar (`parse_number`) bit for bit. A subnormal is the one value the grammar refuses, with its too-small note: the grammar stays decimal-only with no hex, `inf`, `nan` or subnormal, while the CORE readers of potential, configuration and dataset files (`core::to_double`) accept subnormals.
- **Transient filesystem errors are retried before a write fails.** On
  network filesystems a lock, write, fsync or rename can fail for a moment
  (EBUSY, EAGAIN, ESTALE, ETXTBSY, EIO, ENOLCK, ETIMEDOUT, EDEADLK; EACCES
  only when the directory is on a network filesystem -- on a local disk it
  is a permission error and fails at once) and succeed when repeated. The
  durable best potential retries its whole lock → read → compare → replace
  transaction with exponential backoff (10 ms doubling to 2 s, ±20 %
  jitter), releasing its locks between attempts, until 30 s have passed
  since the first failure (the failing calls' own time counts; a call that
  blocks by itself can add its duration at either end). A retry that finds
  its own earlier rename in place (the directory fsync failed, or an NFS
  rename reported ENOENT after landing) completes that commit. Other
  permanent errors (ENOSPC, EROFS, ENOENT, ...) are not retried. The
  per-evaluation logs (`loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah`, `params.tadah`,
  `stats.tadah` and their `best_*` twins), whose write errors were never
  checked, are appended one batch per writer drain
  (`io::RetryingAppendFile`): a failed batch -- a partial write included --
  is truncated back and rewritten, with ONE 10 s budget per drain shared by
  every file and `DUMP`/`BDUMP` archive (each still makes its own attempt,
  plus one immediate re-attempt after a permanent errno). A batch that
  still fails is dropped whole and reported with the rows lost, and that
  file gets single attempts until a write succeeds (repeats summarised, an
  INFO line when writes resume); the run goes on. Archives are written
  whole or not at all. After a shutdown signal retrying goes on for at most
  2 s (`BackoffPolicy::stop_grace`, per durable publication and per drain),
  so one blip after a scheduler's time-limit warning does not lose the
  in-flight best or the last rows. Every retry is a WARNING; DIAGNOSTICS
  gains `Transient I/O retries : N (best-potential a, per-eval
  logs/archives b); rows lost c; archives lost d` only when one of them is
  non-zero, so clean runs write byte-identical journals and files. New API:
  `durable_best::SysError` (errno, operation, path, `may_have_landed()`),
  `durable_best::Options` with `default_backoff()` and 4-argument overloads
  (the 3-argument forms keep the default policy), `io::BackoffPolicy`,
  `io::retry`. Test-only knobs: `TADAH_HPO_TEST_IO_FAULT="site,ERRNO
  [,skip=K][,count=N|always][,partial=B][,land][,latency=MS][;...]"` and
  `TADAH_HPO_TEST_IO_BACKOFF_MS="initial,cap,budget[,jitter]"`
  (`io::testing::ScopedFaults` in-process); with no faults installed an I/O
  call costs one relaxed atomic load. Tests: `test_durable_best.cpp`,
  `test_append_log.cpp`, `[durable_e2e]` in `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp`.
- **Density gates `RHOGATE` and `RHOBARGATE`** (root keys; `rho_gates.h` / `rho_gates.cpp`), for a model whose many-body term is a bare DM_REAM (DM_mJoin is refused). Both reject a candidate before training, right after its context is built and before any MPI traffic, as a graded band-D failure with the `BUMPGATE` bookkeeping (evaluation number, rows, stagnation, `record_fail_band`; `DUMP` potential header-only).
  - `RHOGATE <tol> [R_LO <r>] [NPTS <n>] [SEED ERROR|WARN]` (`RHOFORM SIGNED` only; the positive forms guarantee rho >= 0): scans rho_raw(r) = f_c sum a_n psi_n with `ReamDensity::scan_grid` on NPTS points (default 20001) of [R_LO, RCUTMB] (default R_LO = 0.15 RCUTMB) and fails when rho_min < -tol rho_max, with v = min(1e8, v_peak + v_int) (the worst dip plus its mean depth, relative to rho_max).
  - `RHOBARGATE <bcc|fcc|hcp> <a0> <f_lo> <f_hi> [NPTS <n>] [SEED ERROR|WARN]` (every form): fails when the monatomic lattice density rhobar(a) = sum_s m_s rho_raw(d_s a) is not strictly decreasing on a = a0 [f_lo, f_hi] (NPTS points, default 1001; shells enumerated once for the largest reachable RCUTMB), with v = min(1e8, sum of the rises / max |rhobar|); a flat step fails at v = 0.
  - Saturated measures (DBL_MAX, the top of band D): a non-finite density, rho_max <= 0 or rhobar = 0, a pair weight <= 0, an empty window. A density DM_REAM would refuse gets no verdict (it fails in training as before).
  - Strict grammar: the numbers follow the number grammar of every hpotarget key (`strict_parse.h`: `tol`, `R_LO`, `a0`, `f_lo` and `f_hi` are single finite decimal tokens, so no hexadecimal float, inf, nan or subnormal; `NPTS` an integer in decimal digits, so no sign, fraction or exponent; errors name the line); keywords and values case-insensitive, duplicates, unknown or repeated keywords and missing values refused; no environment override. Startup checks (`validate_hpo_setup`): the bare DM_REAM, `RHOGATE` under `SIGNED` only, `R_LO` and the first `RHOBARGATE` shell inside the smallest reachable RCUTMB (knot-aware).
  - Arming: a gate is evaluated per candidate, its pair weights included; it is armed by an OPTIM line (in any case, anywhere in the file) on `AMPGRIDMB`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRIDMB` or `RCUTMB` (`HPOTargets::density_gate_keys`). A gate without one cannot act (every candidate has the `-c` density and its one verdict; an OPTIM `WATOM` only scales it) and is an error at its line naming those keys (owner decision 2026-09-29; it was accepted with a "not armed" INFO line and the `-c` verdict reused). A pair weight no candidate can make positive is a startup error under either SEED mode.
  - Seeds: the `-c` potential and every CONFIG/WARM point of the resolved INIT chains and of an active RESTART block's METHOD chain (`RestartSpec::seed_points`; `RESTART WARM <file>` in the messages) are evaluated as candidates at startup; `SEED ERROR` (default) stops the run with one message naming every failing seed, `SEED WARN` warns. One INFO state line per gate.
  - Combined grading: when any gate fails every configured gate is evaluated, the candidate is graded by min(DBL_MAX, the sum of the failing gates' v) and counted for the first failing gate (BUMPGATE, RHOGATE, RHOBARGATE); its reject line names the others. Reject lines (`%.4e`) are rate-limited per gate (first 5, then powers of two). SUMMARY gains ` rhogate=N` / ` rhobargate=N` next to ` bumpgate=N`, DIAGNOSTICS `of which rho-sign gate rejects: N` / `of which lattice-rhobar gate rejects: N`.
  - Verdicts are pure functions of the candidate and the settings; the gates read the candidate context through stored values only, so the context and every written potential are the same with and without them. Measured cost per candidate: 0.7 ms (RHOGATE, 20001 points, 20 Blip5 bumps), 0.3 ms (RHOBARGATE, 1001 points).
- **Density report** (`HPOLog::write_density_report`, `record_density_best`), for every run with a bare DM_REAM many-body term, gated or not: POTENTIAL SETUP rows `Density form`, `Density rho(r), -c pot` (window, extremes and their r, rel = rho_min/rho_max, the largest forward-difference slope, "non-increasing on window: yes/no" with a tolerance of 8 eps times the largest term sum, the pair weights W, the RHOGATE verdict) and `Density rhobar` under RHOBARGATE, one set per distinct WARM seed; RESULT repeats them for the best (`n/a (not derivable)` when the density could not be built at startup). A summary that cannot be computed prints `not derivable: <reason>`; the report never throws. Example 27's `hpo_run.log` gains these rows; its fingerprinted outputs are unchanged.
- **Positive-form projection.** Under `RHOFORM NONNEG`/`MONOTONE`, `HPO_Host::operator()` evaluates and records a copy of the candidate with every negative `AMPGRIDMB` coordinate set to 0 (before BUMPGATE and the context build; NaN is left to DM_REAM), so every recorded point is a valid potential. A search that keeps to the box has nothing projected: finite differences keep to the box with either step rule (*Changed*, the bounded stencil); only an optimiser that ignores the bounds still reaches below 0, and the host counts that evaluation as one outside the box before projecting it. The first projection is an INFO line (`... (a point outside the OPTIM box, which only an optimiser that ignores the bounds evaluates) ...`), and DIAGNOSTICS prints `Positive-form projection: N evaluation(s) ...` when N > 0. A startup WARNING names each phase whose optimiser ignores the bounds when `AMPGRIDMB` is OPTIM'd (the phases of `strategy_phase_specs`; `ignores_bounds`: the Ceres line searches, `LN_NEWUOA`: `the PRESTAGE refinement CERES:LBFGS (ignores the OPTIM bounds)`). It also named a phase that took `NUMERICAL` gradients with the Ceres kernel, and said such a step halved the derivative at a zero amplitude; that kernel keeps to the box now, and the clause is gone. No change under `SIGNED` or inside the box.
- **Hard lower bounds in the bound-occupancy audit** (`BoundOccupancy::set_hard_lo`): an `AMPGRIDMB` lower bound of exactly 0 under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` is marked `LO*` with a note that 0 is an admissible optimum, and the widen-that-side advice and HINT are printed only for other marks.
- INIT provenance: `InitOrigin` and `OptimizerSpec::optim_init_origin` (parallel to `optim_init`), filled through `InitStrategy::generate_tagged`; a chain keeps the first origin of a de-duplicated point. `detail::update_candidate_context` (`candidate_context.h`) is the extracted body of `HPO_Host::update_context` (now a one-line call), so startup seed checks build a seed's context exactly as the host builds a candidate's.
- Tests: `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_rho_gates.cpp` (+ the frozen references in `rho_gate_fixtures.h`: extremes bitwise, verdicts at five tolerances and every v bitwise on the nine campaign fixtures, ALL_E rising at 1307 of 3501 / 373 of 1001 steps), `test_compact_refit_rhoform.cpp`, `compact_fitter_signed_goldens.h` (the SIGNED compact fitter bitwise against the fitter before this change), RHOFORM cases in `test_compact_fitter.cpp`, `test_bound_audit.cpp` and `test_ream_fastpath.cpp` (through `pair_style tadah`: the `RHOFORM` banner line on file and `EXTERN` loads, the migration text in the error of a key-less potential, `NONNEG` equal to `SIGNED`, and `MONOTONE` EAMFAST matching the generic kernel); hidden tags `[rhoform_e2e]`, `[rhoform_projection_e2e]`, `[rhogate_e2e]`, `[rhobargate_e2e]`, `[rho_gates_bench]`. Every DM_REAM fixture declares `RHOFORM`.

### Changed
- **The potentials of an `OALGO 3` run carry `WCOV` and `WNOISE`, the covariance of their own fit** (each evaluation's potential and `best_pot.tadah`, from the model's `get_param_file`, Tadah.MLIP): the `SIGMA` they carried was the transposed and unscaled matrix of Tadah.MODELS' `get_sigma`. Test: `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` ("Each OALGO 3 evaluation writes the SIGMA of its own fit", which checks `WCOV` and `WNOISE` against an independent Trainer's).
- **BIAS true with two or more species (BREAKING, see Tadah.MLIP): the trainer's descriptor has one intercept column per ATOM species**, as pair_style tadah's does, so the PC_LAMMPS potentials and the analytical gradient of a multi-species BIAS configuration use the layout the weights were trained in. Before, from 1.3.0-beta.1 (when pair_style tadah, the ridge and the analytical gradient took one intercept column per species while training kept one shared column), every PC_LAMMPS candidate of such a configuration gave wrong LAMMPS energies and forces, and the analytical gradient built its design matrix with the DC_Selector width (N + D columns) from descriptors of 1 + D entries, reading past each atom's descriptor: results of such runs from 1.3.0-beta.1 until this version are invalid. With 1.2.0 and earlier, pair_style tadah used one shared intercept column too, and PC_LAMMPS evaluated such potentials consistently. The analytical gradient refuses (`std::logic_error`) a DC_Selector width other than the trainer's DSIZE. Tests: `test_multispecies_bias.cpp`.
- **Startup applies the NORM/BIAS rules of `tadah train`** (`validate_hpo_setup` calls `mlip::check_bias_constraints`: NORM true with BF_Linear or Kern_Linear needs BIAS true, BIAS true needs ATOM), which every evaluation of such a configuration would now fail, and prints a WARNING when FIXINDEX is used with BIAS true and two or more species, naming the model's intercept weights (a linear model's weights 1 to N, its descriptor weights from N + 1, where they started at 2 before 1.6.0; `BF_Polynomial2`'s products (k,k); a non-linear kernel's, those of the basis its fit draws, which starts with the intercept vectors: a dot-product kernel's weights 1 to N), for FIXINDEX/FIXWEIGHT and `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` alike. The host refuses, when it loads the training data, BIAS true data whose energy-weighted compositions cannot determine the intercepts of the model's function (`mlip::require_intercepts_determined`), a non-linear kernel's too: those of the basis its fit draws, not of a basis the `-c` seed holds (an M_KRR implementation 2 seed, not checked, was refused when it was a potential, and a dot-product kernel passed startup and failed every evaluation). Startup refuses an `SBASIS` below the intercept vectors plus one drawn vector (N + 1 with BIAS true on N species; it took 2), which every evaluation's fit would refuse. The probe `Trainer` reads no `WEIGHTS` of the `-c` potential, so the EMBF0 journal takes them from the configuration (`derive_raw_embedding_f0`). The EMBF0 ZERO hint says intercept columns, one per ATOM species. Tests: `test_multispecies_bias.cpp`, `test_startup_validation.cpp` (its NORM true cases set BIAS true, so they still test the rule they name).
- `make_nlopt` (`validate_nlopt_spec`) refuses an INNER spec with an INIT chain or an INIT `K_TOTAL` (`NLopt INNER spec with an INIT chain: MLSL and AUGLAG choose the INNER search's starting points; an INIT block belongs to the enclosing block`), as it refuses an INNER `SEED` and `GRAD_STRATEGY`: only a spec built outside the parser can carry one, and nothing resolved it. Test: `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **The journal counts an evaluation that threw as one, not as a NaN/Inf clamping** (`EvalLoss::Threw`). An evaluation whose training or prediction raised an exception scores `FAILSCORE`, as before, and DIAGNOSTICS count it under `Evaluations that threw : N (x%)`, with `[!] N evaluation(s) raised an exception and scored FAIL_SCORE; each has a WARNING "eval <n>: eval-thread caught ..." with its message.` when N > 0; the milestone `SUMMARY` adds `threw=N`, and `NaN rate` counts NaN/Inf losses only. It still counts as an unusable loss. Such an evaluation was counted as a NaN/Inf clamping: a run whose every training threw (`OALGO 3` with `BIAS true` and `LAMBDA` > 0) read `NaN/Inf clampings : 3 (100.0%)` and `[!] 100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss that was clamped.`, though no loss was computed. The `LOSS FUNCTION` section's `Fail score` line says what scores `FAILSCORE` under the run's `FAILSCORE_GRADING`: under `OFF` `(the loss of a failed evaluation: a NaN/Inf loss, an exception, a skip cap, a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term or a gate reject; the ceiling of every loss)` (for a run with `PC_OBSERVABLE` lines; `a failed PC_LAMMPS simulation` for one with `PC_LAMMPS` lines, neither for a run with neither: the failed terms the run can have, `HPO_Host::assemble_loss`), under `ON` `(the loss of an evaluation with a NaN/Inf loss or an exception; the ceiling of every loss)` and a `Fail grading` line, `ON -- a skip cap, a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term or a gate reject scores a graded loss in [0.999, 1) x Fail score` (the same failures); until 2026-10-12 every run's lines named a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation and none a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term, which since the partial-failure entry scores `FAILSCORE` or band B; it said `(returned when an evaluation produces NaN/Inf)` whatever the grading. (Under `OFF` a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation scores `FAILSCORE` whatever loss its variables are measured with: see the *Fixed* entry on an evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation.) The reference suite's comparator (`examples/tools/compare_fingerprint.py`) checks the new counter against the fingerprint's `threw_max` (0 by default, from `tolerance_overrides.json` like `nan_clampings_max`), so an example with an evaluation that threw still fails it, as it did while such an evaluation counted as a NaN/Inf clamping; a journal without either counter line fails it (a missing NaN/Inf line left its check out). Tests: `test_hpolog_new_best_guard.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`No usable loss: evaluations that throw count as unusable losses`, which asserted the NaN/Inf wording), `examples/tools/test_compare_fingerprint.py` (CTest `hpo_example_fingerprint_compare`), `test_generate_fingerprint.py`.
- **Every optimiser records why its search ended, and the run's trailer says so: `COMPLETED` is no longer written.** The TADAH (`RANDOM`, `GRID`, `ANNEAL`, `ANNEAL_PT`, `ANNEAL_PA`), DLIB (`GFS`, `BFGS`) and CERES drivers record `<LIB>:<ALGO>: <reason>` with a fixed reason (`run_end.h`, *Added*: `MAXEVAL reached`, `MAXTIME exceeded`, `STOPVAL reached`, `FTOL_ABS reached`, `FTOL_REL reached`, `XTOL_REL reached`, `tol_gradinf reached`, `max_iter reached`, `T_MIN reached`, `FTOL plateau`, `all grid points evaluated`, `no optimisable dimensions`); the journal's stop line gives the numbers, a tolerance's value with `(default)` when its key was not set (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: FTOL_ABS reached after 51 evaluation(s): the loss changed by less than FTOL_ABS = 1e-07 (default) from one iteration to the next`). A Ceres ending without such a reason is named by Ceres' termination type and the head of its message, a fixed text too (below). NLopt keeps its result strings, ASA `ASA: <reason>`, a gradient that is not finite its own status and `RESTART` its stagnation status. Before, these drivers recorded none for most endings and `HPOLog::finish` wrote `COMPLETED`: `DLIB:BFGS` for a spent `MAXEVAL` (its only line was `DLIB:BFGS -- MAXEVAL=N reached; preserving best-so-far`, which stays its stop line) and for its convergence test, which left no line at all; `DLIB:GFS`, `TADAH:RANDOM` and `TADAH:GRID` for a spent `MAXEVAL` and the whole grid; the annealers for every stop, `STOPVAL` and `MAXTIME` included; the CERES drivers for every ending but a rejected gradient's, a `FAILURE` such as `STEEPEST_DESCENT`'s `Numerical failure in line search` included. Per driver:
  - `DLIB:BFGS`: `MAXEVAL reached`, `FTOL_ABS reached` (dlib's `objective_delta_stop_strategy`: the loss changed by less than `FTOL_ABS`, 1e-7 by default, from one iteration to the next) or `tol_gradinf reached` (`gradient_norm_stop_strategy`: the gradient's L2 norm below `PARAM tol_gradinf`).
  - CERES: Ceres' own reason when it converged, read from its message (`Function tolerance reached` → `FTOL_REL reached`, `Parameter tolerance reached` → `XTOL_REL reached`, `Gradient tolerance reached` → `tol_gradinf reached`; another message → `CONVERGENCE (<head>)`) or ran out of iterations (`max_iter reached`; another → `NO_CONVERGENCE (<head>)`); `MAXEVAL reached` when an evaluation refused for the budget made it fail (`GradientProvider::exhausted`, *Added*); `STOPPED -- CERES:<ALGO>: FAILURE (<head>)` for any other failure, the best standing, as after NLopt's `STOPPED -- nlopt roundoff-limited`. `<head>` is the head of Ceres' message, its text before the first `. `, `, ` or `: ` (`detail::ceres_message_head`): `STOPPED -- CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT: FAILURE (Numerical failure in line search)`, where the message goes on with the run's step size, cost and gradient. `USER_SUCCESS`, and `USER_FAILURE` without the host exception that is its only cause, are internal errors. The stop line (`CERES:LBFGS stopped: FTOL_REL reached after 45 evaluation(s): FTOL_REL = 1e-06 (default); Ceres: Function tolerance reached. ...`) gives Ceres' message for a converged solve too; it was logged only for one that did not converge.
  - `DLIB:GFS`: `MAXEVAL reached` for a spent `MAXEVAL`. A `MAXTIME` that ended dlib's run time with calls left records `MAXTIME exceeded` as before, now decided from the budget left and the `MAXTIME` set rather than by reading the clock again, and any other ending with calls left is an internal error.
  - `TADAH:RANDOM`: `MAXEVAL reached`; `TADAH:GRID`: `MAXEVAL reached` when it cut the grid short, `all grid points evaluated`.
  - `TADAH:ANNEAL`, `ANNEAL_PT`, `ANNEAL_PA`: from their stop line, on every MPI rank: `MAXEVAL reached` (also during the T0 probes, or before any proposal), `MAXTIME exceeded` (also during the probes), `STOPVAL reached` (also on a probe), `T_MIN reached`, `FTOL plateau` (the plateau and `ANNEAL_PT`'s best-stall rule), `no optimisable dimensions`; a signal records none (`SHUTDOWN`). Serial `ANNEAL`'s rung loop has no loop condition of its own any more: a `MAXTIME` that passed between its post-rung test and its loop test ended the search `loop completed normally` with no reason; it ends `MAXTIME exceeded`. With `REANNEAL` (the default) a `MAXTIME` that passed after the schedule's last post-rung test and so prevented a re-anneal that was due is `MAXTIME exceeded` too, in the stop line and the status: it was `T < T_MIN` and `T_MIN reached` (a spent `MAXEVAL` cannot end it there: the schedule ends on `T_MIN` only with budget left). With no re-anneal due (its start temperature `T_INIT * REANNEAL_SCALE^n` below `T_MIN / RT`) the schedule's end is the search's, `T_MIN reached`, whatever the time, as under `REANNEAL OFF`: the re-anneal driver tests that before `MAXEVAL` and `MAXTIME`. An end without a reason is an internal error.
  A normal end without a recorded status is an internal error (`drive_run`, right after a best potential that could not be saved, which outranks it, and before the no-usable-loss test, which it outranks): `std::logic_error`, the trailer `FAILED -- internal error: the optimiser ended without recording why it ended`, the queued rows written, exit status 1. `HPOLog::finish` without a status writes `(no status recorded)`, and `run_starts` adds the failed-starts note only to a recorded status (it made up `COMPLETED (N of M MULTISTART starts failed; see the WARNINGs)`). Under `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE` the trailer stays the last start's or the refinement's status: its `MAXEVAL reached` is that start's budget. The examples' `status_accepted` lists name the new statuses: 01, 05, 09 and 26 accept `DLIB:BFGS: FTOL_ABS reached` and `DLIB:BFGS: MAXEVAL reached` (their evaluation count is decided by rounding-level noise at a clamped bound), 03, 14 and 24 drop `COMPLETED`, which NLopt never wrote; no loss, hyperparameter or evaluation count changes. The example scripts print the whole status through `common/journal_status.sh`; they cut it at its first colon (`TADAH`, `ASA`, `STOPPED -- stagnation`). A script that tells success from failure tests the exit status, not the status text. Tests: `test_backend_end_status.cpp` (the annealers', `DLIB:GFS`'s and the CERES drivers' endings on stub hosts, the three Ceres convergence messages pinned), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`DLIB:BFGS records why it ended`, `drive_run refuses a normal end that recorded no status`, a normal end without a status among the endings that reach the cross-rank agreement once and before the final flush, and `End status: every registered backend records why it ended ...`: every backend of the caps registry under `PLAIN`, `MULTISTART`, `PRESTAGE` and `RESTART`), the annealers' stop-line tests, `test_policy_host_exceptions.cpp`, `tests/mpi/test_anneal_mpi.cpp` (every rank's status, and a normal end without a status on the last rank only, which ends that rank with the internal error while the others complete; `TADAH_HPO_MPI` builds, CTest `hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix`), CTest `hpo_example_journal_status`.
- **The example data hold the free energy and the Cauchy stress.** `examples/common/data/eos_bcc.tadahdb` and `elastic.tadahdb` were re-converted from their VASP OUTCARs with the 1.6.0 readers. They held VASP's pressure-signed stress and the energy without entropy. Cells, positions and forces are unchanged, the stress is negated, and each energy moved by its F − E_wo (−2.9 to +1.5 meV/atom), with the same +3.69807734 eV/atom reference (`common/data/README.md`). No example fits or validates stress, so the energies alone move the results.
  - Fingerprints regenerated, 26 of 27 (27 trains nothing). Best losses, old → new: 01 0.1031156 -> 0.1015561; 02 0.06794962 -> 0.06671894; 03 0.07674671 -> 0.07510413; 04 0.07786907 -> 0.07591893; 05 0.1031156 -> 0.1015561; 06 0.1004139 -> 0.09899923; 07 0.07359754 -> 0.07257811; 08 0.06618197 -> 0.06509313; 09 0.1031156 -> 0.1015561; 10 0.1031156 -> 0.1015561; 11 0.0627496 -> 0.06181516; 12 0.06264287 -> 0.06399745; 13 0.1031156 -> 0.1015561; 14 0.06263755 -> 0.06173949; 15 0.07670039 -> 0.07507771; 16 0.1058929 -> 0.1043184; 17 4.249349e-08 -> 3.917428e-08; 18 0.04890901 -> 0.04781589; 19 0.06337421 -> 0.06242866; 20 0.07669833 -> 0.07507819; 21 0.06260619 -> 0.07044785; 22 0.06842257 -> 0.06603426; 23 0.002618601 -> 0.00263443; 24 0.07674671 -> 0.07510413; 25 0.06794962 -> 0.07502931; 26 0.1031156 -> 0.1015561.
  - Evaluation counts (four runs identical): 01 and 26 end after 16 (30 before), 05 runs to MAXEVAL = 30 (16), 13 takes 19 (18).
  - Example 25 grades six failures (four): example 02's four best candidates leave its lattice window, so its best is evaluation 4.
  - Example 23's skip-above threshold stays at RCUT2B ≈ 4.06 Å (the 5 meV/atom crossing moved from about 4.0575 to 4.0615 Å).
  - Example 16's best weight is −22.99, at evaluation 6 (+25); its tolerance rationale says that any probed weight can be the best.
- **`PC_FRMSE` and `PC_SRMSE` leave out the validation structures stored with weight 0** (force weight 0, `fweight`, for `PC_FRMSE`; stress weight 0, `sweight`, for `PC_SRMSE`), per dataset, in the metric and, for `PC_FRMSE`, in its analytical gradient (MLIP `Analytics`, `analytics_grad`); `tadah data convert` stores a structure without a stress tensor, and a `.castep` structure without forces, with that weight 0 and zeros in their place, which the metrics compared with the prediction. No example uses these metrics; no fingerprint changes. A validation dataset in which every structure has that weight 0 is not measured (MK, 2026-10-06): its value and loss term nan in stats.tadah, loss.tadah and the best_pot.tadah result table, left out of `FRMSE[avg]`/`SRMSE[avg]` and of the gradient (`ValidationMetricTargets::set_measured()`: no loss term, slope 0, a global target the loss of the average over the measured datasets), and named by one journal line at startup. A run is refused at startup when no validation dataset has the data, or when an indexed `PC_FRMSE` gives such a dataset a weight above 0, naming the metric, the file and the weight. Test: `test_validation_follows_metric_e2e.cpp` (cases 2a, 2b, 4a).
- **Validation follows its metrics.** `FORCE` and `STRESS` (from `-c`, or `-F`/`-S`, which take precedence) decide only what training fits, as in `tadah train`; validation predicts forces whenever `PC_FRMSE` is set and stresses whenever `PC_SRMSE` is set, whatever the potential was fitted with. `best_pot.tadah` keeps the training `FORCE`/`STRESS`. The analytical gradient covers `PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false` (the `PC_FRMSE needs FORCE true` refusal is gone). With `FORCE true` and no force metric, validation no longer predicts forces nobody reads (about 4× faster validation in the measured case); a validation energy may then differ in the last bit for `D2_LJ`, `D2_ZBL` and `D2_Blip5`, whose energy-only descriptor expressions are written differently.
- **`FORCE` and `STRESS` in the `--validation` file are refused at their line** (also in a file it includes); they were accepted and ignored.
- **POTENTIAL SETUP states what training fits and what validation predicts:** `Fit forces (FORCE) : no  (configuration file c.tadah)`, `yes  (command line -F/--force; overrides FORCE false in c.tadah)`, `(default)`; `Fit stresses (STRESS)`; `Validation predicts : energies, forces (PC_FRMSE)`. `hpo_run(ctx, provenance)` takes the task's `KeyProvenance`; `hpo_run(ctx)` journals `(source not recorded)` for a key the context holds.
- Framework version `1.6.0`, read from CORE's `blueprint/blueprint_tadah.toml` after the Tadah dependencies are fetched, so the module reports the right version in every layout (sibling checkout or FetchContent). A missing blueprint is now a configure error instead of a silent `0.0.0`.
- Tests take Catch2 and its test discovery from cmake-tools (`tadah_setup_tests()`, `include(Catch)`); the vendored `tests/catch2/catch.hpp`, `tests/Catch.cmake` and `tests/CatchAddTests.cmake` are removed. Every discovered CTest entry now selects exactly its own test case and passes `--warn NoTests`, so an entry that matches no test case fails instead of passing. New test sources are picked up without a manual re-configure (`CONFIGURE_DEPENDS`).
- The test `MultiStartPolicy: validate rejects NLopt GN_*` is renamed `... rejects the NLopt global GN_DIRECT` (what it checks). Catch cannot select a name that ends in `*` on its own (the `*` is a wildcard), and discovery now refuses such names.
- cmake-tools is fetched at `v1-dev` instead of `main`.
- The `ASA_SAVE` option hint says HPO runs are not resumable (it said HPO had its own checkpoints).
- The `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` error and the worker-process guard of the MPI build name `TADAH_HPO_MPI` only; `hpo_mpi.h` no longer mentions the legacy build.
- **A `SEED` in the `RESTART` block seeds its `METHOD` chain**: restart r draws its fresh points with `SEED + r`, so the restart points of a run are reproducible. The key was accepted and ignored, and every restart drew its points from `std::random_device`, as it still does without a `SEED` (one draw per run, which the journal names, and a stream of the restart points' own: [Unreleased], *Changed*). The journal's `RESTART r/MAX` line shows the seed first: `RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED 43, SEED 6`. It is a separate setting from the optimiser seeds `RESTART` derives for each round (`SEED + r·S`, `EXPLORE` `SEED + r`), and the restart points come from a stream of their own, not the optimisers' (the `TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` entry below), so the two may be equal. The first round, which starts from the `INIT` points, is unchanged; the fingerprints of examples 12 and 13 are unchanged (neither sets a `RESTART` `SEED`). Like an `INIT` block's `SEED`, one that no `METHOD` of the chain draws from (only `CONFIG`/`WARM`, or no `METHOD` line) gets a startup warning.
- **A Sobol seed selects a whole block of K points** (`INIT` and `RESTART` `METHOD SOBOL K`): seed s draws block b = s mod 65536 (the seed's 16 low bits, as before), the points b·K … b·K + K − 1 of the sequence, so seeds that differ modulo 65536 draw disjoint points: the seeds `SEED + r` of consecutive restart rounds, and an `INIT` and a `RESTART` block with the same `SEED` and K. A seed skipped s points, so with a `RESTART` `SEED` consecutive restart rounds (`SEED + r`) shared K − 1 of their K fresh points (`METHOD SOBOL 3`, `SEED 42`: restart 1 drew points 43–45 and restart 2 points 44–46), and an `INIT` block with the same `SEED` and K overlapped restart 1. `SOBOL 1` is unchanged: seed s draws point s mod 65536. K is the number of points the method draws (`K_TOTAL` for a lone `SOBOL`). A block that would run past the end of the 64-bit sequence is an error naming the block and K. Example 08 (`STRATEGY SOBOL 4`, `SEED 31337`) starts from other points (125348–125351 instead of 31337–31340); its fingerprint is regenerated (best loss 0.06875824, was 0.071539), and its README and config comment say what the seed selects (the comment said Sobol has no run-to-run variance without `SEED`: the block then comes from `std::random_device`). No other example uses `METHOD SOBOL`.
- **`tadah refit` `MAXEVAL` counts objective evaluations exactly, for every NLopt and dlib algorithm** (each call of a stage's objective, for its value or its gradient, is one), and refit results change for three of them when `MAXEVAL` is set: EXPLORE `LIB DLIB ALGO GLOBAL` runs `MAXEVAL` evaluations (it took `max(500, MAXEVAL/20)` calls), dlib's `LBFGS` stops after `MAXEVAL` evaluations counted in its objective wrapper (it capped its L-BFGS iterations: at `MAXEVAL` in EXPLORE `INNER`, 50 × `MAXEVAL` in REFINE), and REFINE `LD_LBFGS` takes `MAXEVAL` evaluations (it took 50 × `MAXEVAL`). Ceres' `LM` and `DOGLEG` still count iterations (`max_num_iterations`), as `MAXITER` says. Without `MAXEVAL` the budgets of `GLOBAL` and of NLopt's REFINE `LD_LBFGS` are the old effective ones, 3000 evaluations (`CompactConfig::dlib_global_maxeval`) and 50000 (`lbfgs_refine_maxeval`); dlib's `LBFGS` keeps the numbers of its old iteration caps, 20000 in EXPLORE `INNER` and 50000 in REFINE, now counted in evaluations (at least two per L-BFGS iteration, as dlib asks for the value and the gradient in separate calls), so a default run whose cap binds stops sooner than before; the runs measured on `Fe_mm.eam.fs` ended on their tolerance long before it. `apply_stage_optimizers` moves next to the fitter (`compact_fitter.h`); the refit log gives each stage's budget in its unit and the evaluations each stage spent (`term=DLIB_GLOBAL:MAXEVAL_REACHED(3000ev)`), and `CompactBlipFitter::objective_evaluations()` counts them.
- **The order of the optimiser's dimensions is kept, and documented as the contract**: by `OPTIM` key (alphabetically, byte-wise), then by index within the key — for `FIXWEIGHT` by `FIXINDEX` slot — whatever the order of the `OPTIM` lines. It fixes the `params.tadah` / `best_params.tadah` columns, the `hpo_search_space.tsv` rows, the journal's `dim` numbers and the index of ASA's `PARAM <Name>[i]`, and it applies to the hpotargets `tadah refit` writes. Declaration order was considered for 1.6.0 and not adopted: it would have silently retargeted existing ASA `[i]` options and changed same-`SEED` trajectories of configurations whose lines are not in key order. The test `params.tadah columns: iteration then slot then OPTIM dimensions sorted by key and index` pins it.
- Optional-fixture tests are reported by CTest as Skipped, not Passed: `hpo_strategy_matrix` exits with 77 (its `SKIP_RETURN_CODE`) when `TADAH_HPO_INTEGRATION_BUNDLE` is unset, and the Corana 1987 oracle (`hpo_corana1987_protocol`) calls `TADAH_SKIP` (cmake-tools' TADAH-SKIP marker) while its reference table is empty. A 77 from the strategy matrix itself is reported as a failure. The hidden LAMMPS-driven benchmark cases (`bench_pair_tadah.cpp`, `test_hpo_phase3_pilot.cpp`, `test_lammps_hessian.cpp`) also call `TADAH_SKIP` when there is no potential (`TADAH_BENCH_POT` unset and no default), instead of passing after a warning; a potential that `TADAH_BENCH_POT` names and that cannot be read, or any potential that does not parse or whose LAMMPS set-up fails, is now a failure, where the Hessian cases skipped. That exposed a set-up defect of these cases, now fixed: none initialised the periodic table, so building the potential failed (`Unknown symbol: Ta`) whenever one ran on its own.
- Building the tests requires Python 3 (`find_package(Python3 ... REQUIRED)`), as the umbrella build already did. The registry drift gate `hpo_anneal_registry_sync` was registered only when Python was found, so without it the gate silently did not exist.
- `optimizer_caps(lib, algo)` takes the names as `std::string_view` (the test helper `anneal_functions::by_name` too). The result refers to the static registry, but a call with a literal bound a temporary `std::string`, and GCC 13's `-Wdangling-reference` reported the reference bound to the result as possibly dangling at six sites (`test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` 340 and 372 among them). The build has no `-Wdangling-reference` warning left; no pragma.
- **`PC_OBSERVABLE` columns are `L(<tag>)[k]` in `loss.tadah` and `<tag>[k]` in `outvar.tadah`, with `k` the 1-based ordinal of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` line** (`Observable::column_tag()`; `cohesive_energy`'s tag is `Ecoh`, so `L(Ecoh)[1]`, `Ecoh[1]`), and the journal names the term `cohesive_energy[k]` in the loss section (with its columns) and in the new-best `top:` breakdown. The index was 0-based, the loss column `L(cohesive_energy[0])` and the value column `cohesive_energy[0]`, which every `DIGITS` setting cut (at the default `DIGITS 6` to `L(cohesive*` and `cohesive_e*`; a second observable became `L(cohesiv~2` and `cohesive_~2`), and the `top:` breakdown named every term `cohesive_energy`. The labels fit every `DIGITS` for `k` up to 99; past that the tag is shortened and the index kept (`L(Eco*[100]`), the rule `params.tadah` uses (`header_detail::fit_keep_index`), and the journal's loss section names the columns as the files write them (the column geometry is `column_titles.h`, shared by the host and the journal). `kind()`, the warm-start cache key, is unchanged. Scripts that read these columns by name need the new names.
- **With `PC_OBSERVABLE`, an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` resolves to `ANALYTICAL`** at parse time in each block that runs an optimiser and obtains gradients (`obtains_gradients`, `phase_specs.h`: a gradient-based backend, or an MLSL/AUGLAG whose local search is one) — the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser and `EXPLORE` — and the journal's OPTIMISER section records it (`GRAD_STRATEGY : ANALYTICAL (resolved for PC_OBSERVABLE)`; each such block now has a `GRAD_STRATEGY` line). It ran finite differences (`NUMERICAL`), which the same rule rejects when written out. An `INNER` block is not resolved: MLSL and AUGLAG hand their local search the gradient of their own objective, which the enclosing block's `GRAD_STRATEGY` obtains (`nlopt_adapter.h`), and an `INNER` block takes no `GRAD_STRATEGY` (next entry but one). Derivative-free blocks are left alone (ANNEAL and ASA reject any `GRAD_STRATEGY`), an explicit `HYBRID` or `ANALYTICAL` is kept, `NUMERICAL` is still rejected (the message no longer calls `HYBRID` and `ANALYTICAL` equivalent), and without `PC_OBSERVABLE` nothing changes. **Behaviour change:** such a run now needs the settings of the analytical gradient (next entry) or `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`.
- **`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` — written out, or resolved for `PC_OBSERVABLE` — is refused at startup on a setup the analytical gradient does not cover** (`validate_hpo_setup`, through `analytical_gradient_blocker()`, `analytical_gate.h`, which the run-time gate of `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` and `compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer` now uses too): no training `DBFILE`, `FIXINDEX`, `STRESS true`, `NORM true`, `OALGO` other than 4, `LAMBDA` below 0 (`-1` included), a basis other than `BF_Linear` / `Kern_Linear`, or `PC_SRMSE` (`PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false` was refused too until validation followed its metrics; see the entry above). It is also refused with a loss term that has no analytical derivative (`analytical_loss_term_blocker()`): a `PC_OBSERVABLE` line with `--loss MAPE` or `RMSLE` (`loss_has_derivative_wrt_observed()`, `sims_grad.h`, which `loss_grad_wrt_observed` now uses too), or with `--unconverged force-scaled`, whose value at a relaxation that does not converge has none (`Observable::analytical_gradient_gap()`); `HYBRID` fills those with finite differences, so the run-time gate does not apply this check. The rule checks the blocks that run an optimiser — the `STRATEGY`-level block and `EXPLORE` — not an `INNER` block, whose search takes the enclosing block's gradient. The message names the block, the setting or the `PC_OBSERVABLE` term, and `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID` (`OPTIMIZER block: GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL (resolved for PC_OBSERVABLE: the block sets no GRAD_STRATEGY) needs the analytical gradient of the loss, which this setup cannot form: OALGO is 1 (it needs OALGO 4, the Cholesky normal equations). …`, `… cannot form: the PC_OBSERVABLE term cohesive_energy[1] uses --loss MAPE, which has no derivative with respect to the value. …`). Such a run used to pass startup and stop at its first gradient with `no registered analytical derivative … register the missing analytical derivatives in dtheta_registry`. Under `HYBRID` the journal warns once with the reason (`No analytical gradient for this setup: …`) and every parameter uses finite differences, as before. The error of an `ANALYTICAL` gradient that cannot be formed at run time names its possible causes (a parameter without an analytical derivative, a candidate whose fit cannot be differentiated, a loss term without one at that point) and `HYBRID`; it pointed at a `dtheta_registry` that does not exist.
- **`GRAD_STRATEGY` inside an `INNER` block is a startup error that names the line**, like `SEED` there: `Error on line N: GRAD_STRATEGY is not accepted inside an INNER block: the enclosing block's GRAD_STRATEGY applies to its local search; set GRAD_STRATEGY on the enclosing STRATEGY block` (or `EXPLORE` / `REFINE` block, wherever the `INNER` block is). MLSL and AUGLAG hand their `INNER` search the gradient of their own objective, which the enclosing block's `GRAD_STRATEGY` obtains (`nlopt_adapter.h`), so a value written in the `INNER` block was accepted and never used. A configuration that sets one now fails to load; move the line to the enclosing block. No example sets one. `make_nlopt()` refuses an `INNER` spec that carries one (`validate_nlopt_spec`), the unknown-key error of an `INNER` block no longer lists `GRAD_STRATEGY`, and the `PC_OBSERVABLE` `NUMERICAL` message and the startup `ANALYTICAL` message no longer mention an `INNER` value.
- The `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` requirement of a finite `FAILSCORE` with room for the four bands is checked by `validate_hpo_setup`, so every caller of the startup validation applies it (the examples' parse guard included); it was checked by `hpo_run()` alone. The messages are unchanged.
- `examples/common/run.sh` passes an example's own `config.tadah` and `config.val` when the example directory has them, else the shared ones in `common/`, and prints which files it uses (examples 22 and 23 rely on it).
- The README badges and links and the pointers to the umbrella's community files (`CITATION.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, …) point to the `v1-dev` line: GitLab `v1-dev`, `https://tadah.readthedocs.io/v1-dev/`. They pointed to `develop`.
- **`hpo_run()` returns `EXIT_FAILURE` (1) for a run that ends on its own without a usable loss**, and `tadah hpo` exits with 1: it returned 0. A job script that took 0 for success now sees such a run fail (*Fixed* below; *Configuration File*, *Stopping a run*).
- **`HPOLog::record_eval(std::size_t, double, EvalLoss)` replaces `record_eval(std::size_t, double, bool was_nan)`**: the third argument is the kind of the evaluation's loss (`EvalLoss::Usable`, `EvalLoss::Failed`, `EvalLoss::NanClamped`, `EvalLoss::Threw`), which the DIAGNOSTICS count of unusable losses needs. A caller passes `EvalLoss::NanClamped` where it passed `true` for a NaN/Inf loss, `EvalLoss::Threw` for an evaluation that raised an exception, and `EvalLoss::Usable` or `EvalLoss::Failed` where it passed `false`. New: `HPOLog::n_unusable()`, `HPOLog::n_threw()`, `HPOLog::set_rank_scope(rank, world)`, `HPO_Host::has_usable_loss()`.
- **`FAILSCORE` must be a positive finite number**, an error at its line otherwise (`Error on line 9: FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got '0'`). A `FAILSCORE` of 0 or below was accepted and left no failure band `[0.999 FS, FS)`: every finite loss, `FAILSCORE` itself included, counted as usable, so a run whose every evaluation failed ended with status 0, `COMPLETED`, `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss` and a `best_pot.tadah` that held a failed evaluation (`FAILSCORE 0`: `HPO_BEST_LOSS 0`), and without a skip cap the ceiling clipped every positive loss to `FAILSCORE`. Negative losses (negative-weight `--varloss` terms) stay usable under any positive `FAILSCORE`: failures sit above every loss. The message of a malformed value names the rule too (`FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got 'abc'`). No example or test configuration used such a value.
- **`PC_FAILPROBE --failscore` must be a positive finite number** (`Error on line N: PC_FAILPROBE --failscore must be a positive finite number, got '0'`). On the probe line it is a failure score: the probe's `S` reads it when the probe itself fails, and an `S` at or above it counts as a failed probe, so with 0 or below every probe read as failed (band D for every evaluation under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`). On a `PC_LAMMPS` line `--failscore` is the value substituted for the line's variables when its simulation fails, which goes through the loss against the target like a measured value: it may still be any finite number, 0 and negative values included.
- **DM_REAM `EMBF0`** (F(0) = 0 by construction, default ZERO): no change to the search, the evaluated numbers or the written rows is needed — each candidate's DM_REAM recomputes g_m(0) from its own CEMBFUNC/SEMBFUNC (fresh and cached-trainer paths), and every written potential carries the `EMBF0` line. Added: `OPTIM EMBF0` is rejected at startup (and in `validate_hpo_setup`); `hpo_run.log` POTENTIAL SETUP rows `Embedding F(0)` and the raw F(0) of the `-c` potential, and a RESULT line with the raw F(0) of the first and best evaluation (linear models only; diagnostic, never in any output row); a HINT when a DM_REAM model under ZERO is trained without BIAS/ESHIFT; a WARNING when an INIT WARM seed declares a different `EMBF0` than the run.
- **Compact refit (`tadah refit`, REFIT_MODE compact) fits the zero-referenced embedding under `EMBF0 ZERO`**: `BlipFit`/`CurveCost`/`EnergyCost`/VARPRO carry an F-only `zero_ref` flag with the matching Jacobians (∂c = b - b0, ∂μ = -c(db - db0), ∂η = c[(x-μ)db - (0-μ)db0]/η), and the engine's energy/curve targets use F_tab - F_tab(0). FREE is textually unchanged. Compact ZERO results change for embedding bumps covering rho = 0 and knots below 0.
- **One rule for how a run ends** (`drive_run()`, `run_driver.h`, owns
  every ending, from the fresh-run reset of `best_pot.tadah` on): an
  error ends `FAILED -- <what>` (was `STOPPED with error: <what>`), a
  non-standard exception `FAILED -- non-standard exception` (was `STOPPED
  with unknown error`), both with exit status 1; a best potential that
  could not be saved ends `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <first
  recorded cause>`, exit status 1, also when the run then ended on a stop
  criterion, a signal or a stagnation stop that outranked or swallowed the
  error (`detail::rethrow_if_best_unsaved`, `durable_failure_cause`, now in
  `run_driver.h`); a signal ends `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)` with 128 + the
  signal number; a run without a usable loss exits 1. `hpo_run()` has no
  handler set of its own. NLopt's end is classified by
  `classify_nlopt_exception()` alone (by type: a subclass of
  `std::runtime_error` such as `durable_best::Error` is never NLopt's); it
  replaces `nlopt_self_terminated()`. A fresh-run reset of
  `best_pot.tadah` that fails (its lock cannot be taken, say) ends the run
  `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <cause>` too, with the RESULT
  section and its retries in DIAGNOSTICS: it was a startup error, and the
  journal ended with an `ERROR startup:` line and no trailer. Tests:
  `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`drive_run fails a run whose best potential
  could not be saved, however it ends`), `[durable_e2e]` (`durable best: a
  failed fresh-run reset fails the run`).
- RESTART with an NLopt inner optimiser now restarts on stagnation (the
  `StagnationStop` ended as a graceful "nlopt failure"). MAXEVAL applies
  per launch, so a RESTART run can use up to (MAX + 1) x MAXEVAL
  evaluations.
- SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGUSR2 during NLopt, a MULTISTART start, PT/PA
  calibration or NOISE_CALIBRATION ends as `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)` with exit
  status 128 + the signal number (*Fixed*, the signal entry). In a
  multi-rank `TADAH_HPO_MPI` PT/PA run the ranks other than 0 still ended
  `FAILED -- ...: rank 0 aborted during ... calibration`, exit status 1
  (fixed since: every rank ends `SHUTDOWN`, see Unreleased).
- `NLOPT_ROUNDOFF_LIMITED` is a graceful stop (was FAILED); an error inside
  an NLopt gradient (e.g. a GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL wiring error) fails
  the run.
- A transient durable-best failure can hold the evaluations whose loss may
  become the new best for up to about 30 s (the others carry on).
- DIAGNOSTICS' transient-I/O note calls the best potential complete only
  when one was persisted. (Its `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable
  loss.` verdict is the count of unusable losses: *Fixed*.)
- `StagnationStop` moved to namespace scope (`tadah/hpo/stagnation_stop.h`;
  `HPO_Host<...>::StagnationStop` is an alias). New
  `host_analytical_grad_fn()` (`gradient_provider.h`).
- Numerics shift at the rounding level: a start point the plain map
  rounded is now evaluated exactly (3e-11 relative for a small weight in a
  wide box), and grids, LSCALE/ESHIFT and LAMMPS read-backs are unrounded,
  so first losses and possibly trajectories differ from 1.5.0 at that
  level; `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` runs are no longer byte-identical to earlier
  releases.
- Examples: `common/lammps/Ecoh.in` and `Ecoh_param.in` pass values to and
  from LAMMPS exactly (see Fixed), which exposes the ~1e-13 relative
  re-evaluation noise of the warm-started lattice relaxation. Examples 05,
  13 and 16 have new reference fingerprints; best losses, loss components
  and pinned hyperparameters are unchanged. In 05 that noise decides
  whether DLIB:BFGS's line search accepts a re-evaluation on the clamped
  bound RCUT2B = 6.0: 30 evaluations on the reference machine, 16 or 17
  after a 1-ulp perturbation (`evals_tol` 14 accepts 16..44; since the
  `elastic.tadahdb` cells were corrected the reference run takes 17, and
  the same tolerance accepts 3..31). 13 sets
  `AFTER_STAGNATION 3` so both restarts still fire (23 -> 20
  evaluations); its restarts draw from `std::random_device`, so only what
  does not depend on them is pinned. In 16 the loss is flat along
  FIXWEIGHT(14), and which tied evaluation is best is no longer pinned.
  `examples/tools/generate_fingerprint.py` keeps hand-written fingerprint
  fields on regeneration.
- **BREAKING — a DM_REAM `-c` configuration must declare `RHOFORM`** (see Tadah.MODELS): `HPOTargets` reads it right after the grids are expanded, before the OPTIM boxes and the WARM seeds that depend on it, and stops without it (`tadah hpo: DM_REAM: RHOFORM is required: ...`). `validate_hpo_setup` validates a bare DM_REAM density for its form (grids, the MONOTONE cutoff list, WATOM > 0 under the positive forms), and the startup probes (`derive_weight_count`, `derive_raw_embedding_f0`, the host's all-weights-fixed probe) rethrow `models::DensityFormError` instead of swallowing it, so an invalid density is a startup error, not a failure of every evaluation. Every potential the run writes carries the key. **Migration:** add `RHOFORM SIGNED` to a seed written before tadah 1.6.0; `SIGNED` runs evaluate bit for bit as before (example 27's seed gained the line; its reference fingerprint is unchanged).
- **Non-negative boxes under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE`.** `OPTIM AMPGRIDMB ... AUTO` with a linear rule clamps lo = max(0, x0 - d) before the LOG check and, without FLOOR/GROUP_FLOOR, takes an implicit GROUP_FLOOR 1e-3 (the refit writer's group floor), so a zero amplitude gets [0, 1e-3 max|x0|]; the new box origin `AutoLinearNonneg` prints as `AUTO FRAC>=0` (the SEARCH SPACE origin column widens when it occurs). `RATIO` needs every seed on the line > 0, a negative seed is an error, and an all-zero line without a floor is an error with a hint. An explicit `AMPGRIDMB` lo < 0, any `WATOM` lo <= 0 and, under `MONOTONE`, any `SGRIDMB` lo <= 0 (explicit or AUTO) are startup errors. The comment header of `hpo_search_space.tsv` gains two lines describing `AUTO FRAC>=0` (every run).
- **`OPTIM` on structure keys is refused** before `context.size(k)`, case-insensitively: `RHOFORM`, `TYPE2B`, `TYPEMB`, `RCTYPE2B`, `RCTYPEMB`, `KDEG2B`, `KDEGMB`, `KDEGEMB` ("OPTIM <KEY> is not a valid search target: ..."); `validate_hpo_setup` repeats the check.
- **WARM and the density form**: a WARM seed whose `RHOFORM` is absent, invalid or different from the run's is an ERROR when `AMPGRIDMB`, `CGRIDMB` or `SGRIDMB` is OPTIM'd and a WARNING otherwise (the seed's density keys are not read); independent of `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`. A `RESTART` refresh applies the rule to its `WARM` seeds as well (its `InitContext` carries the run's configuration), and its fresh points carry their origin (`optim_init_origin`).
- **Compact refit (`tadah refit`, `REFIT_MODE compact`) fits in the declared form** (`CompactConfig::rho_form`, `BlipFit::form`). `NONNEG` fits the bump columns and `MONOTONE` the integrated-bump step columns (dS/dr = -eta B(x)/A, A = 1.5 Blip, 20/11 Blip5, with the matching Jacobians) with every coefficient >= 0: `solve_coeffs` by `models::nnls` with the variable-projection gradient on the passive set, REFINE and FUSE with Ceres lower bounds at 0. The table pre-checks of the dense path run on the compact fit window; an `INIT RHO` anchor with eta <= 0 is an error under `MONOTONE`; the handoff to `EAMRefit` checks the form and every amplitude (>= 0), centre and width. `SIGNED` fitter numerics are pinned to the previous fitter by goldens.
- **OPTIM keys are upper-cased when the line is read** (owner decision 2026-09-29), as configuration keys are case-insensitive: every rule and lookup keyed on them applies in any case -- the `WEIGHTS`, `EMBF0` and structure-key refusals, the `FIXWEIGHT` slot mapping through `FIXINDEX`, `BUMPGATE`, the neighbour-list cutoff (`nn_safe_rcutmax`), the density gates' arming -- and the labels of `params.tadah`, the journal and the sidecars are upper-case (`OPTIM ampgridmb (1) 0 1` + `OPTIM CGRIDMB (2) 3 4` give `AMPGRIDMB[1]`, `CGRIDMB[2]`). A lower-case key was searched as a key of its own and missed every such rule, and `OPTIM cgridmb` + `OPTIM CGRIDMB` wrote `CGRIDMB` twice into the candidate context. Lines of one key that select other indices still add to it; an index an earlier line already selects is an error at the second line, naming the first (a second box replaced the first without a word). Errors about an `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` index name the model weight, not its slot. Tests: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **OPTIM on `DIMER`, `FIXINDEX`, `MODEL` and the first `OALGO` value is refused at its line** with the structure-key message (`HPOTargets::optim_structure_key_error`, `optim_discrete_index_error`): the rigid-dimer layout of the data (its bond length included), which weights are pinned, the model and its integer option, the training algorithm. A searched value was truncated by `Context::get<int>` (`OALGO 2.7` ran GELS), broke `FIXINDEX` or moved the data's bond length. `OPTIM OALGO (2)`, the rcond M of options 1 and 3, is continuous and stays searchable. Tests: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **A radial bump width must be > 0 in every `RHOFORM`** (owner decision 2026-09-29): `SGRID2B` of a `D2_Blip`/`D2_Blip5` pair term and `SGRIDMB` of a DM_REAM `Blip`/`Blip5` density. An OPTIM box whose low bound on such a width is <= 0, explicit or AUTO, is an error at its line, by `models::optim_low_bound_rule(key, ctx)` (the message names what sets the rule: `the D2_Blip pair basis requires every SGRID2B width > 0 ...`); the `-c` potential's widths are checked at startup, naming each key, bump and value (`SGRID2B (2) = -1: ...`); a `tadah refit` `INIT PHI`/`INIT RHO` MANUAL anchor with eta <= 0 is an error naming its channel, place and value (it was fitted as its mirror and written, or failed with "zero entry in SGRIDMB (internal error)"; only MONOTONE refused it). A bump is even in eta, so a negative width evaluated as its mirror while the bump-support checks, which computed the support edge c + w/eta, skipped it. The skips are gone: BUMPGATE's startup checks read the widths as they are, its table and the compact fitter's projection and spill guard throw `std::logic_error` on a width <= 0, and the per-candidate measure counts a width <= 0 (only an optimiser that leaves the box evaluates one) as 1 - eta rcut / w, the continuation of the overhang measure through eta = 0, and a NaN width as the top of band D. The widths > 0 also remove the +inf outer edge a pinned eta = 0 anchor had in FUSE. The NONNEG check that accepted `OPTIM SGRIDMB (2) 0 4` on a Blip5 density now expects the refusal. Tests: `test_bump_width.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_compact_refit_rhoform.cpp`, `test_compact_fitter.cpp`.
- **A `RHOGATE`/`RHOBARGATE` that no OPTIM line arms is refused at its line** (owner decision 2026-09-29), naming the keys that would arm it (`Error on line 12: RHOGATE cannot act: no OPTIM line searches a key that changes the DM_REAM density or its window (AMPGRIDMB, CGRIDMB, SGRIDMB or RCUTMB), ...`); it replaces the "not armed per candidate" INFO line and the reused `-c` verdict. The unarmed paths are gone (`RhoGateTable`/`RhoBarGateTable::armed`, `watom_armed`, `fixed`; the startup error of an unarmed failing `-c` density; `density_gate_scans_for_test`). Tests: `test_rho_gates.cpp`, `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp`.
- **Compact refit: FUSE's `MAXEVAL` bounds the whole stage** (owner decision 2026-09-29): the held re-run passes (the fallback when the linear re-solve after the bump-support projection leaves max |dE|/tol above 1) share the Ceres iterations with the joint solve (`FuseBudget`, `run_held_passes`; `joint_refine` reports the iterations it used). Each pass gets what is left; with nothing left the held re-run is skipped and the re-solve result kept (`FUSE held re-run skipped: MAXEVAL <n> spent` in `refit_opt.log`), and a pass that spends the rest ends the passes. The passes had the full `MAXEVAL` each, up to n_phi + n_rho more solves. Tests: `test_compact_fitter.cpp`.
- **Compact refit under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve`: the FUSE refine of each function runs with FUSE's own `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL`** (`fuse_curve_config`), so curve results change where REFINE's or FUSE's stop keys were set. It ran with REFINE's: REFINE's `MAXEVAL` (an L-BFGS REFINE's evaluation budget, read as Ceres iterations), `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL` set it, also with REFINE `ENABLED false`, whose warning said the REFINE optimiser was ignored, and FUSE's own keys had no effect (FUSE `MAXEVAL 1` and `500` wrote byte-identical potentials). Each of phi, rho and F gets FUSE's whole `MAXEVAL` (in energy mode it bounds the whole stage, above), and each `FUSE[phi|rho|F]` line of `refit_opt.log` logs its Ceres term (`FUSE[phi] curve score 0.0304 -> 0.0287 (NO_CONVERGENCE(501it, maxiter))`). **FUSE reads `GTOL`**, in both modes (`CompactConfig::joint_gtol`, default 1e-14, the energy-mode joint solve's constant before, so default results do not change); it was refused, and before 1.6.0 accepted and ignored. Without these keys a curve run changes only where a FUSE refine reached REFINE's default 1000 iterations (FUSE's default is 2000; the tolerances' defaults are equal). REFINE `ALGO NONE` with a stop criterion is now an error in curve mode too (`REFINE: MAXITER has no effect with ALGO NONE (the stage is skipped); remove MAXITER`), and an L-BFGS REFINE `MAXEVAL` no longer sets Ceres' iteration cap. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp` (the end-to-end harness moves to `compact_refit_harness.h`).
- **`tadah refit` refuses a key of the other `REFIT_MODE`, and compact mode without Ceres** (MLIP's mode rule, `check_refit_mode_keys`, in `RefitEngine::validate()` and `run()`, which `HPORefitEngine` inherits; see Tadah.MLIP): under `REFIT_MODE compact` the dense fit's keys (`REFIT_LEVEL`, `N2B`, `NRHO_BASIS`, `NF_BASIS`, `REFIT_MAX_ESCALATIONS`, `REFIT_PLACEMENT`, `REFIT_CONSTRAIN_F0`) and, under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy`, `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING`, `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` and `REFIT_CURVE_DERIV_WEIGHT` (which `compact_fit` reads under `curve` only), and under `curve` with `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING false` `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` (the weighting's floor), are errors before any fitting; under `REFIT_MODE dense` the compact keys and the `INIT`/`EXPLORE`/`REFINE`/`FUSE` blocks are, so a dense run no longer accepts a stage block that `RefitParser` would refuse. They were ignored. A top-level `OPTIMIZER` block, which neither mode reads, is refused in either mode before the table is read, with the migration text `RefitParser::parse` gives it, now MLIP's `top_level_optimizer_refusal()`, which the parser takes; `validate()` accepted the block, and `run()` refused it in the parser after reading the table (dense mode ignored it). `HPORefitEngine::has_compact_fitter()` is true in a build with Ceres; without Ceres it is false, so `REFIT_MODE compact`, written or the default, is refused before the table is read, where `compact_fit()` returned false and the base fell back to the dense fit with a WARNING (that `compact_fit()` now throws `std::logic_error`). The one fallback left, a reachable pair-distance window that cannot be computed (`r_reach <= 0`; with the crystal gate and `REFIT_REACH_SMIN` >= 0.1 it does not happen), says what it saw (r_reach, `REFIT_REACH_SMIN`, the lattice, a0), that the dense fit reads none of the compact keys and takes its own keys' defaults, and to set `REFIT_MODE dense` to configure it. `refit_curve_objective` is MLIP's `read_refit_curve_objective` (it inserted the blueprint default `energy` into the configuration). The migration text for a top-level `OPTIMIZER` block names the Refitting page (*Stage blocks*) for examples; it named `HPO_REFIT_2B_EAM/REDESIGN/configs`, which is not shipped. After a compact fit the F row of `refit_range_report.dat` says that `refit.eam` carries the original table above the fit window; it described the dense fit's uniform tail basis (MLIP). Its phi and rho rows' `fit_window_lo` is the compact fit's window, the `rwin_lo` that `compact_fit` hands to `EAMRefit::finalize_injected` (Tadah.MODELS: `fit_window_r_lo()`), and so are the curve files' `in_window` flags; it was 0.9 r_reach (MLIP). `tadah refit --edit` and `--retarget` refuse the keys of a refit of an EAM file, `HPORefitEngine` inheriting MLIP's rule. Without Ceres the `compact_fitter.h` header no longer says that the engine falls back to dense mode. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp`, `test_compact_refit_rhoform.cpp` (the compact RHOFORM x `REFIT_BASIS` cases, moved from MLIP's tests, which ran them through the dense fallback), `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` (the migration text; a negative `FTOL_ABS`, refused already, now tested).
- **Compact refit: `REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE`, `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` and `REFIT_COMPACT_INSERT_TOPK` are refused under `REFIT_BASIS Blip` or `Blip5`** (MLIP's mode rule, which `HPORefitEngine` inherits; see Tadah.MLIP), before the table is read: the compact fitter applies them to the knot families only (`merge_close_knots` and the anti-coalescence factor, `opt_every`, `insert_topk`), and they were accepted with no effect. `compact_fit` sets `CompactConfig::merge_guard` for the knot families only, so the `schedule:` line of `refit_opt.log` says `merge_guard=off` for a Blip fit, which never applies the guard (under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy` it said `merge_guard=on`, the value of `auto`); the fits are unchanged. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp`.
- **BREAKING for bounded gradient searches — no finite-difference point is evaluated outside the OPTIM box, with either step rule, CENTRAL or FORWARD, in NUMERICAL and HYBRID** (owner decisions D1-D10, 2026-09-29; `fd_stencil.h`, `src/fd_stencil.cpp`, `gradient_provider.h`). Both step rules of `PARAM fd_kernel` -- `inhouse`, h = fd_step max(|u|, 1), and Ceres' rule, max(2^-26, |u| fd_step), which Tadah now evaluates itself (`fd_plan`, `fd_combine`: Ceres' `DynamicNumericDiffCostFunction` bit for bit inside the box, its points, order and arithmetic; it stays the default of `NUMERICAL` under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`, and the Ceres library is off the finite-difference path, so `fd_kernel ceres` needs no `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES`) -- take the central difference inside the box and, within a step of a bound, the one-sided difference into it, (-3 f(u) + 4 f(u+s) - f(u+2s))/2s, second order (error -h^2 f'''/3 against h^2 f'''/6; noise 2.55 sigma/h against 0.71 sigma/h) at the same cost for `NUMERICAL` (1 + 2n evaluations: it evaluates the point itself first). `diff_method 1` (FORWARD) steps backward at the upper bound. A point outside the box -- an optimiser that ignores the bounds (the CERES drivers, `LN_NEWUOA`) puts one there, or a start it handed on -- is differenced towards it, so no point of its stencil lies further outside (D4). A coordinate proposed outside its box by no more than rounding is evaluated on the bound (D10; `HPO_Host::snap_onto_box`, `SearchSpace::snapped_onto_box`): within `kSnapUlps` = 8 units in the last place of the bound, in real coordinates (of max(|lo|, |hi|) on a linear dimension, of the bound itself on a `LOG` or `ASINH` one) or in the optimiser's, recorded on the bound and counted in DIAGNOSTICS (`Snapped onto the box`) with no WARNING. Under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` NLopt's own arithmetic rounds points on a bound up to 2 ulps of the box's scale past it (`LN_COBYLA`, `LN_PRAXIS`, `LN_BOBYQA`, the unscaled DIRECT variants, `MLSL` and `AUGLAG` with such a local search: a survey of every NLopt algorithm, about 900k evaluations; none in the unit box of `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`), and these points were evaluated outside the box and warned about. The step is never shrunk: a box in which some point has no stencil is refused at startup (`validate_hpo_setup`, every phase that takes finite differences: `takes_fd_gradients`) and by the gradient before it evaluates anything, naming the dimension, a point without a stencil, the step's source (`PARAM`, the default, or the `NOISE` block's `AUTO`: `OptimizerSpec::fd_step_from_noise_auto`), the largest fd_step that fits (`fd_fit`, `fd_largest_fitting_step`, on the steps as computed, and sound; under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO` fd_step <= 1/3 for CENTRAL and 1/2 for FORWARD with the in-house rule, 1/2 and 1 with Ceres' rule, exactly; under `NONE` over the whole box, bounding the computed step exactly for a power-of-two fd_step, else within an ulp of it, but with u +- h taken as exact reals, so that in a box only hundreds or thousands of ulps wide it can refuse a step every point of the box accepts, by up to a few per cent) and `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO` or a `LOG` box as alternatives (D1). `HYBRID` takes the same stencil with its own `fd_kernel` (default `inhouse` under either scale, as before) and `diff_method`, and the loss the optimiser has just evaluated at the point as the centre of a one-sided or forward difference (`GradientProvider::gradient_at`: the NLopt objective, Ceres' `FirstOrderFunction`, and DLIB:BFGS, which asks for a gradient at its start, right after the loss there, and after each line search at the point the search ended on, the last point it evaluated; its line search evaluates the loss alone) -- a gradient asked for without that loss evaluates it, once per gradient, counted in DIAGNOSTICS.
