Startup refusals: a box too narrow for the step; `diff_method 2` (RIDDERS, D3); an invalid `fd_step`, `fd_kernel` or `diff_method`, with the same message on every backend (a number for `fd_kernel` too: `PARAM fd_kernel must be inhouse or ceres (got 1)`); these PARAMs where they never take effect -- an INNER block, `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL`, a derivative-free optimiser (D5; they were accepted and ignored); the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` where the STRATEGY-level phase measures no slopes -- a derivative-free optimiser, ASA (its own probes), `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` written or resolved -- with `the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LN_SBPLX: NOISE AUTO has no effect: NLOPT:LN_SBPLX measures no slopes; remove AUTO from the NOISE block.` (D7; a derivative-free run printed an `ignored` note on stderr), while it now derives `fd_step`, with its journal line, wherever that phase takes finite differences, an `MLSL` or `AUGLAG` whose local search takes gradients included, which it left without; the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` beside a `PARAM fd_step` written in the block, on the phase concerned (D8: `NOISE AUTO has no effect: PARAM fd_step 0.0001 is set; remove one of them.`; the written step was taken, with a note on stderr), and a box too narrow for the derived step advises removing `AUTO` and setting `PARAM fd_step`; ASA settings whose tangent or curvature probes, as ASA computes them, cannot fit a box (`asa_probe_box_error`, also when ASA starts). The bundled ASA's exit curvature evaluates the points it tests (TADAH-PATCH 8, D9): where both x +- Delta |x| lie in the box it evaluated (1 +- Delta) x, which can round an ulp beyond a bound at a point or two of about one box in six, whatever the Delta (`Curvature_0` 0 or -1 only; the default computes no curvature, and the golden gate is unchanged). `DLIB:BFGS` accepts `diff_method`. `HPO_Host` counts every evaluated point outside its real box before the RHOFORM projection, with a WARNING at the first; the `CERES` drivers and `LN_NEWUOA` get one startup WARNING per run (it came at parse time, at startup and at each phase's start) saying that their evaluations can leave the box, are counted and can become the best. The journal: an OPTIMISER line per phase that takes finite differences (`Finite differences : CENTRAL, h = 1e-06 of each box width (default fd_step; in-house rule); next to a bound: one-sided 3-point into the box, same h, same cost`), an INFO line at the first one-sided difference of each dimension, and DIAGNOSTICS counts printed only when not zero (`Snapped onto the box`, `Out-of-box evaluations`, `Out-of-box gradients`, `One-sided differences` per dimension, `HYBRID centre losses`). Runs whose gradients never come within a step of a bound are unchanged bit for bit, with either rule and with HYBRID (they gain the OPTIMISER line), and so are derivative-free, ANALYTICAL, annealing, ASA, GRID and RANDOM runs -- except where a run evaluates an end of a `LOG` or `ASINH` box (a `GRID` over one always does) or, rarely, of a `LIN` box under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`: that end is now exactly the `OPTIM` bound, where it could come out an ulp outside (D6, *Fixed*); and where an NLopt search under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` proposes a point within rounding outside the box, now evaluated on the bound (D10, *Fixed*). A search whose gradient reaches a bound, a NONNEG/MONOTONE run at a zero amplitude, a CERES run whose iterates leave the box, a LOG/ASINH run at a bound (ulp level, *Fixed*), a run with a formerly ignored `diff_method` or `fd_kernel`, a HYBRID run without an analytical derivative (*Fixed*), and an NLopt run under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` that rounded a point past a bound (evaluated on it now, *Fixed*) can take a different path. Examples 01, 05, 09, 23 and 26 (`DLIB:BFGS` to the upper bound RCUT2B = 6) find their best at 6.0, not at 6.000002 outside the box, 09 takes 9 evaluations (8) and 13 takes 18 (20; its stagnation window is no longer reset by the out-of-box point): their fingerprints were regenerated with the first, in-house-only version of this change, and each reports its one-sided difference in DIAGNOSTICS (07's too, with the same best); the rest of the change moves no fingerprint (27/27). Tests: `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (either rule and scheme under AUTO and NONE on LIN and LOG boxes, the NONNEG slope, bit-for-bit guards against Ceres and against goldens of the d5457d0 provider, cost, budget and failures at a bound, HYBRID, the journal), `test_fd_stencil.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_search_space.cpp`, `test_asa.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_scale_validation.cpp`, `test_hpo.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (every gradient backend from the upper bound of the straddle box; LD_SLSQP from 1.8 pinned at 120 evaluations for MAXEVAL 60 -- the NLopt MAXEVAL split, an open issue then, fixed since: 60, see the NLopt `MAXEVAL` entry below -- where it ended after 22 before the one-sided stencil).
- **BREAKING — `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` is refused** in every phase (the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, `MULTISTART`, the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, an `INNER` block), at `ENDOPTIMIZER` for the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser (whether the `STRATEGY` block, a `REFINE` block or the `OPTIMIZER` block itself names it), at `ENDEXPLORE` and at `ENDINNER`, with the phase and the replacements: `Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND is refused: NLopt 2.10 plans each LN_NEWUOA_BOUND step with a nested LD_MMA run that has no time limit and sees no stop request, and once a non-finite value reaches its model each step takes about 10-60 s without evaluating the loss: ...; Ctrl-C, SIGTERM, MAXTIME and RESTART wait that long. Use NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA, Powell's bound-constrained successor of NEWUOA (its STEP rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius), or NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA where the search may leave the OPTIM box` (so until 2026-10-12; the reason now ends at `one radius)`, and `LN_NEWUOA` is refused too: [Unreleased]). It was accepted, and a run stalled once a non-finite value reached its model: the nested MMA subproblem then ran to its caps, about 10^8 model evaluations without an objective call, and nothing HPO does can interrupt it. Above all, every stop HPO makes through the objective -- a signal, a `RESTART` stagnation stop, an error that ends the run -- returns `HUGE_VAL`, NLopt's own convention for a forced stop (HPO keeps it), and from a run's (2n+1)-th evaluation on, the last of NEWUOA's starting points for n dimensions, it cost one such subproblem on any loss (a stop on one of the first 2n returned at once): with `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION 20` each stagnation stop was followed by 20 s without an evaluation, and Ctrl-C waited about 10 s. A flat loss without `XTOL` (300 evaluations in 5 s, then 20 in 590 s; `MAXTIME 20` ended after 31 s) and the approach to a minimum (with `XTOL_REL 1e-12` too) overflowed the model as well. `LN_BOBYQA` and `LN_NEWUOA` return at once after the same stop. The refusal is a table in the caps registry (`backend_refusal()`, `reject_refused_backend()`, `optimizer_caps.h`): the algorithm is not registered, so no list of alternatives offers it (the `MULTISTART` alternatives count one fewer), `optimizer_caps()` throws `LIB:ALGO is refused: <reason>`, `require_driver()`, `require_inner_driver()` and `require_inner_backend()` throw it with the phase (so `run_inner()` refuses a spec built at run time too), `validate_phase_backends()` reports it first, for its own phase and with `validate_run_policy()`'s text, in every `STRATEGY` shape (`reject_refused_backends()`, `strategy_rules.h`: a `MULTISTART` from `INIT` points and a `PRESTAGE` refinement of `TOPK` > 1 included, whose phases need the caps of their backend), and the `ALGO` help prints `REFUSED`. A study moved to `LN_BOBYQA` takes another trajectory: `STEP` rescales each dimension instead of giving one radius, BOBYQA moves a start closer to a bound than its step, and `STEP 0.5` is capped just below. `LN_NEWUOA`'s startup warning named `LN_BOBYQA` alone as keeping to the box (`LN_NEWUOA` is refused since 2026-10-12, [Unreleased]). The analysis and an NLopt-level repro, to re-run before an NLopt upgrade lifts the refusal: `docs/issues/nlopt_newuoa_bound_stall.md`.
- `BUMPGATE` rejects go through the shared pre-training reject path and the per-gate counters (`GateKind`, `HPOLog::record_gate_reject`; `record_bump_gate_reject` kept as a wrapper); its line, counter, numbering and loss are byte-identical.
- **BREAKING for NLopt gradient searches — NLopt's `MAXEVAL` counts every evaluation of its search**, as `DLIB:BFGS` and the CERES drivers count: each loss, each finite-difference point and each analytical gradient (`ANALYTICAL`, `HYBRID`; one evaluation each, owner decision 2026-10-07), in one budget (`nlopt_adapter.h`, `run_nlopt_impl`: the objective counts its loss in the `GradientProvider`'s counter, refuses a call once the budget is spent without evaluating, and stops the search). An NLopt search makes at most its `MAXEVAL` evaluations, and a budget that ends a search ends it `MAXEVAL_REACHED` (a run can make more, as before: each `RESTART` launch has its own `MAXEVAL`, each `MULTISTART` start its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL` (ceil division), the `PRESTAGE` exploration its `EXPLORE` block's and each refinement its share, and the `NOISE` block's `CALIBRATION` repeats and `ANNEAL_PT`'s `INIT_JITTER AUTO` calibration are not charged to any search); the journal and stdout say `NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted after 60 of MAXEVAL 60 evaluation(s)` for every `MAXEVAL` ending (a derivative-free search that NLopt's own count ends wrote `NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted  fmin=<best>`). NLopt counted its objective calls, each a loss and a whole gradient, while the provider kept a budget of its own for the gradients' evaluations: once that was spent every gradient was refused (`GradientFailure::Budget`) and the objective returned `+inf` for points it had evaluated (a run's best could be a loss NLopt had been told was `+inf`). On Rosenbrock's valley in 2-D at `MAXEVAL 60` (`tests/test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`) `LD_LBFGS` made 94 evaluations, `LD_TNEWTON` 99, `LD_SLSQP`, `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ` and `GD_STOGO` 120 and `LD_AUGLAG` 121; `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR2` and `AUGLAG`/`G_MLSL_LDS` with an `INNER` `LD_LBFGS` ended `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged ...)`, `GD_STOGO` `SUCCESS -- generic success`, and `MAXEVAL 1` made 2 evaluations and ended `SUCCESS`. Derivative-free searches that passed NLopt's own `MAXEVAL` stop at it too: `GN_CRS2_LM` (61-64 for 60: it tests `MAXEVAL` only after a trial that does not beat its worst point), `GN_ORIG_DIRECT` (75) and `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L` (71; once per iteration), and `AUGLAG` with any local search (61: it evaluates the point its local search returned once more). NLopt keeps its own `MAXEVAL`, which never ends a search before the budget and is what ends `GD_STOGO`, which ignores a forced stop (the objective refuses its later calls without evaluating; `GD_STOGO` needs `MAXEVAL`, below). The same `MAXEVAL` gives an NLopt gradient search fewer steps, and it can end at a worse loss: raise `MAXEVAL` to keep the old totals; an NLopt `ANALYTICAL` run gets about half as many losses (`LD_LBFGS` at `MAXEVAL 30`: 15 losses and 15 analytical gradients; 28 and 28 before). An `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL` keeps NLopt's count, the objective calls of each local search, which HPO cannot tell apart (owner decision 2026-10-07): the journal's `Inner stop criteria` say so for a gradient-based `INNER` search (`hpo_log.cpp`). The masked-diagnostic warning gives the evaluations made (`... was set after N evaluation(s)`), not NLopt's count of calls. Example 12's `GN_CRS2_LM` exploration makes 60 evaluations (64; its 61st had found the old best), and its fingerprint is regenerated (3 identical runs): best 6.485829e-02 (6.399745e-02), RCUT2B 4.339349 (4.311226), 90 evaluations (94). Tests: `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`; `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`LD_MMA` 120 and `LD_LBFGS` 101 evaluations → 60, `LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 120 → 60, `GD_STOGO` 120 and `G_MLSL_LDS` 104 → 60, each `MAXEVAL_REACHED`).
- **A finite-difference gradient takes the loss its caller has just evaluated at its point as its centre** (`GradientProvider::gradient_at`, `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` as `HYBRID` already did): the NLopt objective, Ceres' `FirstOrderFunction` and `DLIB:BFGS` (when the gradient's point is the last one its line search evaluated) evaluate the loss and then ask for the gradient there, and `NUMERICAL` evaluated the point a second time first — one evaluation per gradient, counted against `MAXEVAL` (when every call takes a gradient, a quarter of the evaluations of a 1-D search, a sixth in 2-D). A gradient now costs 2n evaluations (CENTRAL, and one-sided within a step of a bound) or n (FORWARD) after the loss; `gradient(x, g)`, without the caller's loss, still evaluates x first. Central differences are bit for bit the same; one-sided and forward ones use the loss the optimiser was given (under `--hotvar` a second evaluation of the same point can differ). A `DLIB:BFGS`, CERES or NLopt gradient search does more with the same `MAXEVAL`, and its path can change. Example fingerprints regenerated (3 identical runs each), old → new: 04 best 7.591893e-02 → 7.560174e-02, 06 9.899923e-02 → 8.496187e-02, 07 7.257811e-02 → 7.041855e-02; evaluations 13 19 → 16, 23 16 → 12, 01 and 26 16 → 12, 09 8 → 30 (its run now re-evaluates the clamped bound RCUT2B = 6.0 until `MAXEVAL`, the path example 05 takes; a scratch build of the same code ended after 6). Example 22's README compares its 4 evaluations with 01's 12 or more (16). The evaluation tolerance of 01, 05 and 26 is 18 (14): it accepted both clamped-bound paths, 16 and 30, and is raised only to keep both, now 12 and 30. Tests: `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp` (`gradient_at` evaluates 2n points and never x; NLopt and Ceres evaluate no point twice in a row); `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (the straddle cases make 3 evaluations, 1.6, 1.8 and 1.4, instead of 4; `DLIB:BFGS` 13 → 10, `LD_SLSQP` 13 → 10, CERES 12 → 9, `AUGLAG` 14 → 11, and `LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 ends at 1.798850979; the last four as they were then, changed since by the entry *A failed evaluation fails a gradient* under *Fixed*: those `LD_SLSQP` and CERES searches and `AUGLAG` with its `INNER` `LD_SLSQP` go on to `MAXEVAL`, 60 (with an `INNER` `MAXEVAL 4` `AUGLAG` ends after 9), and `LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 ends at 1.8678643276); `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp` (`[rhoform_projection_e2e]`: the seed, then its stencil).
- **`GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND` need `MAXEVAL`** (`OptimizerCaps::ignores_forced_stop`, `require_stop`; in its own block or, as an `INNER` search, in the enclosing one): NLopt's StoGO tests only NLopt's evaluation count and clock, never the forced stop by which HPO ends a search (on a signal, an error, the `RESTART` stagnation stop, a spent budget, or a loss at or below `STOPVAL` that NLopt was not given). Once HPO has stopped such a search, StoGO calls on and the objective refuses each call without evaluating it, a few microseconds each, until NLopt's own `MAXEVAL` ends it. Without `MAXEVAL`, once a host exception, a signal or the stagnation stop had stopped it, a run with `STOPVAL` or an active `RESTART` as its only stop never ended (measured with a host exception), and one with `MAXTIME` waited out the rest of `MAXTIME` at 100% CPU before it reported; the withheld-`STOPVAL` rule of this release (*Fixed*) stops StoGO the same way (`MAXTIME 4`, `STOPVAL 2`, a withheld loss at `STOPVAL` after 5 evaluations: 4 s with no evaluation). `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or an active `RESTART` was accepted as its stop and is now a startup error, `NLOPT:GD_STOGO needs a stop: NLopt's StoGO has no convergence test of its own and ignores NLopt's forced stop, ...: it then calls on, each call refused, and only NLopt's own evaluation count ends it promptly (MAXTIME only once the rest of its time has passed); set MAXEVAL (it honours no tolerance)`; the `ALGO` help says `MAXEVAL is required (MAXTIME, STOPVAL and an active RESTART are not enough)`. Tests: `test_phase_specs.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp` (GD_STOGO with `MAXTIME` and `STOPVAL` is refused before it evaluates; a withheld loss at `STOPVAL` ends a `GD_STOGO` search through NLopt's own `MAXEVAL`, in well under its `MAXTIME`).

### Removed
- The journal's `PRESTAGE.EXPLORE.INNER init strategy:` line (`(default -- CONFIG only)` for every INNER block), and the INIT resolution and starting points of the PRESTAGE INNER spec: an INNER block takes no INIT block (the parser refuses one), NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG choose the INNER search's starting points, and the copy of the OUTER's points that `share_init_with_nested_phases()` gave the INNER spec was read only by a duplicate start-point check and the density gates' seed scan, which deduplicates. No result changes. Test: `test_init_strategies.cpp` (`PRESTAGE supports a nested INNER sub-algo (MLSL pattern)`).
- `STRATEGY TYPE BASINS` and `TYPE BASINSV2`. Both shipped in 1.3.0-beta.1 and 1.5.0 as experimental strategies that the user documentation never described (the examples README called them experimental). A configuration that uses them now fails: `TYPE` must be `PLAIN`, `MULTISTART` or `PRESTAGE`; their STRATEGY knobs (`BASIN_POOL_MAX`, `BASIN_DEDUP_DIST`, `STOP_NO_IMPROVE_ROUNDS`, `SEED_TOP_N`, `CLAMP_TO_NOISE_FLOOR`, `CHECKPOINT_EVERY` and the BASINSV2 scheduler knobs) and the per-stage `FTOL_LADDER` / `BUDGET_MIN_EVALS` / `BUDGET_MAX_EVALS` / `BUDGET_RATE_FACTOR` keys are unknown keys; and `EXPLORE` / `REFINE` are only valid with `TYPE PRESTAGE`.
- The `checkpoint.tadah` resume. The resume of long runs advertised in 1.3.0-beta.1, and the checkpoint resume of `best_pot.tadah` described in the 1.5.0 documentation, only ever worked through BASINS / BASINSV2, the only strategies that wrote or read checkpoints; it is withdrawn with them. The stagnation `RESTART` is not affected. HPO runs are not resumable: every run starts fresh and removes the previous run's `best_pot.tadah`, which is replaced atomically on each new best and so holds the incumbent at all times. The CORE `Checkpoint` library is kept.
- The modules that existed only for BASINS: the basin pools, the progress tracker and the tolerance ladder, `OptimizerCaps::ladder_ftol` / `apply_ladder_ftol`, `SearchSpace::fingerprint`, and `scripts/parse_hpo_log.py`.
- The "removed from the grammar" hints for pre-release keywords that only led to BASINS keys (`GLOBAL_LIB`, `GLOBAL_ALGO`, `LOCAL_LIB`, `LOCAL_ALGO`, `TOL_GLO_LADDER`, `TOL_LOC_LADDER`, `STAGE_RATE_FACTOR`, `STAGE_MIN_EVALS`, `STAGE_MAX_EVALS`, `EMERGENCY_NO_IMPROVE_ROUNDS`, `GLOBAL_SEED_TOP_N`, `CLAMP_LADDER_TO_NOISE_FLOOR`). No tagged release accepted them; they are reported as unknown keys. `LOCAL` / `ENDLOCAL` (v1.0.0, v1.1.1) still point at `INNER`, without the BASINS sentence.
- The BASINS and resume tests (85 cases), including the optional-fixture test `BASINS resume is idempotent` and its `TADAH_HPO_RESUME_*` variables (it also read `TADAH_BINARY`, which now names the `tadah` executable for the `[tadah_cli]` cases, *Added*). New parser contracts cover the rejections, and no parser error names BASINS. The fresh-run reset of the best comparator, which the resume tests also asserted, has its own host end-to-end case.
- `run_reference_suite.sh --no-clean`: its only effect was keeping `checkpoint.tadah`.
- The legacy MPI build (`TADAH_BUILD_MPI`): `hpo_worker.{h,cpp}`, the host's MPI evaluation path (`run_lammps_mpi`, the `TrainerHost` cache), the MPI branch of `HPOEngine`, the ScaLAPACK link and the `tadah_mpi.hpo` library name. `TADAH_HPO_MPI` is unchanged and is now the only MPI build; cmake-tools rejects `-DTADAH_BUILD_MPI=ON` at configure time.
- `PC_LAMMPS --ncpu` (also on `PC_FAILPROBE`). A line that contains it is rejected at startup, with or without a value: `Error on line N: --ncpu was removed in 1.6.0 (legacy MPI build); parallelism over PC_LAMMPS scripts is THREADS / PC_ISOLATION. Delete --ncpu from this line.` Only the legacy MPI build read it; elsewhere it logged a warning and reordered the `PC_LAMMPS` lines. `LammpsSim::ncpu`, `LammpsSim::greater`, the reordering of `HPOTargets::LS` and the `ncpu=` field of the potential header's `Sim[k]` lines are gone.
- `include/tadah/hpo/lammps_scripts.h` (`LammpsScripts`, the embedded LAMMPS scripts replaced by `PC_LAMMPS` custom scripts). Nothing had included it since then, it needed a `cmrc/cmrc.hpp` that no build provides, and it was still installed with the headers.

### Fixed
- **The journal's OPTIMISER section describes the starting points and the RESTART block as configured.** The run policy prints the RESTART block like an INIT block, under `RESTART init strategy:`: its `METHOD` chain, or `(default -- CONFIG only)` without one (a restart then starts from the CONFIG point), and its `K_TOTAL`, `SEED` and `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`, which appeared nowhere (`RESTART ... K_TOTAL 2 / SEED 77 / WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` printed only `[1] LHS 3`, and nothing without `METHOD`). An INIT, EXPLORE or RESTART block's `K_TOTAL` and `SEED` print under those names; they read `INIT_K` and `INIT_SEED`, the retired keys the parser refuses. `Starting point:` counts the INIT chain's points by origin (`Starting point: 3 points from the INIT chain (3 LHS)`; it read `user-supplied (3 seeds)` for sampled points), and reads `none (the INIT chain produced no point; the optimiser starts from its own default)` when the chain produced none (it read `random (uniform within bounds)`, which the ANNEAL family, starting at the box centre, does not do). An EXPLORE block without an INIT chain of its own reads `(the OUTER INIT points, shared)`, where it read `(default -- CONFIG only)` though the exploration starts from the OUTER's points. Tests: `test_init_strategies.cpp`.
- **A RESTART block's `WARM_ON_MISMATCH` governs the WARM seeds of its `METHOD` chain** (`build_init_strategy` binds a block's policy to the WARM entries of its chain), at startup, where the chain's seeds are resolved, and at each restart: `error` makes a seed that misses an OPTIM key or lies outside the box a startup error, and `skip` drops it, as in an INIT block. A WARM entry took the policy of the INIT block of the spec it is drawn with, which for the RESTART chain is the OUTER spec: the RESTART block's was accepted and ignored (`RESTART ... METHOD WARM seed.pot / WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` with a seed outside the box ran with the seed clamped and a warning, while the journal's run policy printed `WARM_ON_MISMATCH : error` under it), and an INIT block's `WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` stopped the run on the RESTART chain's seed. A RESTART block without the key takes `warn`, the default, whatever the INIT block says. A restart whose chain produces no point (its WARM seed dropped under `skip`, `RANDOM 0`) warns `RESTART r/MAX: the METHOD chain (WARM) produced no starting point; the round starts from the previous round's starting points`, which it did without a word. Tests: `test_init_strategies.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **ASA's stop line names the stop that ended the run.** An exception that leaves an evaluation read `ASA stopped: exception raised during evaluation`, whatever it was; the line now names it: `shutdown requested (SIGINT)` (the signal, when one was received), `RESTART stagnation stop (no new best within AFTER_STAGNATION evaluations)`, or `an evaluation raised an exception (<message>)`. The count `after N host eval(s)` includes the evaluation that raised the stagnation stop or an error, which completed and is in `loss.tadah`: it was one short (16 rows of a run's `loss.tadah` read `after 15 host eval(s)`). A `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or signal stop that falls in the reanneal tangents read `(asa exit: INVALID_COST_FUNCTION_DERIV)`, in the summary (`exit=`) too, the code of a failed evaluation during the tangents, for which the documentation advises `Reanneal_Parameters 0`; it reads `IMMEDIATE_EXIT requested during the reanneal tangents (asa_out: INVALID_COST_FUNCTION_DERIV)`: the pending stop's invalid states are a failed derivative to ASA, so asa() returns that code (`asa_out` keeps it). A host failure in the tangents keeps its text. Statuses are unchanged. New: `ExceptionStash::peek()`. Tests: `test_asa.cpp` (the case `ASA host failure during reanneal tangents aborts gracefully` sets `Acceptance_Frequency_Modulus 10`: with the default its run ended at `P_TEMP_TOO_SMALL` and never computed the tangents), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (CTest `hpo_shutdown_asa`, `hpo_shutdown_asa_block`).
- **The journal's new-best breakdown names an indexed `PC_ERMSE` or `PC_FRMSE` per dataset** (example `23_per_dataset_targets`: `top: ERMSE[2]=1.78e-03(56.3%)  ERMSE[1]=1.38e-03(43.7%)`): `ERMSE[i]` / `FRMSE[i]`, 1-based as in `PC_ERMSE (i)` and the `L(ERMSE)[i]` column (a dataset the metric does not measure has none). Each is the dataset's part of the metric's loss, `L(ERMSE)[i]` / datasets (the parts add up to `L(ERMSE)[avg]`), shown like every part of the line as its contribution to the run's `loss=`, the mean over the loss terms: `L(ERMSE)[i]` / datasets / loss terms. So the parts of all the terms add up to `loss=` (the line lists the five largest), and each is its share of `L(ERMSE)[avg]` only when the indexed metric is the one loss term, as in example 23. It labelled the average of the dataset losses `ERMSE_global` / `FRMSE_global`, the name of the global form, the loss of the average metric, which keeps it. Losses and result files are unchanged. Test: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`The new-best breakdown names indexed PC_ERMSE and PC_FRMSE terms per dataset`).
- **`NLOPT:NLOPT_LD_LBFGS_NOCEDAL` is refused at startup**, in every phase, naming `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS` (the refusal table of the caps registry, as `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`). NLopt 2.10 names it but does not implement it: `nlopt_optimize()` returns `NLOPT_INVALID_ARGS` for it, so every run of it ended `FAILED -- nlopt invalid argument` once its setup was done. The registry-wide end-status test found it; `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp` runs NLopt on it. Docs: Tadah.MLIP's *Configuration File* (the `ALGO` row, the `NLOPT` row of *Algorithms by library*, `MULTISTART`) and *Optimizer*.
- **`DLIB:GFS` takes a `MAXTIME` of any length.** dlib counts its run time in nanoseconds, which hold about 292 years, and the run time a longer `MAXTIME` left (the parser takes any positive number of seconds) did not convert (a negative count on x86-64): dlib's deadline had passed when it started, so the search ended after its `INIT` points with calls left, and the journal said `DLIB:GFS stopped: MAXEVAL reached after 1 evaluation(s)`. dlib now gets at most its own no-limit, `FOREVER` (200 years). Test: `test_backend_end_status.cpp`.
- **The CERES drivers refuse at startup the settings Ceres cannot take**, naming the value: `FTOL_REL` or `XTOL_REL` below 0, `PARAM max_iter` not an integer from 0 to 2147483647, `max_ls_iter` and `max_lbfgs_rank` not one from 1, `nlcg_type` other than 0, 1 or 2 (`CERES:LBFGS requires PARAM max_ls_iter to be an integer from 1 to 2147483647 (Ceres' max_num_line_search_step_size_iterations), got 0`). Ceres refused the negative values and `max_ls_iter 0` when the search started, before any evaluation, with the `FAILURE` `Invalid configuration. ...`, which the run took for the search's ending (the trailer `COMPLETED` when another phase had a usable loss, the no-usable-loss failure otherwise); a fraction was cut to an int (`max_ls_iter 2.5` ran as 2, `nlcg_type 1.5` as 1) and a value past an int converted to an undefined one. Options Ceres refuses all the same are an internal error before any evaluation (`std::logic_error`; `Options::IsValid` before the solve). Docs: Tadah.MLIP's *Configuration File* (the CERES `PARAM` keys). Tests: `test_hpo.cpp` (`CERES validators refuse the tolerances and integer PARAMs Ceres cannot take`), `test_backend_end_status.cpp`.
- **The `ALGO` help of `DLIB:BFGS` says that `PARAM tol_gradinf` tests the L2 norm of the gradient** (dlib's `gradient_norm_stop_strategy`, `length(g)`); it said "gradient-inf-norm". `FTOL_ABS` is the change of the loss from one iteration to the next.
- **`tadah hpo` with a non-linear model** (M_KRR with `Kern_RBF`, `Kern_Polynomial`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ` or `Kern_Sigmoid`, M_BLR with `BF_Polynomial2`): every evaluation's training was refused by MLIP's design-matrix check, so every evaluation got FAILSCORE and no best potential was written; with that check alone fixed, the cached trainer refitted a growing, corrupted basis from the second evaluation on and its random basis made the loss at a fixed point random (MODELS and MLIP 1.6.0). Each evaluation now trains on the same basis draw for the same descriptors, and the potential written is the model evaluated. Test: Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo trains a non-linear KRR model in every evaluation`, `test_pair_tadah_nonlinear.cpp` (`pair_style tadah` evaluates each trained non-linear model as the model predicts, with `BIAS false`, and with `BIAS true` on one species in two LAMMPS instances one after the other, as `PC_LAMMPS` evaluations create them: Tadah.LAMMPS 1.6.0 sets the bias component only for the atoms LAMMPS has). A kernel reads `MPARAMS` only when it is built, and the cached trainer of a worker kept its first one: with `OPTIM MPARAMS` every evaluation after a worker's first fitted with that `MPARAMS`, while `best_pot.tadah` recorded the candidate's value (fixed since: every evaluation builds its own model, see Unreleased). With `BIAS true` on several species, a trained non-linear potential has one intercept component per species, as the descriptor of `pair_style tadah` (see the BIAS entry), and `pair_style tadah` loads it.
- **`FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT` with a non-linear M_KRR kernel are checked at startup.** The weight count of the `-c` model (`derive_weight_count`, `probe_model`) was its design-matrix width before training, 0 for a kernel without a basis, so `FIXINDEX` was not range-checked (an index past `SBASIS` failed every evaluation) and a run without a training `DBFILE` was accepted. It is now `SBASIS` (`mlip::model_weight_count`; `SBASIS` below 2 is refused at startup), a run without a training `DBFILE` is refused (the basis vectors, which the weights belong to, are drawn from the training data), and when `FIXINDEX` pins every weight such a model is still trained each evaluation, not provisioned from `FIXWEIGHT`. Tests: `test_startup_validation.cpp`, `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`fixweight all-fixed with a non-linear M_KRR kernel: every evaluation trains the model`).
- **A LAMMPS error while HPO reads a `PC_LAMMPS` variable back no longer ends the run.** Reading a variable declared with `--varloss`, `--outvar`, `--hotvar` or `--failvar` evaluates its formula in LAMMPS after the script, and that can raise a LAMMPS error: a thermo keyword the script's `thermo_style` does not compute (`variable Pend equal press` under `thermo_style custom step pe`), a divide by zero for one candidate. For `--varloss` and `--outvar` the error escaped the task. Under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` (the only mode of the MPI build) `tadah hpo` then died with SIGSEGV at the next write of the per-evaluation files: the evaluation's catch submitted its partial result, and the row writer read its losses out of bounds. Under `PROCESS` the worker failed the whole script -- a diagnostic `--outvar` took the script's `--varloss` variables with it -- without reading its failvars, kept the errored LAMMPS instance, and journalled `pc-worker task failed (...)` without naming the variable. A `--hotvar` or `--failvar` whose read raised an error was taken as absent, in both modes: no range check and no warm start for the hot value, whose cold scripts ran without it (or on an earlier evaluation's), and a failvar counted as no violation. Now, alike in both modes, each variable is read on its own: an unreadable `--varloss`, `--hotvar` or `--failvar` fails its simulation as a failed run does (its variables take `--failscore`, the failvars still grade band B under `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, a hot script's cold scripts do not run), journalled as `PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): reading --varloss E after the script: <LAMMPS message>`; so does the `S` of the `PC_FAILPROBE` line, which grades every failure (`reading PC_FAILPROBE --outvar S after the script: ...`; the evaluation is aborted and, under `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, graded band D, as when the probe's script fails); a variable declared only with `--outvar` (a diagnostic) that cannot be read is `NaN` in `outvar.tadah` and the evaluation keeps its loss, journalled as `PC_LAMMPS --outvar is NaN (<script>, occurrence n): reading --outvar Pend after the script: <LAMMPS message>`. A variable that reads one that cannot be read (`variable Pdiff equal v_Pend-1.0`) raises that one's LAMMPS message: `LammpsRunner::get_variable` first clears the in-progress marks of LAMMPS's variables (`Variable::clear_in_progress`, as `clear` does), which a LAMMPS error leaves set and LAMMPS reports as `Variable Pend: has a circular dependency` at the next read that reaches them. Both lines are rate-limited per run (occurrences 1, 2, 4, ...). The LAMMPS instance that raised the error is discarded and created afresh for the next task, as after an error inside the script. `PcTaskResult::outvar_errors` carries the outvars' read-back errors to the parent, which knows the roles; the worker protocol is version 3. Tests: `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp` and `test_lammps_variable_readback.cpp` (`[readback]`), `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (new hidden group `pc_readback_e2e`, CTest `hpo_pc_readback_e2e`, and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback`, which runs the built `tadah` under both isolation modes, `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION` left out of its environment, with and without `FAILSCORE_GRADING`).
- **The LAMMPS message of an error inside a `PC_LAMMPS` script is journalled**, in both isolation modes and rate-limited as above: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (bad.in, occurrence 1): ERROR: Unknown command: bogus_command 1 2 (...)`. Such an evaluation failed with nothing in the journal: `LammpsRunner::run_script` dropped the message (it now hands it to a new optional `error` out-parameter of both `ScriptFile` overloads, with the potential's construction error for the `Context` overload). A timeout still has no message. The line read `pc-worker task failed (...)` and came only from the failures that carried a message (a potential that could not be built, an exception in a worker process); it is now `PC_LAMMPS task failed (...)`, as it is not specific to the worker processes (`THREAD` mode writes it too). An exception inside a task that is not a LAMMPS error (no such case is known; `execute_script_task` no longer throws) fails that task with its message and discards its instance in both modes; under `THREAD` it ended the whole evaluation, counted as a NaN clamp.
- **A hot value LAMMPS refuses to inject before a `PC_LAMMPS` script fails that script**, alike under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` and `PROCESS`, journalled with the LAMMPS message on the rate-limited `PC_LAMMPS task failed` line: ``PC_LAMMPS task failed (cold.in, occurrence 1): injecting `variable hot_a0 equal 3.25` before the script: ERROR: Cannot redefine variable hot_a0 with a different style (...)``. HPO injects each hot value as `variable <name> equal <value>` before the scripts that read it (and the warm-start seed before its hot script), and LAMMPS refuses that when the name is already defined with another style -- `--invar hot_a0 3.3` or `-var hot_a0 3.3` on the line makes it an index-style variable when the instance is created; on a cached instance, the script deleted the name and redefined it as a string an evaluation earlier -- or is not a LAMMPS name. The error was swallowed and the script ran on the value it had (here 3.3, in every evaluation, with a feasible loss and nothing in the journal). Now the script does not run, its variables take `--failscore` and the evaluation is aborted as for a failed run; nothing is read back from the instance, whose variables are an earlier evaluation's (under `FAILSCORE_GRADING` the failure is graded by the failed fraction), and the instance is discarded. A cold script is injected the hot values it reads -- `${name}` or `v_name` in its text, `include` files expanded, the test that puts it in the wave after the hot scripts -- and no other, so a value it does not read cannot fail it (`--invar hot_b 7` on a line that reads only `${hot_a0}`); every hot value went to every such script, which went unnoticed while a refused injection was ignored. A cold script that reads a hot value its hot script did not produce (that script could not start, which does not abort the evaluation) is not run and its variables take `--failscore`; it ran without the value, or in a cached instance on an earlier evaluation's. Tests: `[inject]` in `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp`, `[pc_readback_e2e]` (also two hot scripts and a cold one that reads one of them; a hot script whose instance cannot be created) and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **A `PC_LAMMPS` variable read back with no number for its value fails its simulation as one that cannot be read does**, alike in both isolation modes. A variable can be read and still have no number: it is not defined after the script (deleted, or defined in a branch the script did not take), has no single value, or is text (redefined as a string). A `--varloss` variable, the `PC_FAILPROBE` `S`, a `--hotvar` and a `--failvar` (one that is inf or nan too, whatever `FAILSCORE_GRADING`) now fail the simulation -- its variables take `--failscore`, the evaluation is aborted and scores `FAILSCORE` (see the entry on failed simulations below), the failvars grade it under `FAILSCORE_GRADING` -- journalled as `PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): reading --varloss F after the script: no value (the variable is not defined, or has no single value)` (or `its value 'abc' is not a number`, `its value 'inf' is not a finite number`); a diagnostic `--outvar` is `NaN` with a `PC_LAMMPS --outvar is NaN (...)` line, and the evaluation keeps its loss. Before, a `--varloss` value's `FAIL_SCORE` term was averaged with the evaluation's other terms: with two other terms (`PC_ERMSE` and another variable) the evaluation scored about `FAILSCORE/3`, below `0.999*FAILSCORE`, and counted as feasible -- it could become the best. A hot value was skipped (no range check, no warm start; the cold scripts ran without it, or on an earlier evaluation's in a cached instance), a failvar counted as no violation under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` (under `OFF` a failvar's value grades nothing and a value above 0 does not fail the simulation, then and now), and a diagnostic was `NaN` with nothing in the journal. Hot and fail values are parsed as whole numbers, as `--varloss` values already were (a trailing text after a number was ignored: a hot value `'3.25abc'` was 3.25, in its window, and warm-started the next evaluation). Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF` a `PC_FAILPROBE` line's startup warning says what `OFF` changes: `its S neither grades nor gates an evaluation; a probe that fails (its script fails, or its S cannot be read or has no number) fails its evaluation, as any PC_LAMMPS simulation does.` It said `the probe runs as a plain diagnostic sim; no grading, no gate.`, but a probe whose script failed aborted its evaluation under `OFF` too. Tests: `[pc_readback_e2e]` (`--varloss`, `--hotvar`, `--failvar`, the `PC_FAILPROBE` `S` and a diagnostic; text after a number) and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` (with the warning) in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **A `PC_LAMMPS` task that fails before its script runs says why in the journal**, alike in both isolation modes and on the same rate-limited line: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): creating the LAMMPS instance: <LAMMPS message>` for an instance LAMMPS cannot create (an option it refuses), `... rendering the script to a temporary file: <reason>` for a script that cannot be written to its temporary file (`TMPDIR` naming no directory, a full disk). `LammpsRunner::create_instance` dropped LAMMPS' message (it now hands it to a new optional `error` out-parameter), and the parent's `CREATE_FAIL` path returned before the journal line, so such a run failed every evaluation with nothing in the journal. The failure stays an infrastructure one: the script's variables take `--failscore`, the evaluation is not aborted and not graded (it scores `FAILSCORE`, see the entry on failed simulations below), and the creation is retried by the next task. Tests: `[create]` in `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp`, `[pc_readback_e2e]` and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **A single-dash LAMMPS option LAMMPS refuses on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line is an error at its line**, with LAMMPS' own message: `Error on line 9: PC_LAMMPS: LAMMPS cannot start with this line's command-line options: ERROR: Invalid command-line argument: -sff (...)` (`PC_FAILPROBE: ...` on a probe line). The LAMMPS instance that the startup check of the script's variables (`index_defined_variables`) creates with the line's arguments judges them; its failure was dropped -- the check fell back to reading the script as text -- so the line was accepted and every evaluation failed to create its instance, with nothing in the journal. HPO keeps no list of LAMMPS's options. An option this LAMMPS build cannot honour is refused the same way (`-sf omp` without the `OPENMP` package: `ERROR: Using suffix omp without OPENMP package installed`). Tests: `test_lammps_sim_options.cpp`, `test_hpo_targets_order.cpp`; the option-forwarding pins there and in `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` use `-echo none` and `-nocite`, which every build starts with, in place of `-sf omp` and `-pk omp 4 neigh no`.
- **An evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation scores `FAILSCORE` whatever loss its variables are measured with** (`FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`; under `ON` it is graded, as before). A failed simulation -- an error inside the script, a timeout, a variable that cannot be read back or has no number, a hot value LAMMPS refuses to inject or that lands outside its window, a failed `PC_FAILPROBE`, an instance that cannot be created, a script that cannot be rendered -- gives its line's variables their `--failscore`, and the evaluation's loss was the mean of its terms, that line's loss of the `--failscore` value among them. The mean reached `0.999*FAILSCORE` only when the line's loss carried the value past `FAILSCORE`, as `L2` does with the default `--failscore`. Under `L1` or the bounded `TUKEY`, or with a small `--failscore`, the failed evaluation scored below it -- with `PC_ERMSE` and one `L1` `--varloss`, about `FAILSCORE/2`; with a `TUKEY` one, about `(L(ERMSE) + 1)/2` --, counted as feasible and could become the best (`best_pot.tadah`), and a run all of whose evaluations failed ended as a completed one (exit 0, `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss.`). Now `run_lammps` marks such an evaluation (`EvalResult::sims_failed`: aborted, or a task that could not start) and `sims_loss` scores it `FAILSCORE`; the line's terms in `loss.tadah` are still its loss of the `--failscore` value, and `outvar.tadah` has the value. Tests: `[pc_readback_e2e]` (`L1` and `TUKEY`: a `--varloss` with no value, a script error, a script that cannot be rendered, a hot value outside its window) and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` (a script error under `L1`, both modes) in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **An evaluation with a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is a failed evaluation**: it scores `FAILSCORE` under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`, and under `ON` a graded band-B failure measured by the failed fraction of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms (`graded_failscore(FS, B, failed/observables, 1)`; the band table of `pc_fail_grading.h` puts observable failures in band B). A term that cannot be computed -- a relaxation whose lattice parameter leaves its `--hotvar a0` window, a value or a loss that is not finite, an observable that throws -- is `FAILSCORE`, and the evaluation's loss was the mean of it and the usable terms, about `FAILSCORE/n` for one failed term of n >= 2: below `0.999*FAILSCORE`, so the evaluation was usable under `OFF` and `ON` alike (no band was recorded) to everything that classifies a loss -- the journal's census (`[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss.`), the best potential, `STOPVAL`, the annealers' T0 calibration and the gradient provider. Under the default `FAILSCORE` (the largest double) the usable terms vanished in it: the loss was `FAILSCORE/2` all over the region where the term failed, and every finite-difference gradient there was 0. With `PC_ERMSE 0 1` and a cohesive energy whose relaxed lattice parameter leaves its window `[3.0, 3.35]` above `RCUT2B` = 4.94 (`OPTIM RCUT2B (1) 4.0 5.5`, a fixed potential, `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`, `PARAM fd_step 0.05`, from 5.2), on v1-dev: `DLIB:BFGS` ended `FTOL_ABS reached` after 6 evaluations, `LD_LBFGS` `SUCCESS -- generic success` after 3, `LD_SLSQP` roundoff-limited after 3, `CERES:LBFGS` `tol_gradinf reached` after 3, `LD_MMA` spent 45 evaluations there, each with status 0 and `best_pot.tadah` at 8.988e+307; under `FAILSCORE 1e16` the start scored 5.0000000004e+15 and was published as the best, a `TADAH:RANDOM` run inside the region exited 0 with such a best, and `STOPVAL 6e15` was reached at it. (An evaluation with no usable term -- a target whose only term is the failed observable -- or one whose failed terms summed past the largest double, two under the default `FAILSCORE`, scored `FAILSCORE` already.) Now `obs_loss` marks the evaluation (`EvalResult::terms_failed`), and one `assemble_loss` ends every loss assembly (`fit_loss`, `obs_loss`, `sims_loss`): the mean of the terms clipped to `FAILSCORE`, `FAILSCORE` for a failed simulation or term, the graded loss for a classified failure. Such a gradient search stops at its start (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point (the loss at RCUT2B = 5.2 is 1.797693135e+308)`, status 1, no `best_pot.tadah`; under `ON`, where its finite differences are taken, after the start and its two stencil points, `... every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`, since the failed fraction is constant there; under `ANALYTICAL`, the default with `PC_OBSERVABLE`, and `HYBRID` at a graded failure see the next entry), as at any failed point -- under `FAILSCORE 1e16` it used to follow the gradient of the usable terms out of the region, treating its points as usable, graded or not (`FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`: `DLIB:BFGS` 9 evaluations, `FTOL_ABS reached`, the best 7.7476e+04 at the bound 4.0; `LD_LBFGS` 6, `SUCCESS`; `CERES:LBFGS` 36, `XTOL_REL reached` outside the box; status 0 and a `best_pot.tadah` each; now 3 evaluations and status 1) --, the random run ends without a usable loss, and `STOPVAL` is reached by the next usable loss. The term stays `FAILSCORE` in `loss.tadah` and the value `NaN` in `outvar.tadah`; the journal's band-B count reads `B=... (script or observable failed)` (it said `script failed`). A `--varloss` term without a value in `sims_loss` (the branch for a task whose result was never applied, which no run reaches) fails its evaluation too; it was averaged under `OFF`. `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` remain mutually exclusive, so no simulation follows an observable. Tests: `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp`.
- **`refit_opt.log`'s curve-mode note advises the keys the fit's basis family reads** (`HPORefitEngine`): for a costly Ceres `REFINE` at high `NMAX` it said `cap its MAXEVAL / use REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` for every basis, and a Blip family refuses `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY`; it names `REFINE`'s `MAXEVAL` and `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` there now, and `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` for the knot families. Test: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp`.
- **An evaluation with every weight pinned and training data fits under `OALGO 3`** (`HPO_Host`, the all-fixed fast path): an `OALGO 3` fit with every weight pinned gives the potential a zero `WCOV` and the `WNOISE` of the pinned weights' residuals (`M_Train::pinned_covariance`), as `tadah train` writes them for the same configuration, and the fast path, which provisions the weights from `FIXWEIGHT` and computes neither, skipped it: `best_pot.tadah` of an all-pinned `OALGO 3` `M_BLR` run had no `WCOV` or `WNOISE`, and `tadah predict --error` refused it as `weights set from FIXWEIGHT without a fit (tadah hpo with every weight pinned and no training data)`, though the run had training data (a non-linear `M_KRR` kernel, which always fits, had both). The fast path is kept for the other `OALGO`s, which compute no covariance. Test: `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`[fixweight_e2e]`).
- **The journal of a second run in one process is that run's alone** (a task file with two `TASK hpo` blocks, a program that calls `hpo_run` twice). `HPOLog::open` reset only the status, the start tally, the EMBF0, density and occupancy reports, the per-run notes and the finite-difference counters; the census (`Total evaluations`, `NaN/Inf clampings`, `Evaluations that threw`, `Unusable losses` and the verdict, `Graded failures` and the gate and projection counts), the I/O retries, the best and its metrics, the timing and fan-out statistics and the milestones were reset only by `reset_for_test`. A second run that failed 4 of 4 evaluations after a run of 3 journalled `Total evaluations : 7`, `Unusable losses : 4 (57.1%)`, `[!] The majority of evaluations failed`, the first run's evaluations as its fastest and slowest, and in RESULT the first run's best (`Best total loss : 1.0000e+02 (eval 2)`, `Best ERMSE`, `Best potential saved : best_pot.tadah`, which it never wrote); a second run with a worse best had no `BEST` line (`log_new_best` dropped every loss not below the first run's best) and named the first run's evaluation. `open()` now makes the same reset (`reset_run_locked`), and the milestones count from the run's first evaluation (`HPOLog::set_eval_base`, which `hpo_run` gives `HPO_Host`'s evaluation counter), while the evaluation numbers go on from the first run, as before. The hot-variable `rejecting` WARNING is rate-limited by the run's count (`HPOLog::count_in_run`); `warmstart::reject_count`, the process's, is removed: a second run's first reject was, say, the process's 6th, and was not journalled. And `hpo_run` clears every hot-variable seed before `HPOTargets` is built (`warmstart::begin_run`), before the run's `WARM` primes its own: `warm_prime_map`, which a chain's first evaluation falls back to, outlived its run, so a run without `WARM` after one that `WARM`-started from `# HOT` lines started its `--hotvar` values from that seed (`A 42` against its `<initial>` 5). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`[two_runs]`: a second run with no usable loss and one with a worse best against the same run alone, the second run's rejects, a run without `WARM` after a `WARM` run).
- **An evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation or a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is never counted as a NaN/Inf clamping** (`HPO_Host::assemble_loss`, the census's `loss_kind`). The mean of its terms came first, and the failure substitutes in it overflow under the default `FAILSCORE` (the largest double): the `L2` of a failed line's `--failscore` value, or two failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms. That set `was_nan_clamped` before the recorded failure made the evaluation `FAILSCORE`, so with `LOSS L2` and no `FAILSCORE` line every failed simulation -- a crashed script, an unreadable `--varloss` (the entries above) -- and every evaluation with two failed observables was journalled `evaluation produced NaN/Inf -- loss clamped to FAIL_SCORE` with the parameter and bound hints, and counted under `NaN/Inf clampings` (`[!] 100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss that was clamped.`); under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` with a `FAILSCORE` whose square or sum overflows (`1e200`, `1e308`) it was counted both there and under `Graded failures`, two counters the DIAGNOSTICS call disjoint. Now the recorded failure decides: the evaluation is a failure (`FAILSCORE`, or its graded loss), counted under `Unusable losses` and its band, with its own journal line (`PC_LAMMPS task failed (...)`, `PC_OBSERVABLE term failed (...)`) and no NaN warning. (One failed observable, or a finite `FAILSCORE` such as `1e16`, did not overflow and was classified correctly.) Tests: `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (an unreadable `--varloss` at the default `FAILSCORE`, and under `ON` with `FAILSCORE 1e200`), `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` (two failed observables, and under `ON` with `FAILSCORE 1e308`).
- **At a graded failure the optimiser is given no analytical gradient** (`GradientProvider`, under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`). A graded failure's loss is set by its band's measure (`pc_fail_grading.h`: the failed fraction of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms, a skip cap's or a gate's excess), not by the loss terms, and the analytical gradient there is that of the terms that did not fail (the observable chain leaves a failed term out) or of a skip cap's uncapped loss. `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` kept it there, so a gradient search that started where a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term fails -- a band-B failure under `ON` since the entry above -- followed a slope the loss did not have and reported convergence inside the failure region under the default `GRAD_STRATEGY`, which is `ANALYTICAL` with `PC_OBSERVABLE`: on a trained `OALGO 4` model whose observable fails over the whole box (`--hotvar a0 3.29 3.31 3.3`, `FAILSCORE 1e16`), `DLIB:BFGS` ended `FTOL_ABS reached after 4 evaluation(s)`, `LD_LBFGS` made 12 evaluations and ended `nlopt failure -- algorithm converged`, `CERES:LBFGS` left the box and ended `FAILED` (`no analytical gradient for 1 parameter`), and `HYBRID` did the same, nothing being differenced. Now `ANALYTICAL` fails the gradient at the caller's graded loss before its analytical pass, nothing evaluated or counted: `DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point (the loss at RCUT2B = 5.2 is 9.99375e+15: a graded failure, whose grading the analytical gradient does not see; GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID takes finite differences there)` after one evaluation, status 1; `HYBRID` runs no analytical pass there and differences every parameter, so the graded slope applies, or the flat-region rule (`... every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`, after the point and its two stencil points). A candidate a gate rejects before training, a graded failure under `ON`, now gets this cause at the caller's loss (it got the host's reason, `the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`, which `gradient()` without the loss still gives), and its analytical pass no longer counts an evaluation: with the bump-gate fixture of `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`MAXEVAL 20`) `LD_LBFGS` and `LD_TNEWTON_PRECOND_RESTART` end after 15 evaluations right after a rejected candidate (`NLopt ended right after it (nlopt failure)`), where each rejected candidate counted twice and `MAXEVAL` came first. A gradient asked for without the loss at its point (`gradient()`, `DLIB:BFGS` for a point other than its last evaluation) still cannot tell a graded failure. Tests: `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` (a trained model: the default strategy and `HYBRID`, `DLIB:BFGS`, `LD_LBFGS`, `CERES:LBFGS`), `test_gradient_failure.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **A failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is journalled with its observable and why it failed**, as a failed `PC_LAMMPS` task is: a `WARNING` `PC_OBSERVABLE term failed (cohesive_energy[1], eval 2, occurrence 1): the relaxed lattice parameter a0 = 3.3532 is outside its --hotvar window [3, 3.35]` at the first, second, fourth, eighth, ... failed term of the run (`HPOLog::count_in_run`). The reason is the observable's (`ObservableEvalResult::failure`, new: `cohesive_energy`'s relaxed a0 outside its window, or an unconverged relaxation whose `--unconverged force-scaled` value is not finite, `... and its --unconverged force-scaled value, target + slope x fmax = <t> + <s> x <fmax> = <v>, is not finite`: forces that were not, or a product that overflowed; a parsed `--unconverged const` value is always finite), a value or loss that is not finite (`its value is not finite (nan)`, `its loss is not finite (value 1e+300, --loss L2)`), or the message of an evaluation that threw (`its evaluation threw: <what>`). A failed term left only `FAILSCORE` in `loss.tadah` and `NaN` in `outvar.tadah`. Tests: `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` (`a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term is journalled with its observable and why it failed`, `a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term's journal line says why: ...`, `an unconverged cohesive_energy relaxation whose --unconverged value is not finite names its strategy`).
- **With `PC_LAMMPS`, an NLopt MLSL or AUGLAG is judged by its local search**: `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`, `LD_AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG_EQ` with a derivative-free `INNER` block are accepted (`reject_pc_lammps_grad` asks `obtains_gradients`, as the startup rules for `GRAD_STRATEGY` and the finite-difference `PARAM`s do). They were refused for the `D` of their name (`OPTIMIZER's optimiser NLOPT:LD_AUGLAG is gradient-based`), although MLSL and AUGLAG evaluate their own points without a gradient and such an `INNER` block takes none. Without an `INNER` block they run NLopt's default `LD_MMA` and are still refused, now naming it (`... NLOPT:GD_MLSL takes a gradient: NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take one for their local search, and NLopt's default local optimiser, NLOPT:LD_MMA, is gradient-based; an INNER block with a derivative-free one takes none.`); a gradient-based `INNER` block is refused at that block, as before. Tests: `test_pc_observable_parser.cpp` (`[pc_lammps_grad_drop]`), `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_lammps_local_search` (new CTest entry: `LD_AUGLAG` + `LN_COBYLA` and `GD_MLSL_LDS` + `LN_SBPLX` run with `PC_LAMMPS`).
- **Every row of `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah`, `stats.tadah` and their `best_*` twins has its header's columns.** An evaluation stopped before its simulations (`--skip-above`/`--skip-rel`, or a loss already at `FAILSCORE`), rejected before training by a feasibility gate (`BUMPGATE`, `RHOGATE`, `RHOBARGATE`), or ended by an exception wrote only the cells it had: `TOTAL_LOSS/N` fell under an `L(<variable>)` column of `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah` lacked the `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` columns, and a gate reject's metric columns held a computed-looking `0`. A cell the evaluation did not compute is now NaN, in the file's own spelling: `nan` in `loss.tadah` and `stats.tadah` (as for a dataset that is not measured), `NaN` in `outvar.tadah` (as for a value that cannot be read). The rows of a best are complete, so `best_*` files and the example fingerprints do not change. A reader that parses rows with `istream >> double` stops at either spelling; `strtod` and `numpy.loadtxt` read both. Tests: `[pc_readback_e2e]` in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **HPO builds with GCC 15.** ASA's headers include `<omp.h>` inside their `extern "C"` blocks (with `ASA_PARALLEL` and OpenMP), and GCC 15's `omp.h` declares C++ templates, which C linkage forbids: `algorithms_asa.cpp` and `tests/test_asa.cpp` stopped with "template with C linkage". Both now include `<omp.h>` first, at C++ linkage, so the include inside is a no-op; the vendored ASA headers are unchanged.
- **`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy` hands on the relaxed a0 as a0 × the Iso scale factor** (`NativeRelaxer::Result::scale_factor`), the quantity its early-reject gate uses. It read `cell(0,0) / nx`, one ulp off for `--supercell 3 3 3` (30 of the 121 a0 values 2.70 … 3.90 Å moved) and wrong for a cell whose first row is not a0 × nx along x; power-of-two supercells (the default 4 4 4) give the same bits as before, so no example changes. Test: `test_cohesive_energy_observable.cpp`.
- **A training or validation structure with two atoms at the same position stops `tadah hpo` at startup**, naming the structure and the atoms (MLIP `NNFinder`): in the training set every evaluation failed with a LAPACK error naming no structure, and in the validation set every loss was nan (clamped to FAILSCORE), so the whole budget was spent before the run gave up. Test: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`[coincident]`), and `pair_style tadah` in `test_pair_tadah_coincident.cpp`.
- **HPO builds without a system Boost.** `src/init_strategies.cpp` (the SOBOL init strategy) includes `boost/random/sobol.hpp` and `uniform_real_distribution.hpp`, which only a system Boost supplied; they are now in the Boost MLIP vendors, on HPO's include path through `tadah.mlip`. A drift gate, CTest `hpo_vendored_boost_includes` (`tests/check_vendored_boost.py --cxx <compiler>`), checks that every Boost header HPO includes is vendored and, following the includes as the compiler resolves them in the environment the test runs in (`CPATH` included), that every Boost header they pull in comes from the vendored tree; CTest `hpo_vendored_boost_gate_tool` tests the gate on fake trees. A test pins the first SOBOL points (the Sobol sequence, unchanged).
- **`ErRMSE` and `SRMSE` carried wrong units** in the journal (LOSS FUNCTION, the progress line, RESULT), the `best_pot.tadah` and archive headers and the `--skip-above` message: `ErRMSE`, a relative error times 1000, said `meV/atom`, and `SRMSE`, in the dataset's eV/Å³, said `GPa`. They now say `per mille` and `eV/A^3`; the values are unchanged.
- **`PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false` (or no `FORCE` key) stopped the run with a segmentation fault** at the first evaluation, with every optimiser and gradient strategy and in refit polish runs: the validation prediction took FORCE/STRESS from the trained model's context, predicted no forces, and `Analytics::calc_f_rmse` read past an empty vector. `MODEL M_KRR`, whose saved context dropped FORCE/STRESS, crashed even with `FORCE true`, and `PC_SRMSE` compared the reference stresses with zero, a constant RMS(reference) for every candidate (with `--skip-above` it could reject every candidate), with `STRESS false` and, for `MODEL M_KRR`, also with `STRESS true`. Validation now predicts what its metrics measure (see *Changed*). `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` advised `HYBRID` for this setup, which then crashed too.
- **`tadah.hpo` linked NLopt `PRIVATE`** although its public headers `nlopt_adapter.h` and `run_policy_base.h` include `<nlopt.hpp>`: `test_hpo` compiled only when `CPATH` happened to name an installed `nlopt.hpp`. NLopt is now linked `PUBLIC`.
- **Finite-difference points outside the OPTIM box**, with either step rule, CENTRAL or FORWARD: the central stencil evaluated one point outside within a step of a bound (the recorded best of examples 01, 05, 09, 13, 23 and 26 was RCUT2B = 6.000002 in [4, 6]); Ceres' rule, the default under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`, and `fd_kernel ceres` ran inside Ceres, which has no bounds; FORWARD stepped above the box at its upper bound; RIDDERS started 0.32 max(|u|, 1) away. At a `RHOFORM NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` amplitude on its zero bound the projected central difference was half the slope (2.5 for 5 at `AMPGRIDMB (3) = 0` in [0, 0.5]). See *Changed*.
- **Settings that did nothing:** `PARAM diff_method` with the in-house kernel, `fd_kernel` and `diff_method` under `HYBRID` (honoured now), an invalid `fd_kernel` under `HYBRID`, and `fd_step`/`fd_kernel`/`diff_method` in an INNER block, on a derivative-free optimiser or under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` (refused now). `diff_method 2` (RIDDERS) ignored `fd_step` (refused now). The `NOISE` block's `AUTO` on a derivative-free optimiser or ASA, or beside a written `PARAM fd_step` (refused now), and on an `MLSL` or `AUGLAG` whose local search takes gradients (it derives `fd_step` there now). `PARAM fd_kernel 1` on `DLIB:BFGS` was "not supported" by a message that listed it as supported, and the `CERES` list left it out.
- **`HYBRID` returned a zero gradient when no parameter had an analytical derivative** (a setup the analytical gradient does not cover, e.g. `OALGO 1`, a candidate rejected before training, a failed `PC_LAMMPS` chain), although the journal said every parameter used finite differences: `DLIB:BFGS` ended `COMPLETED` at its start after two evaluations (example 01 without `PC_LAMMPS`, `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`). It differences every parameter now. The same `HYBRID` evaluated the loss at the point again for a one-sided difference right after the optimiser had (an evaluation more, a duplicate row, and with `--hotvar` warm starts a value the optimiser had not seen); it takes the optimiser's own now.
- **NLopt points a rounding error outside the OPTIM box under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`** were evaluated there, counted and warned about as points outside the box, and a `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` amplitude a hair below 0 was projected: `LN_COBYLA` pressed against `CGRID2B[2] <= 1.7` asked for 1.7000000000000002 in 29 of 60 evaluations. They are evaluated on the bound (D10; see *Changed*). Tests: `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp` (`[snap_e2e]`: HPO_Host at 1, 8 and 16 ulps past each bound, AUTO and a `NONE` `LOG` box), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`LN_COBYLA` under `NONE`).
- **`LOG` and `ASINH` box ends** came back one ulp outside the box (`SearchSpace::to_real`: `OPTIM X 0.01 10 LOG` at its upper bound recorded X = 10.000000000000002; a linear AUTO box's upper end in about 2% of boxes): the ends of the internal box map to exactly lo and hi, and a point inside it into [lo, hi] (D6).
- The journal said `PC_ISOLATION: PROCESS requested but no PC_LAMMPS sims are configured -- nothing to isolate` when `PROCESS` was only the serial build's default (e.g. example 22, which has no `PC_LAMMPS` line); it now says `PC_ISOLATION: PROCESS (default) -- no PC_LAMMPS sims are configured, nothing to isolate`, and keeps the first line for a `PROCESS` set by `PC_ISOLATION` or `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION`. Test: `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- Five tests whose names contain `;` never ran under CTest: the vendored Catch discovery script split each into two entries, which matched no test case and still passed. They cover the NLopt step plan, `PcFailState` band grading, the validation filter and ESHIFT forwarding, the volume observable on anisotropic cells, and the removed `LADDER_ADAPT_UPDATES` key. All five now run and pass.
- The explicit `hpo_anneal_statistical` and `hpo_corana1987_protocol` CTest entries pass `--warn NoTests`, so a hidden tag that matches no test case fails instead of passing.
- A bare root `LOSS HUBER` / `LOSS TUKEY` used a zero parameter (Huber then scored every potential as 0). `LossFunction::resolve_params` is now the single place for defaults (Huber 50, Tukey 4.685) and validation, shared by root `LOSS`, `PC_LAMMPS --varloss` and `PC_OBSERVABLE --loss`; non-positive, non-numeric and surplus parameters are rejected.
- `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` and `TADAH_PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` are parsed strictly (finite, >= 0); a malformed environment value was silently ignored.
- Error messages: MULTISTART / PRESTAGE-TOPK rejection of single-seed global backends states the real constraint; PRESTAGE without `EXPLORE` names the current grammar (it referenced the removed `PRESTAGE ... ENDPRESTAGE` block); the CERES `STEP` hint no longer names a `PARAM` the adapter rejects; INIT errors say `INIT METHOD`.
- A configuration without any `OPTIM` line is rejected at startup for NLOPT, DLIB and CERES (it previously failed inside the optimiser after the whole setup had run); the TADAH-native optimisers still evaluate the start point once.
- Negative numbers after `PC_LAMMPS` options (`--varloss` targets/weights, loss parameters, `--hotvar` bounds) are values, not options.
- `PC_LAMMPS` lines keep their configuration order, so `outvar`/loss columns and the potential header no longer come out permuted with more than 16 lines. The `--ncpu` sort that permuted them is removed with `--ncpu` (see Removed); `failprobe_idx` is the probe's configuration position.
- **A run stopped by `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` or `SIGUSR2` ends the same way with every backend and strategy, and `hpo_run()` returns 128 + the signal number (130, 143, 140 on Linux).** It returned 0, and `HPOEngine` ignored its result anyway. NLopt reported the signal as `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged ...)`; `MULTISTART` logged it as a failed start and ran the remaining starts, each failing at once, before ending `COMPLETED`; `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` turned a signal during the seed evaluation or a T0 calibration pass into an error (`STOPPED with error: shutdown requested ...`); and a signal during `NOISE_CALIBRATION` escaped `hpo_run()` without writing the queued history rows or the journal trailer. `drive_run()` (`run_driver.h`) now owns every ending of the evaluating part of `hpo_run()`, calibration included: it drains the result queue, writes one trailer (`SHUTDOWN (<signal>)`, `FAILED -- <message>`, `FAILED -- non-standard exception`, `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <error>`, or the status of the optimiser run that ended the run; *Changed*, the ending rule) and rethrows errors after it; `HPOEngine` returns the status and reports a non-standard exception as a failure instead of terminating. `ctest -L shutdown` raises a real signal against `hpo_run()` for DLIB BFGS, DLIB GFS (four threads), NLopt SBPLX, CERES LBFGS, ASA (serial and block-parallel), ANNEAL_PT (two threads), ANNEAL_PA (serial and four threads), MULTISTART, PRESTAGE (during the exploration) and `NOISE_CALIBRATION`, one process each, and checks that `best_pot.tadah` holds the lowest `loss.tadah` row and that `loss.tadah` has one row per evaluation, numbered from 1. TADAH `RANDOM`, `GRID` and serial `ANNEAL` and the NLopt global algorithms have no signal test of their own; `tadah_hpo_exit_status_*` signals the `tadah` binary running TADAH `RANDOM`.
- **`DLIB:GFS` with `THREADS` > 1 no longer aborts the process** (exit 134, core dump, no trailer) when a signal or a `RESTART` stagnation stop reaches its concurrent evaluations. dlib's thread pool rethrew the first exception while the other evaluations were still running on `find_min_global`'s already-destroyed frame, and called `abort()` when a second one had thrown; so `RESTART` never got past its first restart. The adapter now keeps the exception, stops `find_min_global` through its stop condition (later evaluations do not run), lets the running ones finish, and rethrows the exception once it has returned (the NLopt and ASA pattern).
- When several concurrent evaluations throw before the optimiser stops (the `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` worker pool, ASA's block pool, `DLIB:GFS`), the run ends with the exception that matters most: an error, then a signal stop, then a stagnation stop (`ExceptionStash`, `run_stop.h`). The pools kept the first one, so a failure to save `best_pot.tadah` that raced a signal or a stagnation stop was dropped: the run ended as a shutdown (exit 130) or restarted. `HPO_Host<>::StagnationStop` is now one type for every host (`tadah::hpo::StagnationStop`), so the pools can rank it.
- **`RESTART` fires with NLopt backends (including the `INNER` search of MLSL/AUGLAG), with `DLIB:GFS` at `THREADS` > 1 (above), under `MULTISTART`, and under `PRESTAGE` with `TOPK` > 1**; it did not (`PLAIN` runs of most other backends, and a `TOPK 1` `PRESTAGE` refinement, did restart). The NLopt adapter keeps the first exception its objective raises, force-stops NLopt and rethrows it after `optimize()` (the ASA driver's pattern), instead of letting NLopt's C++ wrapper turn it into `nlopt failure`; `MULTISTART` lets the signal stop and `durable_best::Error` through and reports a stagnating start to `RESTART`, instead of treating them as a failed start. **Behaviour change:** a restart re-runs the whole strategy from the fresh points — the `MULTISTART` from its first start, with `min(N_STARTS, K)` starts for the *K* points the RESTART chain draws, or both `PRESTAGE` phases. Each `MULTISTART` start and each `PRESTAGE` refinement seed has a stagnation window of its own; after the last start or seed, a batch in which any of them stagnated restarts the whole strategy, whether or not it improved the global best, as a `PLAIN` run that improved and then stagnated does (the entry "MULTISTART, PRESTAGE and RESTART" under *Fixed*). Test: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`under PRESTAGE, a refinement that improved the global best and then stagnated restarts the PRESTAGE`). A `PRESTAGE` exploration without an `INIT` block of its own now restarts from the fresh points: `RestartPolicy` replaced only the outer spec's points, so every restart replayed the exploration from the original ones and never used the RESTART points (`OptimizerSpec::share_init_with_nested_phases()` is now the one rule for the parser and `RestartPolicy`). Under `PRESTAGE`, a stagnating exploration still hands its top-K to the refinement, which now starts with a stagnation window of its own (it inherited the exploration's, so its first evaluation already crossed the threshold). The stagnation watchdog is disarmed however `RestartPolicy` ends.
- **Each `RESTART` round draws new random numbers.** Restart *r* (1 … `MAX`) runs every phase that has a `SEED` with a seed derived from it: the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser with `SEED + r*S`, where *S* is the number of starts the strategy runs from `SEED` (`OptimizerSpec::seeded_starts()`: `N_STARTS` under `MULTISTART`, `TOPK` under `PRESTAGE`, whose refinement runs candidate *i* with `SEED + i`, and 1 under `PLAIN`), so the starts of round *r* use `SEED + r*S + i` and never another round's seeds (`SEED 7`, `TOPK 3`, `MAX 2`: 7–9, 10–12, 13–15); and an `EXPLORE` optimiser with `SEED + r` (`OptimizerSpec::derive_restart_seeds()`, applied by `RestartPolicy` next to `share_init_with_nested_phases()`). A restart kept every `SEED`, so a seeded stochastic optimiser or exploration (`TADAH:RANDOM`, the annealers, ASA, NLopt's stochastic globals) replayed round 0's random sequence from its new start point and largely found the same points again. Round 0 keeps `SEED`, so a run is unchanged up to its first restart, and so are the examples' fingerprints. The journal's `RESTART r/MAX` line lists the round's seeds.
- **NLopt takes its random seed from the `SEED` of the phase it runs, and from the phase's `INIT`-block `SEED` only when the phase has no `SEED`** (`make_nlopt()`). The `INIT` `SEED` took precedence, so in an NLopt phase that set both keys neither the start seeds `SEED + i` of `MULTISTART` and of the `PRESTAGE` refinement nor the `RESTART` seeds reached NLopt: every start and every round drew the same random numbers (for example the same `GN_CRS2_LM` population). The `INIT` `SEED` still draws the `INIT` points. Round 0 changes only for an NLopt phase that sets both keys; no example does. The startup warning for an `INIT` `SEED` that no `INIT` method uses says it seeds NLopt only when the optimiser has no `SEED`.
- **`SEED` inside an `INNER` block is a startup error that names the line**: `Error on line N: SEED is not accepted inside an INNER block: NLopt draws the INNER search from the enclosing run's random stream; set SEED on the enclosing STRATEGY block` (or `EXPLORE` / `REFINE` block, wherever the `INNER` block is). MLSL and AUGLAG run their `INNER` search inside the enclosing block's NLopt run, and NLopt has one random stream per run; `make_nlopt()` seeded it for the enclosing block and then again for an `INNER` `SEED`, which therefore replaced the enclosing block's seed. Neither the `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE`-refinement start seeds `SEED + i` nor the `RESTART` seeds reached NLopt: with `SEED 10` on a `MULTISTART` `AUGLAG` and `SEED 20` on its `GN_CRS2_LM` `INNER`, all three starts drew the population of seed 20. A configuration that sets `SEED` in an `INNER` block now fails to load; move the `SEED` to the enclosing block, and the starts draw from 10, 11 and 12. No example sets one. `make_nlopt()` seeds NLopt once, for the block that runs it, and refuses an `INNER` spec that carries a seed (`SEED` or `INIT` `SEED`; the grammar has no `INIT` block inside `INNER`); `derive_restart_seeds()` and the `RESTART r/MAX` journal line no longer handle `INNER` seeds, and the unknown-key error of an `INNER` block no longer lists `SEED`.
- **A launch on more than one MPI rank (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) with an active `RESTART` fails at startup**, before any evaluation: `multi-rank HPO does not support RESTART (…): stagnation is tracked per rank; run on one rank or remove RESTART`. It started, and at the first stagnation the rank that stagnated restarted and blocked in the driver's next collective while its peers exited with `worker failed on another MPI rank`, so the job hung until the scheduler killed it. `hpo_mpi_dispatch_refusal()` is the one rule behind `hpo_mpi_dispatch_supported()` and `hpo_run()`'s error, and `RestartSpec::active()` the one "RESTART is on" rule (both `AFTER_STAGNATION` and `MAX` positive). One rank keeps `RESTART`.
- **The journal trailer of a run that ends by itself is the status of the optimiser run that ended it** (under `MULTISTART` the last start, under `PRESTAGE` the refinement; every driver records one, *Changed*: why each search ended); when the `RESTART` budget ends the run it is `STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in W evals after R restart(s)`. Every optimiser run now starts without a status (`HPOLog::clear_status()` in `run_inner`), and ASA no longer records one for a run that an exception ended. The trailer repeated whatever an earlier phase had left: `MAXEVAL_REACHED` from an NLopt exploration under a DLIB refinement, or `ASA: exception raised during evaluation` from the ASA run the last stagnation stop had ended. Example `13_restart` ends on its `RESTART` budget, and its fingerprint accepts that trailer; `12_prestage`'s accepts only its refinement's status, `DLIB:BFGS: MAXEVAL reached` (the `MAXEVAL_REACHED` it also accepted was its exploration's status).
- **A `PRESTAGE` refinement starts only from the exploration's usable losses.** `HPO_Host`'s top-K buffer took every completed evaluation's loss, a failed one included (`FAILSCORE`, a graded failure), and kept it while it held fewer than `TOPK` entries, so while the exploration had fewer than `TOPK` distinct usable losses a refinement seed started at a failed point, and an exploration whose every evaluation failed so was refined from failed points only. (A candidate a gate rejected before training, `BUMPGATE`, `RHOGATE` or `RHOBARGATE`, and an evaluation that threw never reached the buffer.) The buffer takes a usable loss only (`loss_is_feasible`, the rule of best publication). With fewer candidates than `TOPK` the refinement runs from those and the journal says so, `PRESTAGE: 4 usable candidate(s) for TOPK 6 (the exploration made 6 evaluation(s), 2 without a usable loss); refining 4`. With none, the refinement runs from the outer spec's own points (the OPTIMIZER-level `INIT` points, or a `RESTART` round's fresh ones; after a stagnation stop of the exploration too), as it did for an exploration without a candidate, and the `WARNING` says why: `PRESTAGE: no evaluation of the exploration produced a usable loss (9 evaluation(s)); refining from the OPTIMIZER-level INIT point(s) instead` (it read `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`; an exploration that made no evaluation is an error, below). A run that finds no usable loss there either ends as one (`STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`, status 1).
