Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`PRESTAGE: the refinement starts only from usable losses, fewer than TOPK when fewer exist`, `PRESTAGE: an exploration without a usable loss leaves the refinement to the OPTIMIZER-level INIT points`, `PRESTAGE under RESTART: an exploration that stagnates without a usable loss hands over to a refinement from the INIT points`, `PRESTAGE: an exploration whose every candidate the bump gate rejects is refined from the OPTIMIZER-level INIT point`).
- **A `PRESTAGE` exploration that made no evaluation is an error** (`StagedPolicy`: `std::logic_error`, `PRESTAGE: the exploration (EXPLORE block) TADAH:RANDOM returned without evaluating a point, so it has no candidate to refine (...)`; the run ends `FAILED`, status 1). The refinement ran from the outer spec's own points after a journal `WARNING`, `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`. No configuration the parser and startup accept gets there: every exploration driver evaluates at least one point (`MAXEVAL` is at least 1, and every driver makes its first evaluation whatever `MAXTIME`; `ASA` and a `DLIB:GFS` search without an `INIT` point made none when `MAXTIME` had passed before it, the next entry), every evaluation is counted (a throwing one included), and a stagnation stop is raised inside a counted evaluation; so it is a driver that returned without evaluating, or a spec built outside the parser that asks for no evaluation, which the refinement hid. An exploration whose every evaluation failed is refined from the `INIT` points as before (the entry above). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`PRESTAGE: an exploration that made no evaluation is an error, not a refinement from the INIT points`).
- **A search makes its first evaluation whatever `MAXTIME`**; `MAXTIME` keeps the later ones from starting, as it did. `ASA` read `MAXTIME` before every evaluation, its first included, and `DLIB:GFS` without an `INIT` point did not start `find_min_global` once `MAXTIME` had passed (and dlib reads its run time before every call), so a `MAXTIME` that had passed before the search's first evaluation (one shorter than the driver's setup, `MAXTIME 1e-9`) left it without one: a `PLAIN` run ended with status 1, `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`, and a `PRESTAGE` exploration had nothing to refine (the refinement ran from the `INIT` points after the `WARNING` `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`). The TADAH drivers, which read `MAXTIME` after an evaluation, NLopt and `DLIB:GFS`'s `INIT` points made their first evaluation already. `ASA` reads `MAXTIME` once the search has made an evaluation; a `DLIB:GFS` search that made none after `find_min_global` calls it once more for one call without a run-time limit, which evaluates the point dlib starts every search with (`DLIB:GFS stopped: MAXTIME exceeded after 1 evaluation(s)`, `ASA stopped: MAXTIME exceeded after 1 host eval(s)`). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`MAXTIME: a search makes its first evaluation when MAXTIME has passed before it`).
- A failure to save `best_pot.tadah` (`durable_best::Error`) ends NLopt and `MULTISTART` runs as an error, like every other backend; NLopt reported it as convergence and `MULTISTART` moved on to the next start.
- NLopt: an error raised by the objective or the gradient provider is reported as an error, not as the algorithm's `nlopt failure` self-termination; and `NLOPT_ROUNDOFF_LIMITED` is a graceful stop (`STOPPED -- nlopt roundoff-limited (...)`) — the adapter compared the message with `roundoff-limited`, which NLopt never produces, and failed the run. The unused `HPO_Host::nlopt_adapter_cpp`, which bypassed this handling, is removed.
- The final flush (`HPO_Host::flush_remaining`) writes results stranded behind a missing iteration, in iteration order, with a journal warning (`N result(s) stranded behind missing iteration k`); it stopped at the gap and dropped them silently. A row whose write fails is still reported (`dropping iter=N`).
- A new journal does not inherit the termination status of a previous run in the same process (`HPOLog::open` clears it), and it is written even when that run left its journal open (it failed during its setup, before its trailer): `HPOLog::open` opened the new file on the still-open stream, which failed silently, so nothing of the new run reached its journal. `HPO_Host::reset_globals_for_test()` also resets the result writer and the stagnation watchdog, so an in-process test no longer receives another test's unflushed results.
- `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` workers ignore `SIGUSR2` as well as `SIGINT` and `SIGTERM` (`kPcWorkerIgnoredSignals`, which a test checks against the signals `core::SignalHandler` handles). A `SIGUSR2` sent to the whole process group or job step killed the workers, and the evaluations they were running became FAILSCOREs.
- **`STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART` with a global optimiser that consumes a single starting point, and `TYPE PRESTAGE` with a configured `TOPK` > 1 and such a refinement optimiser, are startup errors** (`validate_hpo_setup`), raised before the host is built or any data is loaded; so is `TYPE PRESTAGE` without an `EXPLORE` block. `validate_hpo_setup` now runs as soon as the configuration is read, before the worker pool is sized and before any `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker is started (it ran after both), and the journal records a startup error on an `ERROR` line (`ERROR     startup: <message>`): it went to stderr only, and the journal ended without a word. The policies found out only when they ran: after the host had loaded the data, and under `PRESTAGE` after the whole exploration, only when it had harvested more than one candidate (and not at all when it had harvested one). `PRESTAGE` has its own message: `STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE with TOPK 3 cannot refine with TADAH:ANNEAL: ... Use a local or multi-seed refinement optimiser (...), or TOPK 1 to refine the best candidate once.` The rules live once in `strategy_rules.{h,cpp}` (`validate_run_policy`, `require_multistart_backend`, `require_prestage_explore`, `require_prestage_refinement`); `MultiStartPolicy::validate` and `StagedPolicy::validate` forward to them and still run when the policies do. The alternatives the messages list are sorted (`format_caps_list`); they came in hash order.
- **An optimiser without `LIB` or `ALGO` is a parse error that names its block**, at the line that closes it: the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser (under `PRESTAGE` the refinement, `STRATEGY` level or `REFINE` block) at `ENDOPTIMIZER`, an `EXPLORE` block at `ENDEXPLORE`, and a block the file never closes, or a file without an `OPTIMIZER` block, at its end: `Error on line 8: the OPTIMIZER block names no optimiser: LIB and ALGO are not set; set LIB and ALGO in its STRATEGY block (or directly inside OPTIMIZER)`. It failed at run time with `unknown optimizer NOT SET:NOT SET`. An `INNER` block needs both too, checked at its `ENDINNER` line, naming the block it sits in: `Error on line 10: the INNER block inside STRATEGY names no optimiser: ALGO is not set; set LIB and ALGO inside the INNER block (the local optimiser that NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG run)`; one without `ALGO` failed inside NLopt once the run had started, and one without `LIB` skipped the `STEP` checks. The parser's checks that only tolerated a missing `LIB`/`ALGO` are gone. This is the rule of the hpotarget grammar, and of a `tadah refit` stage block that runs an optimiser (below).
- **An `INNER` block needs an optimiser that runs one, and `LIB NLOPT`**: only NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG algorithms run an `INNER` search, and they run it with NLopt. Both are startup errors naming the optimiser the block belongs to (`strategy_rules`: `require_inner_backend`, called by `validate_run_policy` and again by `run_inner`): `DLIB:BFGS does not accept an INNER sub-optimiser (the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser): ...`, `the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser names DLIB:BFGS, but NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS runs its INNER search with NLopt: ...`. The first was reported only once the host had loaded the data; with another `LIB` the run failed inside NLopt once it had started (`wrong algorithm string`).
- **The `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` gradient rules apply to the `PRESTAGE` `EXPLORE` block and its `INNER` block**, as they did to the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser and its `INNER` block: with `PC_LAMMPS`, a gradient-based optimiser other than `DLIB:BFGS` and any `GRAD_STRATEGY` are parse errors there, and so is `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` with `PC_OBSERVABLE` (`--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS but EXPLORE's optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS is gradient-based. PC_LAMMPS requires a gradient-free optimiser ...`). `NLOPT LD_LBFGS` in `EXPLORE` failed at once inside NLopt (`nlopt failure`) and the run reported success after a wasted exploration; a `GRAD_STRATEGY` there failed only after the first evaluation.
- **`N_STARTS`, `PARALLEL` and `BUDGET_PER_START` outside `TYPE MULTISTART`, and `TOPK` outside `TYPE PRESTAGE`, are rejected at their own line** (`N_STARTS is not accepted with STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN: only TYPE MULTISTART runs N_STARTS starts, so it would have no effect; set TYPE MULTISTART or remove N_STARTS`). They were parsed under every `TYPE` and silently ignored. The check runs when the `STRATEGY` block ends, so `TYPE` may come before or after the key; no example sets one under another `TYPE`. The same check covers an `EXPLORE` or `REFINE` block that a later `TYPE` line takes out of `PRESTAGE` (the run silently became `PLAIN`, without the exploration), and a second `STRATEGY` block is an error at its line (`STRATEGY block given more than once (the first opens on line 4); ...`): it reopened the first, and its `TYPE` replaced the one the first block's keys had been checked against.
- **Numbers in the hpotarget file are parsed strictly** (`strict_parse.{h,cpp}`: `parse_uint`, `parse_finite`, `single_value`, `parse_lammps_timespec`, `parse_script_timeout`). Every numeric key takes one whole token that represents what it holds, else it is an error at its own line: `Error on line 6: MAXEVAL must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647, got '1e5'`, `FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got 'abc'`, `N_STARTS requires a value`, `QFLUSH takes a single value (unexpected '7')`. `iss >> v` and `std::stod` read a prefix (`5x` as 5, `MAXEVAL 1e5` as 1 and then complained about `e5`), stored 0 for a non-number (`FAILSCORE abc` scored every failure 0, `MAX abc` switched `RESTART` off), wrapped a negative into a huge unsigned value (`N_STARTS -2`, `MAXEVAL -1`), read an uninitialised variable for a missing `N_STARTS`/`TOPK`/`MAX`/`AFTER_STAGNATION`, and ignored extra tokens. Covered: `FAILSCORE`, `QFLUSH` (≥ 0), `DIGITS` (1–15), `OUTPUT`/`BOUTPUT` (≥ 1), `DUMP`/`BDUMP` (the directory is created only once the line is valid), `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` (now a single value), `PC_FAILPROBE --gate/--s0` (`--s0` a positive number), `N_STARTS`/`PARALLEL`/`TOPK` (≥ 1), `BUDGET_PER_START` (1 to 2147483647), `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION`/`MAX` (≥ 0), `INIT K_TOTAL`/`SEED` and `METHOD <K>` (now checked at the `METHOD` line; `LHS` and `SOBOL` take `K` from 1, in the same message: `K 0` was `INIT METHOD LHS K must be > 0`), `NOISE CALIBRATION`/`AUTO`, `LOG_HP`, the optimiser attributes (`MAXEVAL`, `SEED`, `POPULATION`, `VECTOR_ARR`, `THREADS`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `FTOL_*`, `XTOL_*`; `LIB`/`ALGO` need a value; `MAXEVAL` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block (below), the `int` NLopt counts evaluations in, as are `BUDGET_PER_START`, each start's `MAXEVAL`, and `tadah refit`'s `MAXITER`, the budgets the refit hands NLopt and Ceres as an `int`: the NLopt adapter converted `MAXEVAL` implicitly, so `MAXEVAL 4294967299` ran 3 evaluations and `2147483648` became a negative budget, which NLopt reads as no limit, and `make_nlopt` now refuses such a value in a spec built another way; `THREADS` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block: `THREADS 0` was accepted, and at the `OPTIMIZER`/`STRATEGY` level the pool quietly ran one worker; `ScriptExecutor`, `init_script_executor` and `PcWorkerPool` now reject a width below 1 instead of raising it to 1; `MAXTIME` is a positive number of seconds (`parse_positive`: `MAXTIME must be a positive number of seconds, got '-5'`), left out for no limit: 0 or a negative value meant no limit to NLopt and `TADAH` and a startup error to ASA and the annealers), `STEP` (the block-wide fraction and an override's must be finite numbers: `STEP inf` failed only when the run started, and `STEP 5x` was taken for an override key; an override's index list has the strict grammar of the `OPTIM` lines, where `STEP CGRID2B(3x) 0.3` dropped `3x` with a message on stderr and applied to every index), numeric `PARAM` values (`PARAM fd_step inf` failed only when the optimiser ran, and `PARAM fd_step 5x` was kept as a word no backend read), the single-value keys (`TYPE`, `PC_ISOLATION`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, `BUMPGATE`, `OPTIM_SCALE`, `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`), the explicit `OPTIM` bounds (`0.1x` was read as 0.1), and the `PC_LAMMPS` options `--varloss` target/weight/loss parameter (a negative weight stays allowed), `--failscore`, `--hotvar` min/max/initial and `--failvar` scale: an optional `--hotvar`/`--failvar` value that did not parse used to be passed on to LAMMPS as a command-line argument. A configuration that relied on the lenient reading now fails at startup with the line. Every real number has one grammar (`parse_number`), the decimal one LAMMPS reads (`utils::is_double`: an optional sign, digits with at most one decimal point, an optional exponent), converted with `std::from_chars` (locale-independent): `parse_finite`, `parse_lammps_timespec`, `PC_<METRIC>` (`parse_metric_number`), `LOSS`, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`, the `OPTIM` numbers, `STEP`, `PARAM` (the `INIT_MODE JITTER` extras too), the `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` options and the `tadah refit` keys all use it, and `is_number_shaped` decides which optional token is meant as a number. `std::stod` also read hexadecimal floats: `FAILSCORE 0x1p40` loaded as 1.0995e+12 and `STEP 0x1p-3` as 0.125, while `PC_<METRIC>` rejected them; they are errors (`FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got '0x1p40'`). A number below the smallest normal double is rejected and says so (`... got '1e-320' (too small: ...)`): a subnormal (`1e-320`), or one that underflows to 0 (`1e-400`). It was reported as not a finite number, and `PC_<METRIC>` accepted it; LAMMPS accepts it too, so this is HPO's own rule. The test is on the bits of the double, like the finiteness test, so it holds with denormals-are-zero on (an executable linked with `-ffast-math`), where a subnormal compares equal to 0. The `PC_<METRIC>` errors name the key and the value (`PC_ERMSE <weight> must be a finite number, got '1x'`; it was `Expected a finite number, got '1x'`), and the `OPTIM` and optimiser-attribute errors name their line once (`Error on line 9: OPTIM WATOM: <high> must be ...`; it was `Error on line 9: OPTIM WATOM on line 9: ...`). A value in the grammar but outside its key's range is named too: `PC_EMAE <weight> must be a number >= 0, got '-1'`, `PC_EMAE --skip-above must be a positive number, got '-1'`, `PC_FAILPROBE --s0 must be a positive number, got '-1'`, `--failvar F <scale> must be a positive number, got '0'` (they were `PC_EMAE weight must be nonnegative`, `... requires a positive number`, `PC_FAILPROBE --s0 must be finite and > 0`, `--failvar F: <scale> must be > 0`).
- **A `PC_LAMMPS` option takes only its own values.** A token after an HPO option's values (`--maxtime 1:30 00`, a typo for `1:30:00`; `--failscore 100 5`) was passed to LAMMPS as a command-line argument, so every evaluation failed on it (`Invalid command-line argument: 00`) and the run reported no best loss. It is an error at the line naming the option (`--maxtime takes a single value (unexpected '00')`), and so is a token before any option; a LAMMPS option (`-var x 1 2`, `-pk omp 4 neigh no`) keeps every value that follows it. The LAMMPS variable `maxtime` is reserved for the script timeout: `-var maxtime`, `-v maxtime` and `--invar maxtime` are errors. Such a value replaced the `timer timeout ${maxtime}` limit unchecked while the journal reported `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` and its deadline, and any `-var` whose value was the word `maxtime` suppressed the limit.
- **`tadah refit` `EXPLORE MAX_ADD` is an integer from 0 to 2147483647** (`parse_uint`); `std::stoi` read `+3` as 3, and a negative value silently meant no cap. Leaving `MAX_ADD` out means no cap. The cap arithmetic no longer overflows for a large `MAX_ADD`.
- **The compact `tadah refit` reads its configuration numbers with MLIP's strict reader** (`read_refit_numbers`, before the table is read; see Tadah.MLIP): `cget`/`iget`/`bget` and the `std::stoi` of `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` (`+3` was 3) are gone, `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED` is read unsigned, 0 to 4294967295 (3000000000 failed converting as an `int`), and `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX`, which `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve` reads, is range-checked by the same reader (under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy` the mode rule refuses the key before its value is read: see the `REFIT_MODE` entry under *Changed*). A `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` whose curve sample grid, at least 4 × NMAX points per function, does not fit an `int` is refused by that reader, before the table is read (`4 * NMAX` overflowed above 536870911). `CompactBlipFitter` refuses an `opt_every` or `insert_topk` below 1, which `std::max` made 1 silently, and a `jitters` outside 0 to `INT_MAX` - 1, whose candidate loop overflowed.
- **`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT`, `MAXTIME` and `--maxtime` are LAMMPS time specs** (`off`, `unlimited` or `[[HH:]MM:]SS`, one to three non-empty parts of the number grammar: a strict subset of what `timer timeout` reads, to the same seconds; LAMMPS also takes an empty or a fourth part and `std::stod`'s forms), checked at their line; they were passed to LAMMPS unchecked. A limit of 0 or less is rejected too (`parse_script_timeout`): `Error on line 9: PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT must be a positive time, 'off' or 'unlimited' (a limit <= 0 stops every script), got '0'`. LAMMPS accepts it, but 0 stops a script at its first timer check, and with 0 or a negative limit `$(timeremain)` is 0, so the section guard stopped every multi-section script and the evaluations scored `FAILSCORE`. `off` and `unlimited` are the ways to write no limit. A spec whose parts add up to an infinite time (`1e307:0:0`) is rejected as well. `unlimited` now means no limit, like `off`: it was injected as `timer timeout unlimited`, which leaves `$(timeremain)` at 0, so the section guard failed every multi-section script as timed out. The PROCESS worker's hard deadline (`pc_worker_deadline_ms()`, now a free function) is computed from the parsed seconds: the old `strtod` read `2:00:00` as 2 s, so the worker was killed after 122 s, and `00:05:00` as 0, so there was no deadline. A limit <= 0 reaching it is an `std::invalid_argument`, no longer "no deadline". The journal prints the parsed limit and the derived deadline. The worker's wait (`pcwire::readn`) polls a deadline above `INT_MAX` ms in slices: `static_cast<int>` wrapped it, so a script timeout above about 715828 s (derived deadline) or a `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` above about 2147484 s killed the worker at once (`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT 1431656`: after 704 ms) or gave it no deadline, while the journal reported the deadline. A timeout whose deadline does not fit in 64-bit milliseconds (a script timeout above about 3.07e15 s, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` or `TADAH_PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` above about 9.2e15 s) is an error at its line (`... is too large for the worker deadline, got '4e15'`); its conversion was undefined behaviour.
- **A `tadah refit` stage's `OPTIMIZER` block rejects the keys the stage does not use** (`read_optimizer_block` takes a key check, `stage_key_check`), naming the keys it reads: `REFINE: THREADS is not used by a tadah refit stage (it reads LIB, ALGO, MAXEVAL/MAXITER, FTOL_REL, FTOL_ABS, GTOL)`. EXPLORE reads `LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL` and `INNER`, its `INNER` block `LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `XTOL_REL`, REFINE `LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `GTOL`, and FUSE the same without `FTOL_ABS` (its `GTOL`, accepted and ignored before, is read now: see the curve-mode FUSE entry); within these, a key the block's `ALGO` does not read is an error too (below). Every other optimiser attribute (`THREADS`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `POPULATION`, `STEP`, `PARAM`, …) and an `INNER` block in REFINE or FUSE was accepted and did nothing. `SEED` is rejected with a pointer to `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED`, which seeds the refit, and `FTOL_ABS` with a Ceres `ALGO` with a pointer to `FTOL_REL`, as before, now at the key. Every stage `OPTIMIZER` block and `INNER` block must set `ALGO` (`EXPLORE INNER: ALGO is not set; set ALGO in the INNER block (NONE for no local search)`): an `INNER` block without `ALGO` silently ran no local search, while an `EXPLORE` optimiser without an `INNER` block runs `LN_SBPLX`; the other blocks failed with `unsupported optimiser LIB '...' ALGO '(unset)'`. `GTOL`, `MAXITER` and the `INIT MANUAL` operands are strict there too. Every error of a stage's `OPTIMIZER` block names the block, the `INNER` block's as `EXPLORE INNER` (`REFINE: FTOL_REL must be a finite number, got '0x1p-40'`, `EXPLORE INNER: unknown key 'FTOLREL' in the INNER block`); a malformed number named no block.
- `PC_OBSERVABLE` values must be finite numbers, and its integer flags take decimal digits only, from 1 (`--supercell`) or 0 (`--maxiter`, `--maxeval`) to 2147483647, in the message of every integer key (`PC_OBSERVABLE: flag '--maxiter' must be an integer from 0 to 2147483647, got '-5'`): a narrowing cast turned `--supercell 4294967300` into 4, a sign was read (`--maxiter -0` was 0), and a negative value got a range error that did not name it; an ASA `PARAM` index too large for a `long` names the `PARAM` (the error was `stol`).
- **`MAXEVAL` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block** (short form, `STRATEGY`, `EXPLORE`, `REFINE`, `INNER`), and so are `tadah refit`'s `MAXEVAL` and `MAXITER`: `MAXEVAL 0` is an error at its line (`MAXEVAL must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647, got '0'`). It meant something different to every backend: no limit to NLopt, no evaluation to `TADAH:RANDOM`, the annealers and ASA, the first starting point to `DLIB:GFS`, an error when `DLIB:BFGS` and `CERES` started, nothing to `TADAH:GRID`, and in `tadah refit` no Ceres iteration. Leave the key out for no evaluation limit; `BUDGET_PER_START` was already from 1.
- **A backend with no convergence test and no finite schedule needs a stop** at startup: `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, a tolerance it honours set above 0, or an active `RESTART` (`GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND`: `MAXEVAL`, see *Changed*), or an error names the backend, why and the stops it honours (`NLOPT:GN_DIRECT needs a stop: NLopt's global search has no convergence test of its own and runs until it is interrupted; set MAXEVAL, MAXTIME, STOPVAL or a positive tolerance it honours (FTOL_REL, FTOL_ABS, XTOL_REL, XTOL_ABS); DIRECT's XTOL_REL and XTOL_ABS end it only once every rectangle is that small, after roughly (1/XTOL_REL)^n evaluations in n dimensions`), in every phase. A tolerance of 0 or below is no stop: NLopt switches it off and `ANNEAL_PT`'s best-stall rule arms only above 0, so `FTOL_REL 0` ran until it was killed. An active `RESTART` (`AFTER_STAGNATION` and `MAX` above 0) is a stop of every phase it wraps (but not the stop of a `GD_STOGO` or `GD_STOGO_RAND` search): once its `MAX` restarts are used, the next `AFTER_STAGNATION` evaluations without a new best end the run (`STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in W evals after MAX restart(s)`), so `GN_DIRECT_L` without a stop of its own under `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION 60 MAX 1` runs as before (699 evaluations on the examples' data) — at startup (`restart_stop_of`) and when a driver checks its spec again at run time, the `PRESTAGE` exploration included (`restart_stop_armed`: `RestartPolicy` has armed the stagnation watchdog); the journal says so (`(none set -- the RESTART stagnation stop ends NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L: once its MAX 1 restart(s) are used, AFTER_STAGNATION 60 evaluations without a new best)`). Measured by running each backend without a stop, through `hpo_run` and on synthetic objectives: NLopt's DIRECT family, `GN_CRS2_LM` and `GN_ISRES` (every tolerance ends them), `GN_ESCH`, `GN_AGS`, StoGO and the MLSL family (no tolerance does; MLSL's reach only its local searches), `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ` and `LD_SLSQP` (the tolerances are their only convergence test), `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` (`FTOL_REL`/`FTOL_ABS`, its best-stall rule), `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` (no tolerance; `GFS`'s `FTOL_ABS` is its solver epsilon) ran until they were killed; the original DIRECT (`GN_ORIG_DIRECT`, `_L`) stops itself after about 100000 evaluations (its cap is checked once per iteration: 100011 to 100071 in the probes) and the other local algorithms converge. DIRECT's `XTOL` does end it, but only once every rectangle is below it, since DIRECT divides the largest rectangle in every sweep: roughly (1/`XTOL_REL`)^n evaluations for n dimensions (`XTOL_REL 1e-3` in two dimensions ran past 20000), which the message says (`OptimizerCaps::tolerance_note`). They were accepted and ran until interrupted, and `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` needed `MAXEVAL` itself. The caps field `OptimizerCaps::needs_stop` (with `stop_note`; `require_stop`, `backend_rules.h`, which takes whether the `RESTART` stagnation stop bounds the run, `RestartStop`) replaces `needs_maxeval`, and the `supports_*` flags now say which criteria end a run. NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG hand their local search (an `INNER` block, or NLopt's default local optimiser) what is left of the enclosing `MAXEVAL` and `MAXTIME`, and the enclosing `STOPVAL` in place of the `INNER` block's (`inner_run_spec`, `phase_specs.h`; `STOPVAL` inside `INNER` is therefore an error at its line, below): an `INNER` global search under `AUGLAG`, or `LD_AUGLAG` without an `INNER` block (NLopt's `LD_MMA`), needs a stop from either block (`require_inner_run_stop`). The journal's stop criteria no longer say that an optimiser without any "may run indefinitely": it ends on its own (`(none set -- TADAH:GRID ends on its own: …)`), under `MULTISTART` each start has its `BUDGET_PER_START`, or the `RESTART` stagnation stop ends it.
- **`TADAH:GRID` honours `MAXEVAL`**: it stops after `MAXEVAL` points in the order it visits them (the `INIT` point, then the grid nodes, the first dimension of the documented dimension order varying fastest), and the journal says so: `GRID: MAXEVAL 5 < 28 grid points (the INIT point, then 27 = 3^3 grid nodes); evaluating the first 5 in grid order`. It ignored `MAXEVAL` without a word (`OptimizerCaps::supports_maxeval` was false and never enforced). Without `MAXEVAL` the whole grid runs, as before; example 11 has no `MAXEVAL` and is unchanged.
- **Each backend's attribute and `PARAM` checks run at startup, on the settings each phase runs with** (`validate_phase_backends`, `backend_rules.h`, called by `validate_hpo_setup` after the `STRATEGY` rules): the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, each `MULTISTART` start (its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL`), the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, and every `INNER` block. The message names the phase: `the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not support MAXTIME`. They ran only when that optimiser started: after the data were loaded, for the `PRESTAGE` refinement after the whole exploration, and under `MULTISTART` each start failed with a warning, so the run ended without an evaluation. The drivers still apply their own checks when they start. The spec of each phase comes from the new `phase_specs.h` (`plan_multistart`, `multistart_start_spec`, `prestage_refinement_spec`, `strategy_phase_specs`), which `MultiStartPolicy` and `StagedPolicy` now run with, so the startup checks see what the run runs. `validate_backend` dispatches to each backend's validator: the annealers' `Knobs::parse` and the `ANNEAL_PT` replica count (`anneal::pt_replica_count`, factored out of the driver), ASA's options, `DLIB:BFGS`, the CERES validators, and `TADAH:RANDOM`, `DLIB:GFS`, `TADAH:GRID` (moved out of `run_grid`; a word-valued `PARAM` is now an error too) and NLopt (`validate_nlopt_spec`, `make_nlopt`'s checks). The annealers, ASA, `DLIB:BFGS`, CERES and `TADAH:GRID` reject a `PARAM` key they do not read; `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` reject every `PARAM` (below), and NLopt passes every `PARAM` to `nlopt_set_param`, where an algorithm reads only the names it knows. The validators journal nothing: the `BOUNDARY_MODE REDRAW` hint and ASA's `Queue_Resolution` hint are written when the run starts (`anneal::announce_knobs`, `announce_asa_options`). No shipped example or test configuration is rejected by the new checks.
- **A refusal of the `OPTIMIZER` block's settings at startup names the line of its block**, as the parser's refusals do (`Error on line 17: the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not support MAXTIME` and `Line: ENDOPTIMIZER`): every backend's checks (`validate_phase_backends`: the CERES validators, `DLIB:BFGS`, the annealers, `TADAH:RANDOM`/`GRID`, `DLIB:GFS`, ASA, NLopt, a backend that needs a stop), the per-phase checks of `validate_hpo_setup` (finite differences, `GRAD_STRATEGY` on a phase that takes no gradient, `NOISE AUTO`, ASA's probes) and the `STRATEGY` rules (`validate_run_policy`: `STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART cannot run ...`, the `INNER` rules). The line is where `HPOTargets::read()` checks the phase's optimiser (`OptimizerSpec::block_line`, `block_line_text`): `ENDOPTIMIZER` for the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, a `MULTISTART` start and the `PRESTAGE` refinement (a `REFINE` block's keys included), `ENDEXPLORE` for the exploration, `ENDINNER` for an `INNER` block, and a block's own line for NLopt's default local search or a missing `INNER` block. They named the phase and no line; the caps-registry refusals (`LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`) and every parse error already named it. One form for both, `config_line_error()` (`strict_parse.h`), and `with_block_line()` (`phase_specs.h`); a spec built outside the parser has no line, and its message is unchanged. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp` (`a phase's settings refused at startup name the line that closes its block`; `judge()` checks the line of every refusal that names a phase).
- **More refusals of a block's settings name the line of the block**, in the same form (`with_block_line`): the check of the `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` setup (`validate_hpo_setup`: `Error on line 20: OPTIMIZER block: GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL (resolved for PC_OBSERVABLE: ...) needs the analytical gradient of the loss, ...` and `Line: ENDOPTIMIZER`; `EXPLORE block: ...` at `ENDEXPLORE`), the gradient rules `HPOTargets::read()` applies after the file (`--hpotarget defines PC_OBSERVABLE but OPTIMIZER's GRAD_STRATEGY=NUMERICAL is set ...`, `--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS but EXPLORE's optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS is gradient-based ...`, an `INNER` block's at its `ENDINNER`), the `STEP` rules (`validate_optimizer_scaling`, at parse time and at startup: `DLIB:BFGS (OUTER) does not use STEP; ...`, a `STEP` outside `(0, 0.5]`, overrides that cover only some dimensions) and `hpo_mpi_dispatch_refusal` of a parsed spec (`multi-rank HPO supports only ...`, `multi-rank HPO does not support RESTART ...`). **The rule that NLOPT, DLIB and CERES need an `OPTIM` line holds for every phase** (`validate_phase_backends`, before the backend's own checks): `Error on line 14: the PRESTAGE exploration (EXPLORE block): NLOPT:GN_DIRECT cannot optimise a zero-dimensional search space (there is no OPTIM line; ...). Add at least one OPTIM line, or use a TADAH-native optimiser, which evaluates the INIT start point once.` and `Line:     ENDEXPLORE`. `validate_hpo_setup` checked the `STRATEGY`-level `LIB` only (`No OPTIM line: the search space has zero dimensions, and LIB NLOPT cannot optimise a zero-dimensional space ...`, no line), so an `EXPLORE` block's NLopt optimiser or `DLIB:GFS` passed startup and failed in the run (`NULL args to nlopt_optimize_`, dlib's `The vectors can't be empty`), and an `EXPLORE` block's `DLIB:BFGS` was refused as `... DLIB:BFGS requires non-empty, same-sized low/high bounds`. A spec built outside the parser keeps its message. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp` (`the GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL check, the zero-dimension rule and the multi-rank refusal name the line that closes their block`, `the STEP rules name the line that closes the block whose STEP they refuse`; `judge()` knows `OPTIMIZER block` and `EXPLORE block`), `test_startup_validation.cpp`, `test_pc_observable_parser.cpp`.
- **A refusal of an `INIT` or `RESTART` block's `METHOD` entry when its chain is drawn names the `METHOD` line** (`InitStrategySpec::Entry::line` and `line_text`, which the parser records; `build_init_strategy` wraps such an entry): `Error on line 3: WARM: cannot open seed file 'missing.tadah'. ...` and `Line:     METHOD WARM missing.tadah`, likewise an unreadable `WARM` file, a `WARM` seed whose `RHOFORM` the run refuses, a `WARM` seed that `WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` refuses (`WARM: 1 parameter(s) not found in seed.tadah: RCUT2B`) and a `SOBOL` block beyond the sequence. The chains are drawn after the file has been read (the `INIT` chains and the `RESTART` chain's seeds at startup, a restart round's points when it starts), and these refusals named no line. An entry built outside the parser keeps its message. Refusals that still name no line of the file: a starting point outside its `OPTIM` box or not finite (`INIT[OUTER] starting point 1: ...`), `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` without a finite positive `FAILSCORE`, and the bump-support and density gates, which name their keys. Tests: `test_restart_seeds.cpp` (`A METHOD chain's refusal names the line of its METHOD`).
- **A `tadah refit` stage block that runs an optimiser names the library of its `ALGO` in `LIB`**, as `tadah hpo` requires `LIB` and `ALGO` in every block: `NLOPT` for the NLopt algorithms and their short names, `DLIB` for `GLOBAL`/`DLIB_GLOBAL` and `LBFGS`/`DLIB_LBFGS`, `CERES` for `LM`/`DOGLEG`. A missing `LIB` (`REFINE: LIB is not set; set LIB NLOPT (ALGO LD_LBFGS is an NLopt algorithm)`) and a `LIB` that does not match (`EXPLORE: LIB CERES does not match ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS, an NLopt algorithm; set LIB NLOPT`) are errors; `LIB` could be left out where the `ALGO` named the optimiser and was ignored when it did not match. When the stage has algorithms of the `LIB` given, the message names them too, since that `LIB` may be the one meant: `REFINE: LIB NLOPT does not match ALGO LBFGS, a dlib algorithm; set LIB DLIB, or with LIB NLOPT set ALGO LD_LBFGS`. The `ALGO` names are the refit's own, not those of `tadah hpo`: its dlib global search is `GLOBAL` (`tadah hpo`'s `GFS`), and `LBFGS` is dlib's L-BFGS (NLopt's is `LD_LBFGS`; in `tadah hpo` `LBFGS` is a CERES algorithm). `ALGO NONE` runs no optimiser, so a `LIB` there is an error (`EXPLORE INNER: LIB has no effect with ALGO NONE (no local search runs); remove LIB`). `UNIFORM`, the refit's own uniform start, runs no optimiser either, so a `LIB` with it is an error too (below).
- **`tadah refit`'s EXPLORE optimiser refuses `ALGO GN_MLSL`, `GN_DIRECT` and `GN_CRS2`**, whatever the `LIB`, naming what each is and the algorithm the refit runs: `EXPLORE: ALGO GN_MLSL is not run by tadah refit (NLopt's MLSL with pseudo-random starts); its MLSL is GN_MLSL_LDS: set ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS`, likewise `GN_DIRECT` (NLopt's DIRECT without the local bias of DIRECT_L) for `GN_DIRECT_L` and `GN_CRS2` (not an NLopt algorithm) for `GN_CRS2_LM`. They were aliases: `GN_MLSL` ran `GN_MLSL_LDS`, `GN_DIRECT` ran `GN_DIRECT_L` and `GN_CRS2` ran `GN_CRS2_LM`, byte-identical results, while `tadah hpo` runs `GN_MLSL` and `GN_DIRECT` as named. The refit's short names `MLSL`, `DIRECT` and `CRS2` stay.
- The journal prints a `PRESTAGE` `EXPLORE` or refinement block without `MAXEVAL` as `MAXEVAL=none` (the *Run policy* lines of the OPTIMISER section, `PRESTAGE EXPLORE  : TADAH:GRID  MAXEVAL=none  TOPK=2`) and `(MAXEVAL=none: no evaluation limit)` (the exploration-phase line); it printed `MAXEVAL=0`.
- `tadah refit`'s L-BFGS `REFINE` computed its budget, 50 per `MAXEVAL`, in an `int`: signed overflow, undefined behaviour, for `MAXEVAL` above 42949672 (in practice a negative budget, which NLopt reads as no limit). The budget is now `MAXEVAL` evaluations itself (see *Changed*), and `lbfgs_refine_budget` is gone.
- **`TADAH:RANDOM`, `TADAH:GRID` and `DLIB:GFS` honour `MAXTIME` and `STOPVAL`**, which they accepted and ignored: no evaluation starts once `MAXTIME` has passed, and the first feasible loss at or below `STOPVAL` ends the search (`GRID` in grid order; `GFS` through dlib's run-time limit and stop condition, the points queued after a `STOPVAL` hit returning without running). The journal says which stop ended the search (`TADAH:RANDOM stopped: STOPVAL reached after 7 evaluation(s)`, `TADAH:GRID stopped: MAXTIME exceeded after 812 of 1000001 grid points`), and the stop is the run's status (`TADAH:RANDOM: STOPVAL reached`); one that spends `MAXEVAL` or the grid ends on that (`TADAH:RANDOM stopped: MAXEVAL reached after 30 evaluation(s)`, the status `TADAH:RANDOM: MAXEVAL reached` or `TADAH:GRID: all grid points evaluated`; *Changed*: why each search ended), also when `MAXTIME` has passed by its last evaluation, which starts no other: `MAXTIME` is tested only while another evaluation would start. `RANDOM` and `GFS` run on `MAXTIME` or `STOPVAL` alone.
- **`TADAH:RANDOM`, `TADAH:GRID` and `DLIB:GFS` reject a `PARAM` they do not read**, naming what they take (`TADAH:RANDOM: unknown PARAM 'FOO' (it takes none)`, `TADAH:GRID: unknown PARAM 'FOO' (it takes GRID_POINTS)`); `RANDOM` and `GFS` accepted any. `DLIB:GFS` rejects `FTOL_REL`, `XTOL_REL` and `XTOL_ABS`, which it cannot apply. The `ALGO` help text of the library (`OptimizerSpec::print_caps`, which no `tadah` command prints) lists each backend's stops from the caps registry, DIRECT's `XTOL` note, and that a backend that needs a stop takes one of them (a tolerance above 0) or an active `RESTART`; `GFS`'s listed the `PARAM`s `SOLVER_EPS` and `REL_NOISE`, which it never read, and `BFGS`'s called `MAXEVAL` an iteration limit.
- **Optimiser keys a backend never reads are rejected at startup**, naming the phase, the backend and the stops it honours or the backends that read the key: `RANDOM` and `GRID` refuse `FTOL_*`/`XTOL_*`, `POPULATION` and `VECTOR_ARR` (`GRID` also `SEED`); `GFS` refuses `POPULATION` and `VECTOR_ARR` (its `SEED` keeps its warning); NLopt refuses a tolerance its search never tests (every one of `GN_ESCH`, `GN_AGS` and `GD_STOGO*`, all but `XTOL_REL` of `GN_ORIG_DIRECT*`, and every one of MLSL and AUGLAG with an `INNER` block, which NLopt hands only to the default local optimiser it creates without one), and `POPULATION` or `VECTOR_ARR` on an algorithm that has none (`POPULATION`: `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ISRES`, `GN_ESCH`, the MLSL forms, `GD_STOGO_RAND`; `VECTOR_ARR`: `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_VAR*`, `LD_TNEWTON*`); `CERES` refuses `SEED`; `STOPVAL` inside an `INNER` block is an error at its line (NLopt replaces it with the enclosing block's); `GRAD_STRATEGY` on a phase whose optimiser takes no gradient (a derivative-free NLopt algorithm, `RANDOM`, `GRID`, `GFS`, and an MLSL or AUGLAG whose local search takes none: see the next entry) is refused (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LN_SBPLX: GRAD_STRATEGY never takes effect: NLOPT:LN_SBPLX takes no gradient; remove it.`). E.g. `TADAH:RANDOM does not support FTOL_REL: uniform random search has no convergence test to apply it to; stop it with MAXEVAL, MAXTIME or STOPVAL`, `NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS does not support FTOL_REL with an INNER block: ...; set it in the INNER block, whose local search tests it` (when the `INNER` algorithm does not test it either, the message names the stops that search honours instead: `...; the INNER block's NLOPT:GN_ESCH does not test it either: stop that search with MAXEVAL or MAXTIME in the INNER block, or MAXEVAL, MAXTIME or STOPVAL in the NLOPT:AUGLAG block, which NLopt hands on to it`), `NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA does not use POPULATION; remove it (it is read by NLOPT:GD_MLSL, ..., TADAH:ANNEAL_PA)`. They were accepted and did nothing: the journal listed them as if they applied (`ftol_rel` among `RANDOM`'s stop criteria, `Random seed` for `GRID`, `Inner stop criteria: stopval`), and an AUGLAG tolerance with an `INNER` block relabelled a run that spent its `MAXEVAL` `XTOL_REACHED`. One NLopt behaviour follows: an MLSL block's `XTOL_ABS` with an `INNER` block that set none of `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS` and `XTOL_REL` switched off the tolerances NLopt gives such an `INNER` search (`FTOL_REL 1e-15`, `XTOL_REL 1e-7`), which it now always gets. `SEED` stays accepted on every NLopt algorithm. `OptimizerCaps::is_population` (unused, and wrong for `ANNEAL_PT` and `GD_STOGO_RAND`) is replaced by `reads_population`, `reads_vector_storage` and `reads_seed`; `validate_backend()` and `validate_nlopt_spec()` take the `SpecRole` of the spec (an `INNER` search's message says where to set its stop).
- **NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take a gradient only when their local search does** (`obtains_gradients()`, `phase_specs.h`): `mlsl.c` and `auglag.c` evaluate their own points without one, and the `D` of `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`, `LD_AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG_EQ` only picks NLopt's default local optimiser, `LD_MMA`, which an `INNER` block replaces. With a derivative-free `INNER` block (`LN_BOBYQA`, …) they were treated as gradient-based: `GRAD_STRATEGY`, `PARAM fd_step`, `fd_kernel` and `diff_method` were accepted and never used (the losses were byte-identical without them), the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` derived an `fd_step` nothing used, the journal printed `GRAD_STRATEGY` and the finite-difference settings, and with `PC_OBSERVABLE` an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` resolved to `ANALYTICAL`, so startup required the analytical gradient for a run that takes none. Now such a phase is derivative-free, as `GN_MLSL_LDS` with the same `INNER` block was: those settings are refused at startup in every phase, the message naming the search that takes no gradient (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GD_MLSL_LDS: GRAD_STRATEGY never takes effect: NLOPT:GD_MLSL_LDS takes no gradient: NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take a gradient only for their local search, and the INNER block's NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA takes none; remove it.`), an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` stays unset under `PC_OBSERVABLE`, and the journal lists no gradient settings. The refusals on `GN_MLSL*` and `LN_AUGLAG*`, which take none through NLopt's default `LN_COBYLA`, name that search too.
- **NLopt `AUGLAG`, `AUGLAG_EQ`, `G_MLSL` and `G_MLSL_LDS` without an `INNER` block, or named in an `INNER` block, are refused at startup** (`NLOPT:AUGLAG needs an INNER block (the PRESTAGE refinement): NLopt has no default local optimiser for it; add INNER ... ENDINNER with LIB NLOPT and a local ALGO such as LN_BOBYQA, or use one of NLOPT:GD_MLSL, ..., which run LD_MMA or LN_COBYLA without one`; `the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser names NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS, which needs an INNER block of its own; INNER blocks do not nest: pick a local algorithm such as LN_BOBYQA`), in every phase, and again by `run_inner()` for a spec built at run time. NLopt refused them once the run had started, after the data were loaded and after a `PRESTAGE` exploration had run in full (`local optimizer must be specified for AUGLAG`), or, inside `INNER`, after some evaluations with only `nlopt invalid argument`. Example 14's README, which said every AUGLAG and MLSL form needs an `INNER` block and that the parser refuses one without it, names the four and the default local optimisers of the others.
- **An unknown `--` option on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line is an error at its line**, naming the options the line takes and the closest of them within two edits (`PC_LAMMPS: unknown option '--hotvars' (did you mean '--hotvar'?): HPO's options are --script, --varloss, --invar, --outvar, --failscore, --maxtime, --hotvar and --failvar; LAMMPS command-line options start with a single '-'`; a `PC_FAILPROBE` line's are `--script`, `--invar`, `--outvar`, `--failscore`, `--maxtime`, `--gate` and `--s0`, without the `--varloss`, `--hotvar` and `--failvar` it refuses; `PC_FAILPROBE`'s `--gate` and `--s0` on a `PC_LAMMPS` line are named as `PC_FAILPROBE` options). LAMMPS has no `--` option, so it was passed to LAMMPS with its values, every evaluation failed (`Invalid command-line argument: --hotvars`) and the run spent its whole budget before ending with no usable loss. Single-dash options still go to LAMMPS unchanged.
- **`BUDGET_PER_START` with a multi-seed backend is rejected** at startup and by the policy (`BUDGET_PER_START has no effect with DLIB:GFS, which runs every start in one optimiser; use MAXEVAL`): `DLIB:GFS` runs once from every `INIT` point, and the key was ignored.
- **`tadah refit`: `ALGO UNIFORM` runs no optimiser, as `NONE`**, so a `LIB` with it is an error (`EXPLORE: LIB has no effect with ALGO UNIFORM (no global search runs); remove LIB`); it accepted any `LIB`, `LIB FOO` included. **A key that has no effect in a block with `ALGO NONE` or `UNIFORM` is an error**, named as written (`REFINE: MAXITER has no effect with ALGO NONE (the stage is skipped); remove MAXITER`); they were accepted and ignored. An EXPLORE `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL` and `XTOL_REL` stay accepted, because they also set the local searches of the EXPLORE optimiser `GN_MLSL_LDS` (`refit_curve_objective` is now the one parser of `REFIT_OBJECTIVE`).
- **A `tadah refit` stop criterion that its block's `ALGO` does not read is an error**, named as written with the `ALGO` and the keys it reads (`reject_keys_not_read`, per-`ALGO` read sets): `REFINE: GTOL has no effect with ALGO LD_LBFGS (it reads MAXEVAL, FTOL_REL); remove GTOL`. The keys were checked per stage, so `GTOL` with REFINE `LD_LBFGS` or `LBFGS`, `XTOL_REL` with an EXPLORE `INNER` dlib `LBFGS`, and `MAXITER` with an L-BFGS REFINE were accepted: that `XTOL_REL` had no effect (but under `GN_MLSL_LDS`, whose local searches it set), that `GTOL` none on REFINE, but under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve` it was the gradient tolerance of the FUSE curve refine (`fuse_one` copied REFINE's settings; see the curve-mode FUSE entry), and that `MAXITER` set the L-BFGS evaluation budget. The NLopt searches read `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL` and `XTOL_REL` (REFINE `LD_LBFGS` `MAXEVAL` and `FTOL_REL`), Ceres' `LM` and `DOGLEG` `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL`, the global searches `MAXEVAL`. **`MAXITER`**, Ceres' iterations, is read by `LM` and `DOGLEG` only (`REFINE: MAXITER counts Ceres iterations; ALGO LD_LBFGS counts objective evaluations: write MAXEVAL`). **dlib's `LBFGS` (EXPLORE `INNER`, REFINE) reads `MAXEVAL` and `FTOL_ABS`**, the absolute change of the objective it stops at (`objective_delta_stop_strategy`), as `tadah hpo`'s `DLIB:BFGS` calls it; it took `FTOL_REL` as that absolute change, and `FTOL_REL` there is now an error naming `FTOL_ABS` (`REFINE: FTOL_REL has no effect with ALGO LBFGS: dlib's L-BFGS stops on an absolute change of the objective; write FTOL_ABS`). The defaults are unchanged (1e-13 in `INNER`, 1e-15 in REFINE: `CompactConfig::dlib_inner_ftol_abs`, `dlib_refine_ftol_abs`). The one key set another stage reads stays accepted: under the EXPLORE optimiser `GN_MLSL_LDS` an `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL` and `XTOL_REL` also set MLSL's local `LN_SBPLX` searches, whatever its `ALGO`, and a key neither reads is an error naming those searches as the readers of these (`EXPLORE INNER: FTOL_ABS has no effect with ALGO NONE (no local search runs; under ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS the block sets MLSL's local LN_SBPLX searches, which read MAXEVAL, FTOL_REL, XTOL_REL); remove FTOL_ABS`); with an `INNER` dlib `LBFGS` the `FTOL_REL` now sets only those, so such a run without `FTOL_ABS` gives different results. **REFINE or FUSE with `ENABLED false` and an `OPTIMIZER` block is an error** (`REFINE: ENABLED false skips the stage, so its OPTIMIZER block has no effect; remove the OPTIMIZER block, or write ALGO NONE in it instead of ENABLED false`); the block was ignored with a WARNING. The `optimiser:` line of `refit_opt.log` gives each stage's settings as its `ALGO` reads them (no `gtol` for an L-BFGS REFINE), the MLSL seed with its local searches' (`EXPLORE.seed{algo=MLSL maxeval=60000 local=LN_SBPLX{maxeval=20000 ftol_rel=1e-13 xtol_rel=1e-11}}`). Every tolerance is a finite number >= 0 (*Fixed*).
- **A negative tolerance in a `tadah refit` stage block is an error** at its key, naming the block and the token as written (`REFINE: GTOL must be a number >= 0, got '-1'`): `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `XTOL_REL` and `GTOL` are finite numbers >= 0 in REFINE, FUSE and the EXPLORE `INNER` block, whatever the `ALGO`; 0 switches the test off. A negative one was accepted: Ceres refuses it in its option check and returned `FAILURE` without iterating, which the refit logged as the stop term (`refine=FAILURE(0it, Invalid configuration...)`, `term=FAILURE(0it, Invalid configuration, cost ...)`) and went on, so REFINE and FUSE did nothing and the run exited 0; NLopt switches a negative tolerance off, and dlib's L-BFGS requires one >= 0. A malformed tolerance keeps its message (`must be a finite number`). `CompactBlipFitter::refine`, `refine_edges` and `joint_refine` throw `std::invalid_argument` naming Ceres' reason when Ceres refuses their options (`compact_fitter refine: Ceres refuses the solver options (Invalid configuration. ...)`), before they change the fit: a refused solve leaves its amplitudes, centres and widths, and its EMBF0 reference and RHOFORM, as the caller passed them (it does not lift a negative NONNEG amplitude or a width below the floor first). Only a caller that sets `CompactConfig` itself can reach this now. `read_optimizer_block` offers each key to the command's hook (`ExtraAttrFn`) before `dispatch_opt_attr`, so a command reads a standard attribute by its own rule.
- The `tadah refit` EXPLORE `INNER` search with `LD_LBFGS` ran through a dangling pointer: its NLopt objective context was a local of the branch that set it and was gone when `optimize()` ran. Both contexts now live until it returns. The test `compact fitter: each EXPLORE INNER local search lowers the rung's objective` runs that search; built with AddressSanitizer it reported a stack-use-after-scope in the objective on the old code (the Release build does not show the defect).
- **An ASA run no longer stalls when a periodic test follows a new best**, deaf to `MAXEVAL`, `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` and `SIGUSR2` until `SIGKILL`. ASA's periodic test (every `Acceptance_Frequency_Modulus` acceptances, 100 by default) resizes its block of generated states to the average, over the last `Gener_Mov_Avr` tests, of the number of states generated since the last new best (or since a periodic test that found the accepted-to-generated ratio below `Accepted_To_Generated_Ratio`), rounded down and capped at `Gener_Block_Max`. That number restarts at 0 at every new best, so a test right after one averaged `{1, 1, 0}` to a block of 0 states, and `asa()` then looped at full CPU without generating or evaluating a state; with the block hook installed (`THREADS` > 1 or `Gener_Block_Max` > 1) only `MAXTIME` could still end it. About one run in five at the default options stalled at the first periodic test (6 of 30 seeds on the termination-test fixture; the examples' data with `OPTIM RCUT2B (1)` and `SGRIDMB (1)` and `SEED 1` after 210 evaluations). TADAH-PATCH 5 in `external/asa/asa.c` keeps the block at one state or more, as upstream's own option check requires (`TADAH-PATCHES.md`); a run that did not stall keeps its trajectory, and at the default `Gener_Block_Max` 1 the block stays at one state, which is canonical serial ASA. The `Gener_Block_Max` description no longer claims that setting it equal to `Gener_Block` freezes the block size: the resizing keeps the block between 1 and the cap, and can shrink it below `Gener_Block`. Tests: an objective whose every state is a new best (serial, block-parallel, and the golden gate against the pristine build), and `hpo_shutdown_asa_periodic`, which raises `SIGTERM` past the point where an ASA run stalled. A real-signal case whose run stops evaluating now fails with a message instead of at the CTest timeout (300 s): once three evaluations have started, the signal is raised after 20 s without an evaluation, and the case fails 60 s later if the run has not returned (about 80 s in all); before the third evaluation it fails after the 20 s, without a signal. A run that has not returned 60 s after its signal, or that has neither ended nor reached its signal within 4 minutes, fails the same way.
- **A stop ends an ASA run inside ASA's sampling loops.** Outside its main loop ASA draws states until one is valid, bounded only by `Limit_Invalid_Generated_States`, in three places: the `Number_Cost_Samples` samples of the initial cost temperature, the random start drawn with `User_Initial_Parameters 0` (the default without an `INIT` seed), and the resampling at a periodic test with `Reanneal_Cost` above 1 or below -1. Once a stop was pending (`MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`/`SIGUSR2`, an evaluation's exception), every draw returned an invalid state without evaluating, and upstream ignores its exit request while it samples the cost temperature: the loop ran on for the 1000 draws of the default limit and ended with `TOO_MANY_INVALID_STATES`, and with the documented `PARAM Limit_Invalid_Generated_States -1` it spun at full CPU for ever, deaf to further signals, until `SIGKILL` (for example `MAXEVAL 3`, or a Ctrl-C during the cost-temperature samples). TADAH-PATCH 6 in `external/asa/asa.c` ends each loop at the stop with `IMMEDIATE_EXIT`, as a stop in the main loop does (`TADAH-PATCHES.md`); a run evaluates nothing more after the stop either way, so only how it ends changes. Tests: each loop and stop with the limit off and at its default, and `hpo_shutdown_asa_sampling`, a `SIGINT` among 1000000 cost-temperature samples with the limit off.
- **PC_LAMMPS scripts no longer leave `tadah_pc_<pid>_<n>.in` files in the temporary directory.** Each script was rendered to a named file that only the destructor removed, so a process that ended without running destructors left it behind: a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker killed at its hard deadline (`PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`), crashing inside LAMMPS, or killed with its parent; the worker-pool tests left one per full ctest run. The rendering is now created under a random name (`mkstemp`-style, `tadah_pc_<pid>_<random>.in`, so stale files of an earlier process with the same pid neither block it nor get overwritten or removed), unlinked as soon as it is created and read by LAMMPS through `/proc/self/fd/<fd>`, so the kernel frees it however the process ends. Identical scripts share one rendering while one is alive (one descriptor per distinct script, not per `THREADS` slot). Tests: `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp`, `test_pc_worker_pool.cpp` (count the files in a scratch `TMPDIR`).
- **The analytical gradient of a `PC_OBSERVABLE` run matches the loss** (`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` / `HYBRID`; `compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer`, `compute_observables_grad_dWchain`). It chained each observable only through the refitted weights (dO/dW · dW/dθ) and left out the observable's own dependence on the descriptor parameters at fixed weights — for a cohesive energy the descriptors of the relaxed crystal and of the isolated atom, a term that largely cancels the first — and it summed the observable terms instead of averaging them with the validation metrics as the host loss does. On the examples' Ta model it gave dL/dRCUT2B = 61.8 where central differences of the loss give −0.0097, so `DLIB:BFGS` stepped away from the optimum and stalled at the start point. An observable now reports the energies its value is linear in (`ObservableEvalResult::energy_terms`), which the gradient differentiates with the training rows' descriptor walker, and every chained term is divided by the host's term count; the gradient agrees with central differences to about 0.1 % (new test on the examples' model, with and without a validation metric). An unconverged relaxation's constant penalty contributes no gradient (`value_is_constant`) instead of routing every parameter to finite differences through a size-mismatch exception, and a value without an analytical derivative (the force-scaled penalty) routes them there explicitly. Tests: `test_sims_grad.cpp` (`compute_observables_grad_dWchain`: a constant value, a value without dO/dW, the explicit dO/dθ term and its size check). A `PC_OBSERVABLE` run without `--validation` also has an analytical gradient now: an empty validation set no longer sends every parameter to finite differences (`phi_val missing`).
- **`DLIB:GFS` no longer crashes (SIGSEGV) when its evaluations fail.** dlib's trust-region step (`qopt_impl::pick_next_sample_using_trust_region`) takes as best sample the first whose negated loss exceeds −1e300; handed the default `FAILSCORE`, the largest double, for every failure, a run whose first *dims* + 2 evaluations all failed left it without one, and it indexed an empty vector — whether or not later evaluations would have produced usable losses (CLI example 2 with its old `OALGO 3` crashed at `MAXEVAL` 12). `run_dlib_global` now gives dlib a finite stand-in for a failed evaluation (`detail::GfsLossForDlib`): the worst usable loss of the search so far plus a margin (the larger of the usable spread and the worst loss's magnitude; twice that for an ungraded failure, between one and two times for a graded one by its place in the graded-failure band), or, before any usable loss, `FAILSCORE` capped at 1e100. A stand-in is fixed when dlib gets it, and each search (a `RESTART` round is a new one) starts afresh, so graded failures keep their order only among the failures handed over in the same state (all before the first usable loss, or while the same worst and best usable losses hold). No value dlib sees exceeds 1e100 in magnitude. `loss.tadah`, the journal and `best_pot.tadah` keep the loss the evaluation reported, and the journal notes the stand-in once per search (`DLIB:GFS: dlib is given a finite stand-in for each failed evaluation of this search …`).
- **A run in which no evaluation produced a usable loss exits with status 1** (`hpo_run()` returns `EXIT_FAILURE`), with an `ERROR` journal line and a line on stderr (`[HPO] ERROR: no evaluation produced a usable loss; no best_pot.tadah was written`) and the trailer `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`. It returned 0 with `COMPLETED`, although no `best_pot.tadah` was written. `drive_run()` decides it after an ordinary end (a normal end or a stagnation stop) from `HPO_Host::has_usable_loss()`, over every MPI rank; a signal (128 + N) and an error keep their own status and trailer. Runs with a usable loss end as before. The question over the ranks is one collective that every ending reaches exactly once, before the final flush, so a rank whose flush fails, or whose run an error or a signal ends after the drivers' last collective, leaves no other rank waiting in it (`tests/mpi/test_anneal_mpi.cpp`, `hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix`, runs `drive_run()` on 1, 2 and 4 ranks).
- **The journal's DIAGNOSTICS count every evaluation without a usable loss** (`Unusable losses : N (x%)`: NaN/Inf clampings, evaluations that threw, `FAILSCORE` from a skip cap or a failed script, and graded failures), and the verdict reads that count: `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss.`, `[!] No evaluation produced a usable loss.` or the majority warning. The verdict read the graded failures alone, so the journal said `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss` right after `100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss`, and for runs whose every loss was a positive `FAILSCORE`. (A `FAILSCORE` of 0 or below, which left no failure band, is refused at startup: *Changed*.) Each rank of a multi-rank run keeps its own journal, which counts that rank's evaluations: its verdict names the rank (`[!] No evaluation on MPI rank 1 (of 2) produced a usable loss.`), since whether the run produced a usable loss is decided over every rank (`HPOLog::set_rank_scope`).
- **A search that a point without a usable gradient ended says so** (`STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil`; on v1-dev until 2026-10-07 the status read `STOPPED -- gradient not finite: …`, but under a finite `FAILSCORE` the difference with a failed evaluation is finite, and it fails the gradient all the same: the next entry), where it reported success or a spent `MAXEVAL`. With the default `FAILSCORE`, the largest double, a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil made the difference with a usable loss overflow (for any step below 1/2 in the optimiser's internal coordinates: half the box width under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`), and such an evaluation now fails the gradient whatever `FAILSCORE` is (next entry). In one dimension with `PARAM fd_step 0.2`, `MAXEVAL 60` and a skip cap that fails the stencil point 1.4 of the start point 1.6, every gradient-based backend stopped after the same four evaluations and reported `MAXEVAL=60 reached` and `COMPLETED` (`DLIB:BFGS`), `SUCCESS -- generic success` (NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR1`, `LD_VAR2`), `roundoff-limited` (`LD_SLSQP`) or `COMPLETED` (every CERES driver); `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` asked for the same point until NLopt's `MAXEVAL` (120 evaluations of three points), and so did `LD_AUGLAG` (its default local search `LD_MMA`) and an `INNER` `LD_MMA` or `LD_CCSAQ`, while `AUGLAG` with an `INNER` `LD_LBFGS` or `LD_TNEWTON` reported `FTOL_REACHED` after the same evaluations and one more, AUGLAG's evaluation of the point its local search returned. The adapters now read why the gradient failed (`GradientProvider::failure`, *Added*). `DLIB:BFGS` cannot reject a point: such a gradient ends the search (`detail::GradientNotFinite`). NLopt and Ceres reject the point (`+inf`; a failed evaluation) and step back, as before; a local NLopt gradient search that has had no usable gradient yet is stopped at that rejection (`force_stop`: the same evaluations, except the repeats) — an `LD_*` algorithm without an `INNER` block, or an `AUGLAG` whose local search is one (`single_local_search`, *Added*; `force_stop` reaches that search). An NLopt search that ends right after a rejection ended because of it: no evaluation after the rejection but `AUGLAG`'s evaluation of the point its local search returned (`detail::ended_on_rejection`), and neither `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME` nor `STOPVAL` met, nor the `MAXEVAL` or `MAXTIME` of `AUGLAG`'s local search, which `AUGLAG` reports as `FTOL_REACHED`; a tolerance (`XTOL_REACHED`, `FTOL_REACHED`) reached there is reported as the rejection's ending. So does a Ceres solve that ends with `FAILURE` right after one. `MLSL` and the global `GD_*` searches are not stopped at a rejection. The journal has a warning that names the backend, the point and the stencil point (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil (at CGRID2B[2] = 1.6, the stencil point CGRID2B[2] = 1.4 failed: loss 1.797693135e+308); best-so-far preserved`), and the status is the trailer of a run the search ends. A gradient that is not finite for another reason ends it the same way with its own cause (`the finite-difference quotient overflowed`, `the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`, `… is not finite at this point`). Such a run ends with status 0 when an evaluation produced a usable loss (the stencil's other points normally did), and with status 1 and the no-usable-loss trailer otherwise; a signal or an error still decides the ending first (`run_driver.h`). Nothing stands in for the failed evaluation and the search does not go on past the point where it ends. Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` ("Gradient not finite: …", per backend, `AUGLAG` and its local search's `MAXEVAL`, `MLSL` and `GD_STOGO`, `MULTISTART`, and for a later point), `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (the endings right after a rejection, a tolerance and the local search's `MAXEVAL` on an outage stub: "No usable gradient: an NLopt search …", "… a Ceres search …"), `test_phase_specs.cpp` (`single_local_search`), `test_gradient_failure.cpp`.
- **A failed evaluation fails a gradient, whatever `FAILSCORE` is** (`GradientProvider`, for every gradient backend: `DLIB:BFGS`, NLopt's `LD_*`, the local search of `MLSL` and `AUGLAG`, the CERES drivers; every `GRAD_STRATEGY`). Only a loss that was not finite, or a difference that overflowed, failed a finite-difference gradient, and the analytical strategies did not read the loss at all, but `HPO_Host` clamps every failure to a finite `FAILSCORE`: between two failures the difference was an exact 0, and the analytical gradient at a failed point is that of the loss before the failure (a skip cap's uncapped loss), small but not 0, so a start inside the failure region looked converged; under a finite `FAILSCORE` a failed stencil point gave a slope as steep as the jump to it, which the searches followed. On a one-dimensional problem (`OPTIM CGRID2B (2) 1.0 2.0`, a skip cap failing the box below about 1.47, `PARAM fd_step 0.2`, `MAXEVAL 60`), from 1.25 on the pushed v1-dev (2026-09-30), where each finite-difference gradient also evaluated its point again: `DLIB:BFGS` made 8 evaluations (1.25, then 1.25, 1.45 and 1.05 for its gradient, twice) and ended as converged, NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*` and `LD_VAR2` 4 (`SUCCESS -- generic success fmin=1.79769e+308`), `LD_SLSQP` ended roundoff-limited, `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` asked for the same point and its stencil until `MAXEVAL` (120 evaluations for `MAXEVAL 60`, when NLopt counted only its own calls), every CERES driver ended `CONVERGENCE` at iteration 0; a `MULTISTART` `DLIB:BFGS` start drawn there (`METHOD RANDOM`, SEED 3: 1.206) made 8 evaluations without a word and the trailer read `COMPLETED`; under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` (the default under `PC_OBSERVABLE`; `OALGO 4`), or `HYBRID` with nothing differenced, `DLIB:BFGS` and such a `MULTISTART` start ended as converged, Ceres `CONVERGENCE` at a cost of 1.79769e+308 and `LD_LBFGS` `nlopt failure -- algorithm converged`, every loss `FAILSCORE`; under `FAILSCORE 1e9`, from 1.6, `DLIB:BFGS` evaluated the bound 2 28 times in a row and ran to 60 evaluations, `LD_LBFGS` ended `SUCCESS` after 8, `LD_SLSQP` `bug: more than iter SQP iterations`, `LD_MMA` ran to 120, `CERES:LBFGS` left the box (2.6). The loss at the point decides now (`StencilReport::classify`): at a usable loss the first failed stencil evaluation (`StencilReport::failed`: not finite, or at least 0.999 `FAILSCORE`, `loss_is_feasible`) fails the gradient and the points after it are not evaluated; at a failed loss — `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`, a loss equal to `FAILSCORE`, or a loss that is not finite — the gradient fails at once, before any stencil point; at a graded failure under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` (`OptimizerSpec::failscore_grading`, *Added*) the differences are taken as they are, the graded slope out of the failure region (or the jump to a usable neighbour), and the gradient fails only when every differenced component is 0 (`… and every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`). `HYBRID` classifies the caller's loss (`gradient_at`) the same way, and without it evaluates the point (counted under `HYBRID centre losses`) for a one-sided or forward stencil, or under grading when a central stencil point failed; under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF` a failed central stencil point fails its gradient at once. `ANALYTICAL`, and `HYBRID` before its analytical pass, read the caller's loss too: a failed one fails the gradient at once, `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point` (`kGradientFailedPointCause`), with no analytical pass; a graded failure under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` fails it too under `ANALYTICAL`, and `HYBRID` differences every parameter there (the entry on a graded failure's analytical gradient). The searches then end or step back as for any point without a usable gradient (previous entry): from 1.25 a local search — `DLIB:BFGS`, an `LD_*` algorithm or an `AUGLAG` whose local search is one, a CERES driver — stops after one evaluation under every `GRAD_STRATEGY`, its stop line naming the loss (`(the loss at CGRID2B[2] = 1.25 is 1.797693135e+308)`), and the run ends without a usable loss (status 1), while `MLSL` and the global `GD_*` searches reject the start and go on to usable points; such a `MULTISTART` start makes one evaluation and, as the last start, its stop is the trailer; under `FAILSCORE 1e9` from 1.6 every backend stops after 1.6, 1.8 and 1.4, as under the default. Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` with `FAILSCORE 1e9` from 1.25 the graded searches are unchanged: `DLIB:BFGS` reaches the bound (95.25) at its 4th evaluation, `LD_LBFGS` after 6. A search that touched a failure from a usable point no longer takes that difference: with the ERMSE target 10.5, `LD_SLSQP` from 1.72 and the CERES drivers from 1.8, which were handed `FAILSCORE` with a zero gradient at a point inside the region (1.074, 1.0586) and ended right after a later rejection (13 and 12 evaluations on the pushed v1-dev), reject that point and go on to `MAXEVAL`; `AUGLAG` with an `INNER` `LD_SLSQP` of `MAXEVAL 4` ends after 9 evaluations (14 on the pushed v1-dev). Under `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` a failed point costs no analytical gradient, and NLopt and Ceres are given a rejection (`+inf`, a failed evaluation) for it, not `FAILSCORE` with the analytical gradient, so a search that meets failures takes another path. Limitations: a usable point whose stencil reaches a failure has no gradient, so a search cannot settle within one step of a failure (`DLIB:BFGS` stops there, NLopt and Ceres reject the point); an evaluation whose loss mixed failed and usable terms (the mean of terms of which one is `FAILSCORE`, below 0.999 `FAILSCORE`: a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation, a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term) was a usable loss to this rule -- it is now a failed evaluation (the entries on a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation and a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term); at a graded failure next to a usable point the difference spans the jump from the failure band to the usable loss, as steep as that jump and not the band's slope (a follow-up: difference a graded point only against graded ones, or bound that step), so a search started there can sit on a bound or stop at once — under `FAILSCORE 1e9` with `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` from 1.4, `DLIB:BFGS` evaluates the bound 2 23 times in a row (31 evaluations), `LD_SLSQP` ends after 3 evaluations with `bug: more than iter SQP iterations (algorithm converged; …)`, `CERES:LBFGS` leaves the box (2.4), a behaviour this fix does not change; and a gradient asked for without the loss at its point (`gradient()`, `DLIB:BFGS` for a point other than its last evaluation) cannot tell a failed point under `ANALYTICAL`. Example fingerprints do not change: no evaluation of a gradient-based example reads a failed loss. Tests: `test_gradient_failure.cpp` (the provider rules, `HYBRID` and its flat graded region and lazily evaluated `FAILSCORE` centre, `ANALYTICAL`, the status text), `test_fd_stencil.cpp`, `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (the NLopt and Ceres adapters on stub hosts: a start inside the failure region, `MLSL` and `GD_*`, grading), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` ("No usable gradient: …": every finite-difference backend from 1.25 and under `FAILSCORE 1e9`, `MULTISTART`, grading and `TADAH_FAILSCORE_GRADING=ON`, `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` from 1.25 and a `MULTISTART` start under either), `test_failgrade_parse.cpp` (the flag through the parser, the override and every spec copy), `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`.
- **Under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` a candidate rejected before training is a point without a usable gradient, not an error.** Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` a candidate the bump-support gate (`BUMPGATE CANDIDATE`) or a density gate (`RHOGATE`, `RHOBARGATE`) rejects scores a graded failure, so the analytical pass ran there; `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` handed every parameter to finite differences, its trainer slot holding no fit of the point, and `ANALYTICAL` took that for a parameter without an analytical derivative: `FAILED -- GRAD_STRATEGY=ANALYTICAL: no analytical gradient for 2 parameters at this point (indices: [0, 1]) ...`, exit 1, at the first rejected candidate a step reached (NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_MMA`, `LD_SLSQP`, `LD_CCSAQ`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR2`, `CERES:LBFGS`, `WOLFE`, `NLCG`, after 5-9 evaluations of a D2_Blip search under `Cut_Dummy`). `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient_gap()` says why the slot holds no fit of the point (the gate's reject note, a training that did not complete), and the `ANALYTICAL` provider asks it when its pass leaves parameters to finite differences: such a point has no usable gradient (`the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`, its detail naming the point, the reason and `HYBRID`), which NLopt and Ceres reject and step back from, as from any point without a usable gradient, and `DLIB:BFGS` (which asks no gradient at a point its line search rejects) would stop at, `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`. A parameter or a loss term without an analytical derivative stays an error, which no longer names rejected candidates among its causes. `HYBRID` takes finite differences at such a point, as before. Tests: `test_gradient_failure.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL: a candidate the bump gate rejects before training ...`).
- **Under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` a point whose fit the analytical gradient cannot differentiate is a point without a usable gradient, not an error.** `compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer` factorises the normal equations of the fit, Phi^T Phi + `LAMBDA` I over the training rows, for its implicit-function solve; where they were not positive definite (the trainer's LAPACK factorisation of the same matrix succeeded: the two differ at rounding level on a numerically singular one) it returned with every parameter handed to finite differences and nothing said why (the one-time gradient diagnostic named a parameter without a derivative), and a throw from it (an inconsistency between the trainer and its lists or descriptors) was written to the journal and to stderr at every gradient. `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient_gap()` was empty there, so `ANALYTICAL` took the list for parameters without a derivative and ended the run `FAILED -- GRAD_STRATEGY=ANALYTICAL: no analytical gradient for N parameters at this point ...`. The helper throws at such a point, naming the solve, the matrix and `LAMBDA` (`the implicit-function solve has no solution at this point: the normal equations of the fit (Phi^T Phi + LAMBDA I over the training rows, LAMBDA = 0) are not positive definite in the gradient's Cholesky factorisation`); `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` makes any failure of the helper a point without an analytical gradient, `analytical_gradient_gap()` says why (`its analytical gradient could not be formed: ...`), and `ANALYTICAL` fails the gradient there (`the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`), as at a candidate rejected before training (above); `HYBRID` takes finite differences for every parameter there, as before. The journal has a `WARNING` at occurrences 1, 2, 4, ... of the run (`Analytical gradient could not be formed at this point (occurrence 1): ...`). The `ANALYTICAL` error for a parameter without an analytical derivative no longer names an ill-conditioned fit among its causes. Not reproduced end to end: no portable fixture makes the two factorisations disagree; the host's part is tested with the helper made to throw (`testing::ScopedAnalyticalHelperFailure`, *Added*). Tests: `test_hpo_analytical.cpp` (`compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer: normal equations that are not positive definite fail the gradient at the point`), `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` (`A gradient helper that fails at a trained point leaves it without an analytical gradient, and the host says why`, `hpo_trainer_e2e`).
- **`TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` draw a stream of their own for their `SEED`, not the generator of the starting-point methods `RANDOM` and `LHS` for the same seed.** Both seeded `std::mt19937_64` with the integer `SEED`, as `RANDOM` and `LHS` (`init_strategies.cpp`) seed theirs, and read it through `uniform(0, 1)`, as `RANDOM` does, so equal seeds of two keys gave the same numbers: with `INIT { METHOD RANDOM 1, SEED 2 }` and `TADAH:RANDOM SEED 2` evaluations 1 and 2 were the same point, and so were the exploration's first two with `EXPLORE INIT { METHOD RANDOM 2, SEED 2 }`; with `SEED 5` in the `RESTART` block (its `SEED` is new in this release) and on `TADAH:RANDOM` under `PLAIN` (round r seeds both with `5 + r`) every round's first draw was the round's fresh point, evaluated twice (documented, with the advice to give the two keys different values); `ASA` with `SEED s` drew the `RANDOM` method's numbers for `s`. `seed_stream.h` (*Added*) has the seeded stream of every optimiser's random consumer, `seed_stream(seed, salt, idx)` (a `std::seed_seq` of the seed, the consumer's salt and an index; `anneal::make_stream` forwards to it, so the `ANNEAL` family draws as before), and the registry of the salts (`seed_streams`: the `ANNEAL` family's chains, probes, swap coins, warm-up and resampler, `kRandomSearch`, `kAsa`), checked distinct at compile time; `TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` draw `seed_stream(SEED, kRandomSearch)` and `seed_stream(SEED, kAsa)`. The `METHOD` chains draw as before (an `INIT` block's points and a `RESTART` round's are unchanged by this entry), and two consumers of one kind given the same seed still draw the same numbers (two `INIT` chains of one phase; an `INIT` block's `SEED s` and a `RESTART` round whose `RESTART SEED + r` is `s` did until a `RESTART` round's `RANDOM` and `LHS` got a stream of their own: [Unreleased], *Changed*); the `PRESTAGE` refinement draws its own (the next entry). A `TADAH:RANDOM` or `ASA` run with a given `SEED` takes another path: examples 02, 21 and 25 have new fingerprints (best losses 0.06671894 -> 0.07525412, 0.07044785 -> 0.04680598, 0.07502931 -> 0.07525412; 25 grades eight failures, six before). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Termination: TADAH:RANDOM does not draw the points of a RANDOM method with the same seed`, `Termination: a RESTART round's fresh RANDOM point is not the seeded TADAH:RANDOM's first draw of the round`), `test_asa.cpp` (`ASA draws random numbers of its own and not the RANDOM method's for the same seed`); `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` pins SEED 3's new draws.
- **A `PRESTAGE` refinement draws random numbers of its own, not its exploration's** (`OptimizerSpec::stream_phase`, `phase_stream_seed`, *Added*). An optimiser drew the same streams for a `SEED` in the refinement as in the exploration, so a refinement with the exploration's optimiser drew the exploration's numbers whenever the two seeds were equal: with `TADAH:RANDOM` and `SEED 7` in the `STRATEGY` and `EXPLORE` blocks the refinement evaluated the exploration's draws again (evaluations 7-10 were 2-5).
